# ArchiveVS

A fast-paced 1v1 / 2v2 arena prototype with two main modes — **Duel** (single stock) and **Trio** (three-unit rosters). (The game is ArchiveVS since b1105ad; Gun VS Gun was the project's working name, and the package and service names, `gvg-…`, still carry it.) In the **angled** point of view the unit aims by itself, every shot at the locked enemy; in the **free** one the player aims a crosshair (see *Free pov*). The fight is about resource management: when to sprint, when to dodge, when to break line of sight, when to fire.

## Modes

Two main modes, each playable **1v1 or 2v2**, offline and online:

- **Duel** — classic single stock: one unit per fighter; a team loses when all its fighters are down.
- **Trio** — three-unit stock: every slot (human or bot) fields an **ordered roster of three units**, repeats allowed. When a unit dies, the slot's next unit respawns with the standard 3 s spawn immunity — at the slot's own start, or, on the ten maps that name more spawn places (Town, Neon, Compound, Dust, Warehouse, Hospital, Spacecraft, District and the two Backrooms), at one of those: a place no living enemy sees, drawn at random, on Spacecraft any free place (see *Spawn places* under Maps); the killer keeps position / HP / boost — no kill reward. A team loses when every roster on its side is spent. Each fighter's remaining units show as a row of small weapon renders under their side's HP bars (one line per team member; Duel shows its single unit the same way), and a small golden glow-bar marks each Trio line's currently fielded weapon.
- **Spectating (2v2, both modes)**: if you're out for good while your ally fights on, the camera follows the ally with your own-unit visual kit (rear art + through-wall X-ray); the lock reticle stays up and mirrors the ally's actual target (the TARGET button goes inert), and the boost bar and ammo readout follow the ally too (2026-09-29 — they used to stay frozen on your dead unit's numbers).

Orthogonal to all of the above, every player also picks a **view mode** — **Shooter** (the chase camera you pilot directly; named "Classic" until 2026-10-10) or **Command** (a commander's diorama view where your unit fights on its own bot brain and you give it orders). See the **Command mode** section below.

**A match's course** (every mode, offline and online). A start goes through the **loading screen** (b1105hd), up until everything the match needs is in — online until every player's page has it, 30 s at the most (b1105hh). Then the **game-start intro** (b1105gz; every match but the Shooting Range's): team 1's shot and team 2's under GET READY, then the viewer's own view under START — 3.8 s in all, the match held meanwhile, PAUSE the only HUD, nobody able to skip it; under Sudden Death its banner follows. No unit is immune at the start. The match ends on the **ending cameras** (b1105gb on): **K.O.** over the losing side's last unit down as it falls, one flight to the unit that dealt the final blow, and **FINAL BLOW** over that unit as it reloads once and then holds its stance — or **WINNER** over the unit of its side that stands, where the final blow's unit fell too (its roster's next unit, b1105gh; its teammate, b1105in). A tap, a click or a key goes to the end page once 1 s has run (TAP TO SKIP); left alone, the last shot stays up until one. A forfeit goes straight to the end page. Online the end page then offers Leave, RESELECT and REMATCH (b1105is: *After a match*, under Online). Each part has its own entry under *Random & All Random cards* below.

### Offline
- **1v1**: you vs one enemy bot.
- **2v2**: you + an ally bot vs two enemy bots. Friendly fire is off between teammates.
- **Trio picks**: you select your three units in order, then each bot's three — same selection grid, titles count up (1/3 → 3/3).
- Optional "Dummy" mode, in the main menu's **Practice** row — zeroes out damage from every bot (enemies and your ally), so you can practice movement and observe bot behaviour without dying.
- Optional "Spectator" mode beside it — a bot takes over your unit and you watch the match: **TARGET** cycles the camera across every unit on the field (both teams), the HUD (HP / boost / ammo) follows whoever you're watching, you hear that unit as its own player does — its steps, dodge, jump and landing, its bolt, its gun's metal sound, its reload, the round going in and its scope (b1105id, b1105if, b1105ig, b1105ih) — the edge arrows stay viewer-relative, and the end banner reads **TEAM 1 WINS / TEAM 2 WINS**. Stacks with Dummy: the bot driving your slot still deals damage exactly as you would, and every other bot's shots are blanks (2026-10-03 — before that the combination zeroed everyone for a no-deaths exhibition).
- The main menu also carries a **Guide** button, the `?` in its corner: a popup on the rules, the menu, the controls, the two points of view, fighting, the ladders and transporters, the weapon types and the command-mode gestures (b1105hj), and at its foot the disclaimer in small gray print (b1105jw); its header carries the **Music: On / Off** button, which the Pause menu has too (b1105hk, see *Sounds & music*). **Server Boot-up**, which opens the online debug panel, left the main menu at b1105o and stands on the online connecting page.

### Online
- **Mode selection**: the host picks **Duel or Trio**, then **1v1 or 2v2**; joiners inherit the lobby's modes.
- **1v1**: the host presses **Start Match** when ready. The opponent slot holds a bot (default **Saori / Unit 1**) until a second human queues in and takes it — start early to play the bot, or wait for a player.
- **2v2**: the host presses **Start Match** when ready; empty player slots fill with bots (2v2 rooms open as Saori / Hoshino / Aru / Atsuko by slot; Trio bots field three copies of that unit unless the host picks otherwise). Up to four humans can play (any split between teams); bots fill any remaining slots.
- **Match start** (b1105hh): when the host starts, every player's page shows the loading screen until all the players' pages have loaded the match — 30 s at the most — and then everyone sees the game-start intro together and the match begins for all at once. A player who leaves, a bot and a spectator are not waited for.
- **Bot unit selection**: in the lobby, the host can tap any bot slot to pick which unit that bot plays — in Trio, its three units in order (default: three copies of the slot's usual unit). A human joining the slot always overrides the bot.
- **Trio queue room**: every slot's roster is shown in three lines and updates live as picks land.
- **View mode**: each player picks Shooter or Command in the queue room; the pick is final once the match starts and is never shown to the other team (teammates see it as a gold **[CMD]** tag on the roster line). Details in the Command mode section.
- **Slot-colored corner HUD** (online, 2026-08-22): the corner HP chips (bars until b1105w) follow the **server slot** — left column team A (p1 top / p3 bottom), right column team B (p2 top / p4 bottom), each in its slot color (p1 blue / p2 pink / p3 mint / p4 orange), the same picture for every viewer and spectator (1v1: p1 left, p2 right); the chip's side word, **YOU**, says which one is yours (b1105w; a thin white rim on the bar until then). The command diorama's markers, cards, rings and lock triangles use the same slot colors online (offline keeps the role palette).
- **After a match** (b1105is): the end page has **Leave**, **RESELECT** and **REMATCH**. Every seated player chooses — the choice stays pressed on the page and may be changed until the room moves — and beside each other seat the page says what its player did: REMATCH, RESELECT, CHOOSING…, or **LEFT · BOT NEXT** for a player who has gone. The room moves when everyone still seated has chosen. All REMATCH: the match again, the same map, units and seats, an empty seat a bot's. One RESELECT: the room is a waiting room again, where everyone picks a unit anew and the host a map — the last picks tagged **LAST** — while the mode (Duel / Trio, 1v1 / 2v2, the rules) stays as it was; that waiting room is open like a new one, so a new player may take a free seat, a 2v2's players may change sides, and the host starts the match.
- **A player who leaves is named** (b1105is): in a 2v2 match a line says whose unit a bot took over; a 1v1 left in mid-match ends at once and its end page says why; at the end page the seat reads LEFT · BOT NEXT, and the room waits 3 s after such a leaving before it moves, so that the others see it; in a waiting room or a picker a line says a player left.
- **Host migration**: if the host leaves a waiting room, the longest-waiting player is promoted to host (their unit picks carry over; the lobby keeps its Duel / Trio and 1v1 / 2v2 settings and the new host re-chooses the map). At the end page nobody is moved (b1105is): a REMATCH needs no host, and a RESELECT makes the longest-waiting player the host as the room goes back to waiting.
- **A dropped connection** (b1105it): a page whose connection comes back is put into a room anew — its old seat is gone, and the units and the map are picked again, the last picks tagged **LAST**. A host who comes back as a host gets the mode and the rules it chose again without being asked; a player who comes back as the host for the first time is asked them; a player who comes back into somebody else's room plays that room's mode.
- **The unit picker follows the room** (b1105it): a player who comes into a room while its host is still choosing the mode sees the picker of the room as it stands — Duel, 1v1, Health Point — and the picker is drawn anew when the host's choice lands.
- Multi-lobby — when an existing lobby is full or running, new joiners spawn their own lobby and become host.
- Team swap in 2v2: any non-host player can `Join` an empty slot to switch teams (e.g. two humans want to co-op on one side against two bots).

### Unit profile card

The first tap on a unit card opens its profile beside the grid (tap the same card again to confirm, anywhere else to cancel): the character art on the left and the **weapon panel** on the right — the gun's real-world name, its render, and since 2026-09-22 a **3×2 stat grid** under the render (owner pick "C" from three in-game samples: a third plate beside the panel and a label/value row list were the other two; the demo line carries the same stats as a row list). The six cells read live from the unit data:

- **Mag**, **RPM** and **Reload** (seconds) as plain numbers.
- **Dmg** shows every tier where a gun has them: Aru's PSG1 `50/35/20` (far / mid / near), Kei's Railgun `30/20` (quick / charged), the shotguns per pellet `4 ×8`.
- **Movement** is the walk-speed tier word (walk speeds retuned 2026-09-24): **Fast** (walk 16 — Atsuko, Marina, Yuuka, Hoshino, Haruka, Rabu), **Medium** (12 — Saori, Asuna, Subaru, Minori, Fubuki, Aris, Iori, Aru, Kei), **Slow** (Koyuki, Kurumi, Maki and Hiyori at 10, Hina at 8).
- The **Spread picture** is a seeded mini-simulation of a **walking spray at a common 60-unit reference** (the shotguns at their own lock range, 80; 40 until b1105eg): the ring is a standing target's half-width at that distance, the orange dots are up to 30 rounds — magazine-limited — each leaving on the cone the sim gives that round (base + bloom, per-round bloom up to the cap, and the between-round recovery of the no-delay marksman rifles), fading with round order so the first rounds read brightest. The spray stops at the first round whose cone no longer fits the frame (2026-09-22, owner pick from samples — pinning spilled rounds to the frame's edge had piled Fubuki's 27 out-of-frame rounds into a rectangle), so nothing is drawn outside the frame: Saori's whole 30-round spray stays inside the ring, Koyuki's and Hina's pictures end a few rounds short of the magazine with their late rounds at the frame's edge, and Fubuki's and Aris's show their first three quick rounds — one on the ring's centre, two at its edge — because the fourth already flies wider than the frame; the shotguns show their 8-pellet pattern (the Beretta 1301 with its 1.4× horizontal stretch — Hoshino's since the two shotguns swapped patterns on 2026-10-10).
- A unit may carry a **note** (`cardNote`, user 2026-10-24): one line at the card's bottom-left, under the art. Kurumi's reads "This unit has 20% more health." A map may carry one too (`cardNote` in the client's `MAP_DATA`; District's and Compound's read "Unit has more stamina in this map.", user 2026-10-29 and 2026-10-30), shown on its info card the same way, under the picture. (The notes were off for part of 2026-11-01, while every map had their boost, and came back with it; Neon carried the note from 2026-11-01 until its 5 went on 2026-11-03.)

The grid keeps the same sizes on phones (checked at 390 px: nothing wraps or spills).

### Random & All Random cards

Both offline and online pickers carry them:

- Every unit grid has a gray **Random** card (question-mark thumbnail): it rolls a unit the roster being built doesn't already contain (different players can still land on the same unit). The map picker is two sections, each with a Random card of its own: **Scene Maps** — the maps cut from modelled scenes (Town, Spacecraft) — over **Concept Maps**, the hand-made arenas; a section's Random rolls among that section alone and skips **Shooting Range** and **Plain Field** (`noRandom` in the map table; user 2026-10-19) and, since b1027p, **District** too (user 2026-10-30, "too much of a special map" — a manual pick only, offline and online alike: the roll is the picker's, the host's included). A map's row may say which part of its card picture the wide thumb shows (`thumb: 'top' | 'bottom'`; the thumb box cuts a square picture to a band, the centre by default) — Backrooms and Warehouse show their upper part, Spacecraft its lower; the profile shows the whole picture (user 2026-10-24).
- Every unit grid also carries a golden **All Random** card: one confirm fills every remaining unit slot of the current flow at once and advances it (offline: the rest of your roster, then every bot picker that follows — Duel included; online: the rest of your roster). The map picker deliberately has no All Random.

**The Splat skin** (PT, b1105l, 2026-11-05) — the first pass of the UI rework, the user's pick of seven directions drawn on the "GunVS UI Rework Samples" design canvas (A Tactical, B Arcade, C Signal, D Slash after Persona 5 and Valorant, E Holo after Apex and Overwatch, F Splat after Splatoon and Jet Set Radio, G Ops after Call of Duty: Mobile): direction F. Every menu is an opaque cream sheet with three ink blobs (an inline SVG in `style.css`, pinned while the content scrolls; user: the cream covers the 3D behind), the titles white Titan One stickers with an ink outline and a magenta drop, the buttons and option chips white pills with a 3 px ink border and a hard shadow (the chosen chip magenta; Sudden Death ink with yellow type), each a degree or two off square. The lobby keeps its flow — the first unit pick is made on it — with its four option rows labelled Play as / Teams / Roster / Rules (`showSelectMenu`). The unit grid is 84 px sticker tiles (`unitGridHTML`), seven to a row on a landscape phone so the 17 units and the two Random cards sit in three rows without scrolling; a tile shows the character's name and a weapon-class tag on its corner — AR, RF (rifle), SMG, SG, MG, SN (sniper) — and is coloured by that class (cyan, cyan, lime, yellow, magenta, ink). The map picker's two sections are tabs now, Scene Maps | Concept Maps with their counts (`mapGridHTML`, wired in `wireMapGrid`), one grid shown at a time; a tab's Random card rolls its own tab alone, as the stacked sections did. The profile card of both pickers (`.unit-profile-popup`) is a white sticker with a lime "tap to confirm" tag hanging off its bottom; its contents — the art, the weapon panel, the six stats, a unit's or a map's note — are as before. The match HUD keeps every box the JS mirrors (the corner bars' 180 px, the thumb buttons' `--btn-size`, the joystick's 140, the dio cards' 180x68 / 132x52) and wears the skin inside them: the bars a white rim with an ink outline and the fills lime (you) / magenta (enemy) / cyan (ally) / yellow (enemy 2), the online slot colours still set by the JS; the thumb buttons white, DODGE cyan, SPRINT magenta, TARGET yellow, FIRE's count in Titan One and its reload ring lime; PAUSE a sticker; the commander's cards ink with a white rim. The crosshair is untouched (the CS classic, user). Titan One and Rubik are bundled from `client/src/fonts` (latin and latin-ext, SIL Open Font License — the OFL texts beside them; 72 KB in all) so the PWA has them offline; the stylesheet's `url()`s go through Vite, so the hashed files follow the `./` base. Not in this pass: real ink-splat art in place of the SVG blobs, Chinese type. Online room, pause / guide / settings and the commander's order tray took the same components with no design of their own, as asked.

**The slot lobby and the pickers' page** (PT, b1105m, 2026-11-05; user: "i want that layout previously shown in the artifact" and, of the HUD sample, "i especially love those buttons and the simple arrows inside") — the lobby is the design canvas's: your unit's card on the left (the profile art, the name, the class and the weapon; a Trio roster as three thumbs), the option rows in the middle — Play as / Teams / Roster / Rules / Where (Online hands over to the online lobby, as the old button did) — with the Dummy and Spectator toggles under them (they sat on the map picker's page), the map's card on the right (its picture, its name, its section and its size in metres from the boundary table, its stamina note), and along the foot the summary chip, a Random die (rolls your unit, or your whole Trio roster), All random (rolls you and arms the chain) and GO!!. A card opens its picker and comes back; GO!! fills what is still missing — the pickers for an incomplete roster, then the bot pickers as ever — and starts on the card's map, so the map picker is no longer the chain's last page (`showSelectMenu`, `renderLobbySlots`, `startFromLobby`, `startFromMap`; `state.mapKey` starts at Plain Field). The pickers' page (`showUnitPicker`, `showMapPicker`): a back arrow (to the lobby) and the title on one row, the unit picker's class filter chips beside them (All / AR / SMG / SG / MG / Sniper; the rifles count as AR), the preview card on the left — the art, the name and class pill, five range pips from the lock range, the weapon's name and its six stats (the gun render stays on the floating card), or a map's section, size and tags (manual pick only, offline only, the stamina note) — with a CONFIRM! button; the tapped tile wears a PICK! tag, and tapping it again confirms as before. `spawnProfilePopup` fills the page's side card when the menu has one and floats the old card otherwise (the online pickers); on a portrait phone the side card floats over the grid as the old popup did. The thumb buttons carry arrows over their words — JUMP up, DODGE a sidestep, SPRINT a dash, TARGET a sight (`HUD_BTN_ICON`). The first lobby and the first frame moved to the module's end: the lobby's map card reads `GLB_MAPS`, declared far below the old spot, and the first frame's `updateMapRoof` read it before it stood — the render error logged on every page load since the roof hiding is gone with it.

**The lobby's second pass** (PT, b1105n, 2026-11-05; the user's five notes on b1105m) — the cards' art is the square thumbnail (the face-centred crop the tiles use) shown a little above its middle, where the full profile art cut to a band lost some units' eyes; the picker's side card the same. The two offline toggles are the **Practice** row (Dummy bots, Spectator — each on or off alone, lit yellow) under a new **Bots** row (I choose | Random: who picks the bots' units at GO!!, `state.botPick`). The foot: **Guide** and **Server** on the left (they were a "?" and "SRV" at the top-right, and the lone "?" read as the guide), and on the right **Random unit** and **All random** as dice with their words, then GO!!. **All random** no longer starts the match by itself: it rolls your unit (your Trio roster) and sets the Bots row to Random, so GO!! asks nothing more; the pickers' All Random card does the same and returns to the lobby. A site open lands on Saori (as before) and **Town** (Plain Field until now).

**The lobby's third pass** (PT, b1105o, 2026-11-05; the user's next five notes) — the option rows are taller and further apart (38 px, 10 px between; five of them: Play as / Teams / Roster / Rules / Practice). The Where row is gone: **ONLINE** is a sticker of GO!!'s height beside it ("vs player" under the word; it opens the online lobby as the old button did). The Bots row is gone with it: **All random · GO** rolls your unit (your Trio roster) and every bot and starts the match at once — the pickers' All Random card does the same — while GO!! alone runs the bot pickers as ever. The guide's round **"?"** is back at the top-right; Server stays a small pill at the foot's left. The **Trio** card draws the roster as the user's sketch: three slanted panels side by side, each on its unit's class colour with the face-centred thumbnail, the slashes between them the ink showing through, a slot still to pick a yellow "?" panel; the three names under them, and "n more to pick" while the roster is short (GO!! then opens the pickers for the missing slots alone).

**The lobby's fourth pass** (PT, b1105p, 2026-11-05; the user's next five notes) — the guide's **"?"** sits in the sheet's own top-right corner (a direct child of the sheet, not of the centred head). The **Server** pill is gone from the lobby: **Online** leads to an online connect page — the status chip, a word on the sleeping server, **Server Boot-up** (the debug panel, as before), **Leave** back to the lobby — in place of the bare "Connecting…" overlay, at the first connect and at every reconnect (renderOnlineUi's 'connecting'). **All random** rolls every slot of both cards (you — your Trio roster —, the ally in 2v2, the enemy, the second enemy) and shows them on the cards; it no longer starts the match (b1105o's All random · GO; user: "All random+Go is not ideal"). **GO!!** starts with nothing left to ask when every card is full, else the pickers for the missing picks alone, slot by slot (you, ally, enemy, enemy 2). An **enemy card** stands on the right where the map's card was — the same card as yours: one unit in a Duel 1v1, otherwise a row of slanted panels per slot (two rows in 2v2) and a line of names per slot (YOU / ALLY, ENEMY / ENEMY 2); its Pick / Change runs the enemy's pickers ("Select Enemy Unit", "Select Enemy 2 Unit"). A card's **Pick** fills the missing picks alone while something is missing (Saori, the default, stays as slot 1, so a fresh Trio's pickers start at 2/3); **Change** on a full card asks the roster(s) afresh; the pickers' All Random card fills the rest of that card only and returns to the lobby (in b1105o it started the match from there). The **map** moved to a chip at the foot's left (thumbnail, name, MAP · section · size, Change → the map page); the chip yields width first, so the four buttons never wrap on a landscape phone, and under 760 px the foot wraps (the chip full width, the buttons centred in a row of their own). A single roster's three names may take two lines (never a cut name); a 2v2 card lists its two rosters in Rubik 11.5 px with thin spaces. Verified headless (`ui/ui_shots5.mjs`, 844×390 and 412×915): the corner "?" 9 px from the edges; All random refreshes both cards with no picker and no match; GO!! after it opens the HUD with no picker; the 2v2 Trio cards show two rows of three panels and fill from All random; the Trio card's Pick runs (2/3)(3/3) and returns to the lobby, Change asks (1/3), the enemy card asks "Select Enemy Unit (2/3)", GO!! with the enemy trio missing asks the same; Online shows the connect page (Server Boot-up / Leave) and Leave returns to the lobby with no overlay left.

**The lobby's fifth pass** (PT, b1105q, 2026-11-05; the user's next five notes) — the **enemy and the map swap places**: the map's card is back in the right column and shows its **whole square picture** (`object-fit: contain`, letterboxed on ink, 134 px tall; user: "the full uncropped image, not the further cropped version" — MAP_DATA.thumb stays the picker's wide tile's business), and the enemy side is a **chip at the foot's left**: a strip of its units' faces (small slanted panels, one row per slot; in 2v2 Trio the two rows stacked so the names keep their room), the names with ENEMY / ENEMY 2 pips before them, Pick / Change; the whole chip runs the enemy's pickers. A **2v2 Duel** card is one row split left / right (you | ally) instead of two full-width rows. Every roster panel is now a flex item of its row, overlapping its neighbour by 15 px and clipped to a parallelogram (slanted edges 18 px), so the **face sits in the middle of its own panel** (user: "the face should be in the middle"; the old thirds-of-the-row geometry showed a slice offset from the face), and the single card's wide band is taken lower on the square thumbnail (`center 70%`: the eyes sit at about 62% of its height, so the old 40% band cut them in a short row). The **Random unit** button is gone (All random stays). Small things: the size line uses no-break spaces, the sub line keeps 52 px clear of the Change word, a 2v2 Trio card's sub reads "three each, in turn". Verified headless (`ui/ui_shots6.mjs`): the foot holds in 390 px with no Random unit button; the map card's image is the whole 1024-square, contained; the 2v2 Duel card is one row of two panels; the 2v2 Trio card two rows of three and the chip six faces in two stacked rows; the card's Pick runs (2/3)(3/3); the chip's Pick asks "Select Enemy Unit (2/3)"; the map card opens "Select Map"; GO!! after All random opens the HUD with no picker; portrait wraps the chip full width and the buttons under it.

**Faces, the rifles' colour, the memo tape** (PT, b1105r, 2026-11-05; the user's next notes) — **Face placement**: every picture box of a unit (the lobby card's wide band, every slanted panel, the chip's small faces, the picker's side card) is a `face-box` — a CSS size container — and `UNIT_FACE` (main.js) gives a unit's face centre x and eye line y as fractions of its square thumbnail, read off a contact sheet with a 10% grid (`ui/face_sheet.mjs`; 0.5 / 0.62 unless set: Fubuki 0.43 / 0.63, Haruka 0.47, Atsuko y 0.68, Aris / Asuna / Koyuki / Kurumi x 0.52, Hina 0.48, Rabu 0.53 / 0.63). `object-position` is computed from the box's own width and height in container units so that point sits at the box's horizontal centre and at 44% of its height — a tall box crops the sides, a wide box the top and bottom, and a min()/max() pair makes the other axis 0 so the picture always covers the box (user: the eyes "should be higher" on a single character — the side card's band was cut at 42% from the top, where the eyes of these thumbnails are at about 62% and Atsuko's at 68%, so Fubuki's eyes sat on its bottom edge and Atsuko's below it; and Fubuki "cropped a bit more to the right": her face is at 43% of her thumbnail, so a centred crop showed it left of the panel). The side card's band is 100 px now (88). Browsers without container units leave the picture centred. **Rifle** (RF: Fubuki, Aris) has its own class colour, lavender `#C9B2FF`, no longer AR's cyan (user: "rifle and AR are not the same category"), and the picker's filter row has a **Rifle** chip; AR no longer folds the rifles in. **The memo tape**: a translucent yellow strip, torn at both ends, stuck over the top edge of the pickers' side cards (unit and map), left of centre, a few degrees off (user: "inside the selection menus, i want that yellow memo tape style" — the design canvas's F map card); in portrait, where the card is a scrolling overlay, it sits just inside the edge. Verified headless (`ui/ui_shots7.mjs`, `ui/ui_shots8.mjs`): the computed object-position of the lobby card (−64 px), the side card on Fubuki (−90 px) and Atsuko (−100 px), Fubuki's middle panel (−11 px sideways), the chip's and the 2v2 rows' panels; the Rifle filter shows unit10 and unit7, AR unit1, unit9, unit14; the RF tag is lavender, AR cyan; the tape 96 × 22 px at the top edge of both side cards and inside the portrait overlay.

**HUD colours, the spectator ring, the tape and the map card** (PT, b1105s, 2026-11-05; the user's next notes) — **JUMP** is lime (the stick's old colour) and the **stick's knob red** (`--red #FF4F4F`; user: "give jump button the green tone the joycon has. change joycon green to red"). **Spectator**: the watched unit's corner bar wears a thick yellow ring inside an ink line and a wide halo that pulses yellow / white (1.1 s; still under reduced motion), and the Trio icon row's watched line glows yellow too (user: the white glow "is not obvious enough"; the ring stops 6 px out so the player's bar's ring just meets the ally's bar). **The memo tape in portrait** hangs over the card's top edge as in landscape: the overlay card no longer clips its outside — its inside scrolls instead when it must (user: "the tape shouldn't be cropped on the top"). **The map picker's info card** shows the whole square picture (contain on ink, 164 px tall in landscape, a square box in portrait; user: "the info card is not using the uncropped image"). **The folder and the memo** (PT, b1105t–u, 2026-11-05; user: "make the character box on the left file folder style and the map on the right a memo-pinned-on-the-wall style … implement that to all character cards and map cards") — every unit card is a sheet in a **manila file folder** and every map card a **memo on the wall**. The lobby's left column is the **LINE-UP folder** (`.lobby-folder`, manila `#F6D27A`, a 24 px tab reading LINE-UP, a thumb notch on its right edge) holding two sheets: your unit's card (its YOUR UNIT sticker moved to the sheet's right, under the tab) and the enemy's slip (the face strip, the ENEMY / ENEMY 2 pip on its own line over the names, Change / Pick as a magenta sticker on the slip's top-right corner; in 2v2 Trio the strip's two rows stack). The map's card on the right is the **memo**: a white note with cyan ruled lines under the picture, a strip of the yellow tape over its top edge, centred, the whole square picture on ink, MAP · section · size, Change on the name's line. The grid is two rows now: the folder spans both, the option rows and the memo fill the first, the foot's buttons (All random / Online / GO!!) the second. The unit picker's side card is a folder too (tab UNIT FILE, a paper clip on the sheet's corner), the map picker's a memo (the tape over the top edge, no pin — b1105u, user); the floating cards of the online pickers follow (`.pp-folder` / `.pp-memo`). **SR** is the sniper rifle's tag (was SN; user), in the tiles, the filter chip and the class colours. Verified headless (`ui/ui_shots10.mjs`): the 1v1 and the 2v2 Trio lobbies fit 390 px (sheet scroll height 390), the slip opens "Select Enemy Unit", the Sniper filter shows unit3 and unit6 with SR tags and no SN left, the folder card carries the tab and the clip, the memo card the 84 px tape and no pin; portrait keeps the folder and the memo side by side under the rows. (b1105v, user: "make the file slightly longer so the ally box and enemy box do not overlap") the sheet and the slip are 12 px apart on every screen — the slip's Change sticker, 9 px over its top edge, no longer touches the sheet — and on a screen at least 440 px tall (desktop, portrait) the folder is longer: the sheet 208 px, its art 118 px, the two 16 px apart. The slip's text lost its ENEMY pip and its corner sticker names the side instead of saying Change — ENEMY / ENEMY TRIO / ENEMY TEAM (user: "remove the enemy text box; change the change text to enemy text"); a short roster still says "n more to pick" in its small line. The design canvas (artifact 1489ieHXfUJZ3okkbwshBu, round 5) holds the folder-and-memo lobby as built and a **proposal for the match HUD**, not built: the corner HP bars grow into the F sample's chips (an ink pill with the portrait in a ring, the name with YOU / ENEMY, the bar, the number; the Trio row as faces under it; the ally's and the second enemy's chips smaller under the first two; the spectator's watched chip with the b1105s yellow ring), and the bottom boost bar becomes a yellow BOOST sticker in the sample's ammo-chip style (the word, an ink gauge with a white knob, the number; magenta when low).

**The HP chips and the BOOST sticker** (PT, b1105w, 2026-11-05; the design canvas's round 5, built on the user's "implement the HUD on PT") — the match HUD's corner bars and the Trio weapon-icon rows are gone; each slot has an **HP chip** (`.hp-chip`, main.js `hpChipHTML` / `renderHpChip`): an ink pill with a white rim and an ink outline, the portrait of the unit on the field in a ring of the side's colour (the thumbnail at 150% with its UNIT_FACE point at the frame's centre), the name in Titan One, the side word (YOU / ALLY / ENEMY / ENEMY 2) in the colour, and the bar — as long as the unit's HP (`--hp-scale`), a round knob at the fill's head (`--w`) — no number. In **Trio** the roster's other two units sit at the chip's far end as small faces, a fallen one greyed (the reserve, from the slot's roster and cursor offline, the snapshot's roster fields online). Yours top-left, the enemy's mirrored top-right; in 2v2 the ally's and the second enemy's smaller under them (cyan, yellow). Online the chips anchor by server slot as the bars did, in the slot's colour (`--c`); the side word says which is you (the thin white "self" rim is gone). **Spectator**: no side words while the viewer is a spectator — the Practice toggle or an online spectator (user: "if it's spectator mode, remove you/ally/enemy/enemy 2 text; if the spectator mode is triggered by player dying, then these text remains") — and the WATCHED chip (the camera's unit, by the toggle or because the player is out) wears the b1105s yellow ring with a WATCHING tag; a slot out for good dims with a DOWN tag. **Sudden Death** hides the chips' bars alone. The fullscreen button docks right of the left chip by `--left-chip-right`, a ResizeObserver on the chip (the chip is as wide as its name, its bar and its faces). The **BOOST sticker**: yellow, level, at the bottom centre — the word and an ink gauge with a white knob (`--bw`), no number; the fill turns magenta under 30% (`.low`). The DOM of a chip is rebuilt only when its unit, its word or its reserve changes; the bar and the classes move every frame. Verified headless (`ui/ui_shots11.mjs`): a 1v1 Trio match shows both chips with two reserve faces each and the fullscreen button 10 px right of the left chip; a 2v2 Spectator match shows four chips with no side words and the ring on the player's chip, moving to the ally's on TARGET; a 2v2 Sudden Death match shows the chips with the bars hidden (`display: none`) and the words in place; a 1v1 on a 1280 px window. Not exercised headless: the DOWN tag and the after-death WATCHING ring (they need a death). (b1105x, the user's three notes on V472) the chips' far side has 18 px of padding (16 on the small chips) and the reserve faces start 12 px after the track, so the knob — 8 px past the track at full HP — stays 10 px inside the rim ("the HP bar should stay well inside the box"); the BOOST fill is the SPRINT button's magenta at every level, no ink and no low-state colour ("shouldn't be black"); and the round frames show the thumbnail at 125% with the face's middle at the centre, clamped to keep the frame covered, so the whole head fits ("face should be at the center" — 150% with the eyes at 46% cut the hair and the chin). (b1105y–z) the chips were levelled for a build on a misread of "still tilted" and **tilt again** (±1.5°, as the boards had them; the BOOST sticker alone is level) — the user meant the faces, which sat left and high in their browser: the round frames now place the face by an **inline transform computed in JS** (`faceTransform`: the thumbnail at 130%, moved so its UNIT_FACE point meets the frame's centre, clamped to ±15% so the frame stays covered) instead of the CSS calc / clamp / container-unit maths of b1105x, which rendered differently from browser to browser. The design canvas's round 6 (`End_F`, `End_F_2v2`) proposes the end-of-match page — the map memo in the middle, a roster panel each side in the HUD's round frames with the fallen greyed, Select Unit and REMATCH below — not built; after the user's notes on it the panels are wider (280 px, the last frame 24 px in from the edge), the memo carries the picture and the map's name alone (no MAP · section · size line, no rules caption) and its faces use the same transform.

**The memo's die** (PT, b1105aa, 2026-11-05; user: "add a random map dice at the bottom right of the map memo paper that picks randomly from both scene map and concept map pool") — the lobby's map memo has a **die sticker** at its bottom-right on the ruled lines (`.memo-dice`: the All random button's die, magenta, 36 px, tilted −6°): a tap rolls **one draw over both tabs' maps together** (`rollLobbyMap`: every map of MAP_DATA but the noRandom ones — Plain Field, District, the Backrooms, the Shooting Range, the same manual-only set the picker's Random cards skip — and never the map already on the memo), the memo shows the new map at once with a half-second spin of the die, and the picker stays shut; the rest of the memo still opens the picker. Shooter mode is as before: the Spectator toggle, an online spectator and the after-death camera still ring the camera's chip. **The end-of-match page, the command cards' colours, the reserve's order** (PT, b1105ac, 2026-11-05; user: "implement the ending screen in PT", "use the HP bar color as base, make the color of command card aligned", "the next-up unit should be placed at the uncovered position") — the match's end is the design canvas's round-6 page (main.js `showEndPage`, shared by the offline `showEndMenu` and the online `showOnlineEndMenu`): the verdict as the sticker title (YOU WIN / YOU LOSE; TEAM 1 WINS / TEAM 2 WINS for a spectator; MATCH ENDED on an online tie), a **roster panel** each side — the HUD chip's ink and white rim as a card, TEAM 1 (the viewer's side, left: the player's team, or the camera slot's team for an online spectator) and TEAM 2, the yellow WINNER sticker on the side that won — with a row per slot: the slot's word (YOU / ALLY, ENEMY / ENEMY 2; SLOT 1 / SLOT 2 for a spectator) and the slot's roster in the HUD's round frames with the names: a fallen unit greyed with a slash, the unit on the field alive with the dot, a reserve unit plain (Trio from the roster and its cursor, offline `trioRosters` / `rosterIdx`, online the snapshot's roster fields; Duel the one unit); the **map's memo** in the middle (the lobby's — the tape, the ruled lines, the whole picture on ink and the name alone); **Select Unit** (Leave online) and **REMATCH** below, the lobby's GO!! style. The rows' colours are the chips' (lime / cyan, magenta / yellow). The sheet centres the three; portrait wraps them and scrolls. **Command mode's cards** wear the HP chips' colours now (`DIO_SLOT_META`: lime YOU, cyan ALLY, magenta ENEMY 1, yellow ENEMY 2 — the card's border, its HP fill, its marker square and its line; online the slot palette as before, which the online chips share), and the cards' stamina gauge is white (two yellow bars on ENEMY 2's card read as one). **The chips' reserve faces** are ordered so the NEXT-UP unit is the outermost, last-drawn face — whole, not under its neighbour: the fallen first (inner), then the upcoming from the farthest to the next (`renderHpChip`). Verified headless (`ui/ui_shots14.mjs`, `ui/ui_shots15.mjs`): a 1v1 Trio ended by zeroing HP from the dev state shows YOU WIN with WINNER on TEAM 1, your row one out / one standing / one in reserve against three out, the memo's picture loaded and 172 × 122 inside it, and REMATCH starts a match; a 2v2 Duel under the Spectator toggle shows TEAM 2 WINS with SLOT 1 / SLOT 2 words and Select Unit returns to the lobby; a 1v1 on a 1280 px window and a 2v2 Trio in portrait (the three stacked, the sheet scrolling) render; a 2v2 Trio in Command shows each card's border and fill equal to its chip's ring colour and the chips' last reserve face equal to the slot's next prebuilt unit.

**ArchiveVS; the online room's names; the last kill on the end page; bigger reserve faces; the cards' names** (b1105ad, 2026-11-05; the user's notes on the 0.9.5 line's first online matches) — the game is **ArchiveVS** in the lobby's title, the guide's first line, the page title and the web manifest (was GunVS / Gun VS Gun). **The online queue room** names a player's units by character (Saori, not "Unit 1 / Assault Rifle"), as the bots' rows did, and the host's "tap to change" hint sits LEFT of a bot row's names (`.roster-hint`), so every row's names end on the same right edge. **The end page online** read the mechs, which are mirrored only in the playing frames — the frame that turns the page over mirrors nothing, so the last unit killed still showed alive (user: "the last killed unit is shown as alive"); `endRowUnits` now reads the LAST SNAPSHOT (the server sends the final tick's before `match:end`) and, on a knockout, marks the losing team's every unit out whatever the data says. **The chips' reserve faces** are 34 px, side by side 4 px apart (24 px overlapping by 7 until now; user: "bigger without overlapping with others or changing other existing layouts") — the chip alone grows, by its own width (a Trio chip 320 px, was 289), the rows and the fullscreen button's dock follow as before. **Command mode's cards** name the unit, in capitals, as its chip does (user: "change the YOU / ENEMY 1 / ALLY / ENEMY 2 to unit name as in HP box"); the side is the card's colour. Verified headless: an online Trio 1v1 against the branch's own server (`ui/ui_online1.mjs`, the server on a local port) — the room reads "You — Saori Marina Hoshino" and "tap to change (BOT) Saori Saori Saori" with the hint's right edge left of the names', and after the idle host's three units fall (51 s) the end page reads YOU LOSE with the three out and the bot's unit standing, the last snapshot at hp 0; the Command and HUD checks (`ui_shots15.mjs`, `ui_shots13.mjs`) show the cards named by unit, the chips 320 px wide, two rows 15 px apart and no two boxes overlapping; the offline end page (`ui_shots14.mjs`) as before. One brain, online and offline (the user asked): `shared/src/sim/nbrain.js` drives every bot — the server's `tickBot` and the offline client's `updateEnemy` each host it — and the Sudden Death brain is shared the same way.

**The next-up marker** (b1105ae, 2026-11-05; user: "now user cannot tell who's up next — add a little glowing bar or triangle at the marked areas so user will know the one next to it is the next unit") — in a Trio chip a small **glowing white triangle** (`.chip-next`, 8 × 12 px, a soft yellow halo, breathing) sits between the track and the reserve and points at the **next-up** face, which is now the FIRST of the reserve, right beside it; the upcoming units follow in roster order and the fallen come last, greyed (b1105ac had put the next-up outermost because the faces overlapped then; since b1105ad they stand apart, so the natural order returns). No marker when nothing is left to come. The chip grows by the marker alone (a Trio chip 333 px). PT and the 0.9.5 line alike. Verified headless (`ui/ui_shots15.mjs`, `ui/ui_shots13.mjs`): in a 2v2 Trio every chip's first reserve face is the slot's next prebuilt unit and the marker lies between the track's end and that face on both sides; the rows keep 15 px apart with no two boxes overlapping.

**The memos' red crayon** (b1105af, 2026-11-05; the design canvas's round 7, the user's pick: style B) — District's and Compound's little "· more stamina" leaves the lobby memo's size line and the map card's note line, and Backrooms (Anomaly) reads **Backrooms** on the memos (`MAP_DATA.shortName`); in their place a line of **red crayon** on the memo's ruled lines — "More Stamina!" (any map with a `cardNote`, or its own `crayon`), "Anomaly!" — in **Permanent Marker** (bundled, Apache 2.0, `client/src/fonts`), 17 px, four degrees off, multiplied into the paper so the rules show through the stroke, its edge roughened by an SVG noise filter (`#crayon`, put on the page by main.js before the first lobby). The lobby memo and the end page's memo place it by hand, left of the die (`.memo-crayon`); the picker's side memo and the floating card let it flow under the size line and the tags (`.memo-crayon-flow`). The picker's grid tiles keep the full names. **The narrow landscape phone** (b1105ag, 2026-11-05; user, with pictures of a phone about 640 × 342 CSS px in landscape: "the mobile end layout is off, too much overlapping in game, and i want the main menu layout same as desktop end") — **The lobby** keeps the desktop's three columns on every landscape screen: the wrap (`@media (max-width: 760px)`) is for portrait alone, and a landscape screen narrower than the columns (722 px + the sheet's 32) **zooms the grid** to fit (`showSelectMenu` fitLobby: CSS `zoom`, re-measured on resize — 0.84 at 640 px, 0.93 at 700, 1 from 754 up). **The match HUD**: an HP chip never passes its half of the screen less PAUSE, the fullscreen button and the margins (`max-width: calc(50vw - 112px)`): the bar gives first (down to 56 px), then the name is cut with an ellipsis; the face and the reserve keep their size. The fullscreen button docks right of the left chip but never past PAUSE's left edge (`min(--left-chip-right, 50% - 96px)`). The BOOST sticker is shorter under 720 px (32 px tall, the track `min(150px, 22vw)`). Verified headless (`ui/ui_shots17.mjs`) at 640 × 342, 700 × 360 and 844 × 390: the lobby shows three columns with no sideways scroll (zoom 0.842 / 0.925 / 1) and the folder, the options and the memo side by side; in a 1v1 Spectator match and a 2v2 Trio match no chip overlaps PAUSE, the fullscreen button or the other chip, and the BOOST sticker clears the stick and the ammo dial (at 640 the Trio chips' bars are at their 56 px floor); a 412 × 915 portrait lobby still wraps.

**The narrow phone, second pass** (b1105ah–ai, 2026-11-05; the user's notes on b1105ag's pictures: "no '…', make sure text fit", "the full screen button is overlapping", "HP bar / button / thumbnails / triangle overlapping, cut off text, HP bar too short; if one of YOU / ALLY / ENEMY and the unit name must be omitted for the space, omit the side word") — **The lobby's** middle column asks 320 px at the least (was 250), so every option pill holds its words at the zoomed sizes; the zoom's reference is 792 px. **The chips' name line** wraps instead of cutting: the name never shrinks or takes an ellipsis; when the side word does not fit beside it, the word drops to a second line that the line's height clips — the word goes whole, by the space alone. **Under 720 px** the chips come in a smaller cut (46 px, a 34 px face, a 120 px bar, 26 px reserve faces, a smaller marker), PAUSE is a size down with the **fullscreen button under it** in the middle (between the second row's chips in 2v2), so each first-row chip may run to 58 px short of the middle and the second row to 44 px; the second row sits 70 px down; the thumb buttons and the stick are a size down so the second row clears DODGE. **At 844 × 390** the second row steps in by 108 px and is a little narrower: DODGE's top reaches into the row's band there, so the second enemy's chip must end before the thumb cluster, and the ally's steps in the same for one stagger. The fullscreen button docks 16 px off the left chip (clear of its WATCHING ring) and never past PAUSE. Verified headless (`ui/ui_shots17.mjs`) at 640 × 342, 700 × 360 and 844 × 390: no option pill is cut; in a 1v1 Spectator match and a 2v2 Trio match no chip overlaps PAUSE, the fullscreen button, DODGE, the stick or the other chips, the fullscreen button sits 13 px or more from every chip, every name shows whole, the side words show where they fit and go whole where they do not, and the bars read 120 / 120 (1v1) and 114 / 111 / 120 / 120 (2v2 Trio) at 640, 150 and 128 / 128 / 112 / 112 at 844; the portrait lobby still wraps.

**The pickers' card a touch smaller, by plain numbers; the fullscreen button in the menus' top-left corner** (b1105am, 2026-11-05; user: "just a little bit smaller so the whole file can be displayed. the full screen button should be in the up left corner") — on the user's phone the b1105al fit still cut the unit file's bottom by about 9 px: that fit read the zoomed header row's `offsetHeight`, and what such a reading means under `zoom` differs between browsers. `fitPickerMenu` measures nothing zoomed now: the header is 40 px (its back button), the card 378 px (unit file) / 341 px (map memo) — the card at zoom 1 with the top margin for the folder tab or the memo tape and the 2° tilt — and the budget is the visible height (the menu's or `innerHeight`, whichever is less) minus the menu's top padding, two flex gaps and 12 px to spare. The fullscreen button in menus sits at 10 / 10 (inside the safe area) instead of 36 px down; the pickers' header keeps its 40 px left padding, so the back arrow clears it. Verified headless (`ui/ui_shots19.mjs`) at 707 × 327, 640 × 342, 667 × 308, 844 × 390, 1280 × 720 and portrait 412 × 915: the card whole with 14–16 px to spare on the phone sizes (zoom 0.687 at 707 × 327, the same before and after the tap), the button at 10 / 10 clear of the back arrow and the lobby title everywhere.

**The lobby memo one height in every mode, the die under its lines; the folder as long; the lobby fits the height too; PRACTICE whole; the thumb buttons by 12vw** (b1105an, 2026-11-05; user: "both the file and the memo can afford to be slightly higher (longer), especially the memo shouldn't shrink itself when the mode is 1v1 and block the dice") — the memo's own height was 235 px (its lines end at 199, the die's 41 px zone began at 194) and the grid row only grew in 2v2 (245), so in 1v1 the die sat on the size line. `.map-slot.memo { min-height: 266px }`: the paper is 266 in 1v1 and 2v2 alike, the die 31–33 px under the lines, and the folder beside it (the same rows) grows as much — 304 px instead of 264 / 284. `fitLobby` now takes the height into account as well (the floor would have overrun a short, wide screen): measured UNZOOMED — the grid's tracks are fixed widths, so its height does not depend on the menu's width and nothing zoomed is read — the zoom is the least of 1, the width fit and (the visible height less what the sheet scrolls beyond the grid at zoom 1) / the grid's height. **The box fade back — the walls go see-through again** (b1105be, 2026-11-06; user: "the transparent effect in concept maps seems to be gone, try to bring them back") — since 9f9dabe (Oct 23, Prison) a registered box is `transparent` only while it fades: the flag flips on as the opacity leaves 1 and off as it comes back, so that a solid box is drawn in the opaque pass. three.js (r152 and later; 0.179 here) bakes that flag into the shader — an opaque material's program forces alpha 1 (its `OPAQUE` define) — and re-picks a material's program only on `needsUpdate`; flipped bare, a fading box kept its opaque program and drew SOLID at opacity 0.25 (the number read 0.25; the pixels did not). Every box fade had gone: the concept maps' boxes and the modelled maps' merged fade copies alike. The flip now sets `needsUpdate`, so the right program is picked — flips happen only at a fade's two ends and each program compiles once — and a fresh box stays transparent through its first two passes (`fadeWarm`), so its blended program compiles with the match's first frame (the intro) rather than as a hitch at its first fade. Verified headless on arena2: the mechanism reproduced on the built page (a big building at opacity 0.25 with the flag alone → solid; with `needsUpdate` → the building behind shows through), and with the fix the building between the camera and the watched unit fades to 0.25 and the unit shows through it (the before/after pictures in the session's report).

**Neon's scaffold made right — the review's findings** (b1105bk, 2026-11-06; user: "fix Blockade as you suggested. To PT and 9.5" — the V429/V430 review's confirmed findings, re-checked that morning on the current files and all still there but the tooling one) — the collision data, the model, the client and one sight rule each took a part; the data is the README recipe's reading with four patch steps on top (`slits`, `decks`, the extension joint, the deck flag), 7765 boxes and 3650 floors. *The corner slits (data):* the room-side pocket of the inner-corner slit at (−4.44, 40.38) let a plain jump carry the body through the corner onto the road (436 of 504 jumps) — above the 0.858 m bases the airborne body drifted into the slit's mouth, which had no box over the bases, and the push-out of six faceless "air" boxes over the pocket flung it on; one `noProjectile` box in the mouth (h 0.858..5.253) and the six air boxes gone: 0 of 503 plain, 0 of 704 sprint jumps, 0 of 3040 room-side runs of each kind, 0 of 864 jumps at every heading, the street side held as before, the pocket still a place to stand in and walk out of. Rounds stopped on invisible boxes along lines the eye sees clear through both slits (13.4 % / 6.4 % of such lines; 2 of 9 game-tick cases flew): 29 boxes at the slits re-partitioned from the model's faces — solid only where a post section, a brace stub (a four-step stair along its wedge) or a base footing is, the rest `noProjectile` — 9 of 9 cases fly, 0.96 % / 0.45 % of clear lines still stop at the slit (post corners grazed under 4 cm, or low shots on a base), no round through a post face. The 0.858–1.002 m foot band under 60 % of the scaffold's wall columns, through which shotgun pellets crossed see-through fences (937 / 767 shots): 43 boxes, one per wall run, close it — 0 of 12,282 wall columns open, 0 pellets through any fence, rifles unchanged, 72 street walks along the fills unchanged, the east wall's last metre closed with them. The jamb at the west cage's north opening: seven boxes trimmed from x −4.300 to the post face −4.183 (an 11.7 cm invisible wall gone; rounds along it 150 of 150 stopped → 0). *Floor B (data):* one floor at 5.7775 m — the model's frame-rim top — over the whole deck and both joints, in place of a 5.737 floor, a 5.787 lid strip and 5.918 / 5.973 strips that stood a unit 14–20 cm in the air (the cages' toe boards spread over the deck by the solid reading's 0.25 m cells); the original deck's and the copy's last metre toward floor B slope down to it. The landing plant at the north-west corner (a hidden 19 cm drop onto the lid, then 0.60 s frozen; 95 of 475 starts) → 0; walk lines airborne over the joints (123 of 158 and 45 of 76, up to 208 ms) → 0; 32 beam boxes' bottoms raised from 8.2525 to 8.431 m, the frames' visible underside, so a jump under the walkway no longer throws the body sideways (0.54 m in one frame → 0 of 7083 standing points); a 2 m hole in floor B's east rim (x 19.31..19.56, z 35.37..37.37 — the model has the rim) filled. The extension's east rim strip, where it meets the original deck's sloped last metre (x 2.81..3.25, z 41.12..41.87), is one slope from the slope's own height up to 5.918 m, so walking north there drops nothing (it had dropped 15–20 cm off the 5.973 m strip, 3 lines airborne 128–144 ms). *The deck slopes and bot sight (shared code):* `sightHitsSurface` (physics.js) reads a plane as a solid mass down to the street — right for a ramp on the ground, wrong for a slope in a deck on an open frame: the ten new slopes made the bots blind at eye level under them (the old joint slit: 1362 of 4641 eye pairs → 0). A plane the data marks `deck` is now read like the flat deck beside it — only crossing it blocks — (`materializeSurface` in arena.js keeps the flag; every slope of the fixes carries it, no other map has one): 1362 of 4641 again, rounds and walking untouched. *The catwalk (model):* it stood 0.48 m north of the copied walkway's end piece, so a body walking east along its north strip walked off the end and fell 8.5 m. Moving it south was no good — the cage's top ring and Object_441 are in the way of its south-west corner — so Node_204_204_cw / Node_205_205_cw are trimmed to z 29.88..33.365 (scaled 0.871 along z about their south end; the ends set a few millimetres inside the solid reading's rows, so no row reads as bars with a hole between), and the walkway's south band, which had no floor west of x 5.57, is closed by a 0.735 m piece of the scaffold's own fence panel (Node_216_216_copy_stub / Node_217_217_copy_stub) standing on the walkway's west edge from the catwalk's south-east corner. The data regenerated with the README's recipe (12 boxes and 10 floors out, 13 and 8 in, all inside the catwalk rectangle), the route grids rebuilt (20,630 walkable nodes). Joint falls 77 of 300 walks → 0 of 282; no new standing place. The original deck's east edge north of the catwalk — open, the model has no railing along it — now runs 0.5 m further south, to the catwalk's new north end. *The decks' fade (client):* the extension's deck, the two b1102h decks and the catwalk's grating were never fade things — the tallness rule saw 0.47 m of them over the solid reading's 5.30 m floor strips — so a unit under them vanished behind an opaque grating; `fadeForce` on the neon GLB_MAPS entry names the nine nodes that are fade things whatever the rule finds (`buildGlbArena`): Neon's fade meshes 229 → 240, the other modelled maps unchanged, the grating over a unit at opacity 0.10 like its neighbours. **Neon's transporters** (PT b1105fk, b1105fl and b1105fo, 2026-11-10; user: "make a golden glowing circle area at this wall ... Within the wall there would be golden text showing \"Hold Jump to Transport\". When unit near to the wall holds jump button, a golden half-transparent circle timer will start running with same style text \"TRANSPORTING\" showing up. The timer takes 1 sec to complete the circle. When the timer is done, the unit will be transported to this position ... The position also has a golden circle area on the ground, but no text. The transportation can be done from this spot as well. Bot should know this as part of the path", and "Transporting unit's own crosshair indicator is enough") — two pads, a new kind of arena record (`shared/src/sim/pads.js`, rows in arena.js `ARENA_PADS`, as the ladders are): A at the tunnel's T junction, a golden ring on its floor that is the zone itself (0.54 m, r 1.1 m round model x −3.30, z 8.00, the middle of the junction's 2.7 m square, 0.25 m clear of the north wall), and the words alone on the north wall over the tube (b1105fj's seal, 3.36 m up; a `sign`, no zone) — b1105fl, user: "Move the transporting circle and its detection on the floor instead of on the wall. The text stays on the wall though, without detection"; b1105fk had drawn the circle round the words on the wall and set the zone 0.9 m in front of it; B on the south tower's walled walkway (21.24 m, r 1.0 m round x −3.31, z 19.76), a ring on its floor. Standing in a zone, a fresh press of jump starts the charge instead of a jump; while the button is held the unit stands (no move, shot, dodge or jump), and 1 s after the press it stands on the other pad's arrival point (a spot beside it if another body is there). Letting go or leaving the zone drops the charge; a hit does not; a unit that jumped in holding the button is not caught; after the move (or a charge dropped with the button held) the held button is spent until let go, so an arrival never sends a unit straight back; no cooldown. Online the hold is the frame's new `jumpDown` (the button's own state; `jump` stays a tap for a unit that does not fly), so the server runs the second and the client's prediction agrees; offline the client runs the same rule. The charging player alone sees a golden ring round its crosshair (the screen's centre in the angled view) filling in the second, TRANSPORTING under it. Bots: each pad is a link on the route grid (`attachLadderLinks`' third argument; cost the second at the climb's speed, never under the cells it spans), taken where the ladders are — travel, Pursue / Maze and command orders, not Defense, the Hide search or Sudden Death; the follower walks to the zone with the goal set (`botPadGoal`), the pad charges and moves it, the brain sleeps for the second. B was reachable by no one but Aris before; every spawn now routes to it through A, and a drop link still takes a bot off B. Verified: `pads.test.js` (8: the records on their floors, the hold, the let-go, the jump-in, the latch, no shot or dodge meanwhile, the second arrival beside the first, the route from every spawn, a bot's trip to a target on B, the snapshot), the shared suite 194 of 194; headless offline both ways, the ring, a jump off the pads still a jump, an offline bot's trip; on the real server with a client's frames, A to B, the held button spent, back to A, a 300 ms press dropped. b1105fl: each pad's ring lies whole on its floor and clear of every wall (a test), the shots from the stem and the real server's run again. **The silver pair** (b1105fo; user: "Add another pair of transport points as well. use silver instead of golden this time"): C on the ground floor south of the bridge, in front of the framed door with the diamond sign (0.54 m, r 1.0 m round model x 4.24, z 67.45, where the user stood; the same floor and nothing in the way 2.65 m round), and D in the covered bridge near its south end (20.24 m, r 1.0 m round x 4.07, z 56.40, where the user stood; the walls 3.3 and 4.0 m off), each sending to the other, no sign. A record's `color` (padRow, 'gold' unless set) is its look alone: the client draws a silver ring and halo (main.js `PAD_LOOKS`) and the charging player's ring and TRANSPORTING in silver (style.css `#pad-ring.pr-silver`); the rule, the hold, the latch and the bots' links are the same for every pad. Routes now take whichever pair is shorter: from the spawns nearest C to the bridge through C, from the others through A, B and the strip. Verified: `pads.test.js` 10 (the records with both pairs and their colours, the user's spots in the zones, C to D and back, a bot's goal on D, the route grid's four links, routes to B and to D from every spawn up through A or C and no other way), the shared suite 196 of 196; headless offline C to D, the held button spent, D to C, the silver ring at the crosshair, the gold A unchanged; on the real server C to D, held on, back to C, a 300 ms press dropped; bots from the five spawns to a player at the bridge's south end, two by the silver pair (there in 3.9 and 8.3 s), three by the gold; the offline client's bot from the ground floor walks to C, charges and arrives on D.

Verified: round the three partitions no standing spot pins its route's goal behind a wall (80 of 7,775 at W50 before); with the player at the user's spot, bots from the eight starts that stuck no longer press into the panel (0% of the time, 79% before) and see the player over half the time (0% before); with the player against the moved panel's south face (z 50.81) they still press its north face now and then, 8 to 15% of the time in stands of 1.7 to 1.9 s (the Maze's beeline after an arrival without sight, nbrain.js, not touched); the west lane now stops at z 50.81 from the south, sight and real rounds across W50 are blocked (0 of 20), the middle lane is clear (19 of 20); the bridge bot runs are as before; the shared suite 196 of 196.

**Town: the east plateau's floor where its grass runs on** (PT b1105fp, 2026-11-10; user, at x 48.19, z −12.31, standing at 0.00 m beside #1413, #1920 and #3361: "there are few big gap causing unit to fall into pit") — the model's grass on the east plateau (its top at 3.85–3.95 m, open sky over it) runs north of the collision's floor: the strip in front of the brick building between the plateau's deck (#1413, z −12) and the terrain tiles' edge (#1920, z −16.13), the lawn east of the building up to where the grass starts down the hill (z −28.4), and a sliver along the arena's east edge (x 66.5..67, z 4..22). The generator had written no floor at that height there, so a body stepped off the deck and fell into the pit under the terrain, 4 to 5 m down (the street level, or the plates at −1.09 m), and stayed there. The fill (`town_fill.mjs` in the session's scratch, on the installed data): every such cell a body reaches from the plateau (a flood at 0.25 m through grass-level cells with no box at body height, from the model's faces and the collision's floors), widened by up to two cells over the grass beside it under something low (a ledge, an awning), is floor at the plateau's own height — 32 flat surfaces at 3.885 m with a box under each to 3.88 m (topBuffer 0.8), as the plateau's own deck — 309 m² in all; and where the new floor's edge meets neither floor nor wall — the hill's top, and the brick building's east face, which had no box at body height — an invisible wall 0.25 m thick from the floor to the flight ceiling (noProjectile, so shots and sight pass). The things on the lawn (the sign #3361, the posts, the trees) already had their boxes. Nav rebuilt. Verified: the flood from the plateau reaches no grass the collision lacks (309 m² before, none now); walks north off the deck at x 43 to 50 stay at 3.88 m (they fell to −1.09 m before), along the lawn to the hill's edge and jumping at it stop at the wall, the east edge at z 8 stays (it fell before); a body set at the user's spot stands at 3.88 m; Town's tests 26 of 26.

**Town: the row houses' basement windows, and the wall over two door awnings, closed to a body** (b1105gj, 2026-11-12, PT then 9.7, online and offline; user, at x −37.25, z 21.06, feet 1.49 m, inside the red row house on the west street: "there's a breach around here. A quick fix will do.", then "Update cameras and Town to 9.7.") — a row house's front is read as thin walls: its base 4 cm in front of the wall and 0.84 m tall (#1986, #1988: x −36.89..−36.85), the wall itself from 1.50 m up (#1961: x −36.95..−36.91, z 18.50..23.25, to 4.65 m). Under a basement window the base is the sill alone, 0.27 m (#1987, z 20.75..22.25), and the window over it — 1.5 m wide, 0.27 to 1.50 m — had no box. A box holds a body only while the body's centre is no more than 2 units (0.5 m) under its bottom (`boxHoldsAt`, physics.js): a body on the street, its centre at 0.61 m, is 0.89 m under the wall and steps over the sill as over a kerb, so it walked in through the window. The house is hollow behind its front (the street's floor runs on under it), and a jump inside put the body on the floor behind the front door, 1.49 m: the user's spot. **13 boxes added to `town_data.json`**, the rest of the file untouched (the old text with the boxes spliced in after its last obstacle): each on its wall's own line and as thin, from the floor up to the wall's bottom, over the stretch a body passed and half a metre either side, `topBuffer` 0.8 as the walls about it. A body, a round and a bot's sight stop there as at the wall beside the window. The opening was looked for map-wide, not at the one window (`breach_fix.mjs` in the session's scratch, on the installed data: every thin wall 1.5 m tall or more with an opening under it lower than a unit, 1.6 m, and a floor either side within a step; each stretch then walked by the sim itself, both ways): 15 stretches, 13 of them crossed — 11 basement windows of this kind, and 2 stretches of wall over a door awning, where a body standing on the awning (4.2 m) passed under a wall that begins 1.34 m over it. The boxes, in model metres — the west street, x −36.95..−36.91: z 20.31..22.44 (the user's) and z 25.06..27.19, −0.05..1.50 m; the awning two doors south, x −36.92..−36.88, z 12.53..15.28, 4.19..5.53 m. The north row, z −40.10..−40.06, −0.05..1.50 m: x 0.31..2.44, 3.56..5.69, 8.31..10.44, 19.56..21.69, 24.31..26.44, 27.56..29.69, 32.31..34.44. The south house, z 31.80..31.84: x −32.44..−30.32 and x −27.69..−25.56, −0.05..1.50 m, and its awning, x −30.24..−27.74, 4.21..5.55 m. The two stretches a body did not cross (#5151, #5152, the centre building's two doors at the top of their stairs, x 6.5..8.8, z −1.4 and 2.1: it is held 0.3 to 0.5 m in) are as they were. Wall pieces under 1.5 m tall are outside the scan; a wider run of it (0.5 m or more) lists 34 more short pieces that a body set beside them passes under — over the centre building's barred windows and beside its south stair, a stop sign's plate, the fire truck's ladder over its roof — and by the walks made none is a way in: at the barred windows, from either street, 0 of 246 starts end inside; from the strip beside the stair 0 of 96 end under its landing. They are as they were. The route grid rebuilt: 15,822 → 15,521 nodes, the 301 gone all behind the north row's and the south house's fronts, where a bot could route (the user's house had none); 675 → 672 jump links, no node new. Verified on the old data and the new. The sim's own walks at the three fronts, every 0.125 m along each from the street (walking, jumping, sprinting): 101, 110 and 101 of 509 starts ended behind a front before, none now; standing on the two awnings (311 starts, 0.30 to 0.70 m off the wall): 36 ended behind the wall before, none now; from the recess beside each front's end, 32 walks each, none ends inside the house. The scan again on the new data: the 2 held stretches, none crossed. In the PT site's built page (headless, offline, free pov, the forward key held from the street): at the user's window the unit went on to x −43.2, 6.3 m inside, and now stops at x −36.62, its radius off the front; the house's second window, a north-row window and a south-house window the same (x −42.7 → −36.62; z −46.1 → −39.77; z 33.31 → 31.51). Shared suite 200 of 200; no page errors. Here the data file is the PT site's byte for byte, and the route grid this tree's builder makes of it (`town_nav.nav`) equals the site's. The server reads the same two files at its warm-up (`server/src/warmup.js`), so an online match is closed with them. On this tree's build the unit stops at the three windows walked: the user's at x −36.62, a north-row window at z −39.77, a south-house window at z 31.51. No page errors; 200 of 200.

**Town: the hedges solid, and three places where the east terraces' ground was missing** (b1105hu, 2026-11-14, PT and 9.7, online and offline; user, with five readouts of Town — x 35.40, z 1.70, feet 3.88 m; and x 34.08 at z 2.98, 9.65, −4.96 and −11.25, feet −0.05 m: "units got stuck at these locations.") — **The five places are hedges.** A hedge is one block to the eye; the reading made thin boxes of its faces and nothing of its inside, so to a body it is a shell. Four are the low hedges at the foot of the east terrace's west wall (x 33.79..34.42, 2.1 m long, 1.13 m high): their faces have no top buffer, so each holds a body up to a metre over its top, and a body that comes down into one from the terrace above stands inside it where neither a walk nor a jump takes it out. The fifth is in the hedge along the terrace's west edge: the hedge's top is a floor at 4.83 m with no box under it, its faces are rails to 4.63 m, and between the top's edge and the outer rail lies a strip 17 cm wide whose only floor is the terrace's own, 0.95 m lower — a body over it comes down into a trough under the hedge's top. **The hedges are solid** — 69 blocks, each from the thing's floor to its rim, a body's to stand on (top buffer 0.8, a floor on its top), and the 38 boxes of their shells given the same top buffer: 28 at street level (the four reported, the rows along z −36.5, z 28 and z −19), 26 on the two east terraces (the troughs along their edges), 15 on the west plateau (the planters, a dumpster, a crate). They were found by a scan of the whole map on the sim's own collision — every pocket a body set down in cannot walk out of, then a body let fall on a 0.15 m grid over each and round it: 84 places a body falls into and cannot walk out of — and filled by rule where a unit can get over the place in play (a standing place the spawns reach, near and high enough) and the block that comes out is hedge-sized (1.3 m high and 2.2 by 4.7 m at the most), in rounds until a round added nothing; four pieces by hand (two of the north trough, whose cross walls run on under the next building; the hedge that runs north at the terrace's north-east corner, which had no ground under it at all and is filled from the model's lower ground at −1.13 m; and its corner). Of the 84, 20 remain: 15 inside buildings' double walls, behind door leaves, on roofs and inside a truck, where no unit comes, and 5 shallow places a jump leaves. **Two floor seams at the east terrace's north edge** (x 49.7..62.2; z −16.09..−16.02 and −16.22..−16.17, 7 and 6 cm): the terrace's floors and the floors of the b1105fp fills did not meet, and a body whose centre stayed over a seam fell 5 m to the model's lower ground, inside the solid boxes. One floor over both. **The south-east terrace's lawn south of z 36** had two triangles of its ground missing (x 42.0..42.5 widening south to 39.0..42.5; x 50.0..50.5 widening to 46.5..50.5: the reading made stepped boxes of one triangle of each of two rectangles): a body walking south on the lawn fell 3.86 m to the arena's bare ground under the map and could not come back. 16 boxes of the terrace's own make, a floor on each. Town's data: 7,709 boxes (85 added; none moved or removed) and 8,335 surfaces (86 added); the nav file rebuilt (15,500 nodes). **In play** a hedge can now be stood on and, at street level (1.1 m), jumped onto and crossed; before, it was a wall no jump cleared. Checked on the sim: bodies let fall at the five places and over the strip beside the fifth — 5 of 6 could not walk 5 m off before (the four hedges at most 1.5 m, the trough 3.4 m), none now, each resting on the hedge's top. Bodies let fall every 2 cm across the terrace's north edge: 3,885 of 13,546 fell under the terrace before, none now. A body walked south at every 0.1 m of the south-east terrace, plain and jumping: it fell at 58 places before, at none now. The nav grid's nodes the spawns reach from which no route leads back: 2,039 before, 439 now (those behind ten links at the west plateau's north edge that no walk or jump follows: the plateau's floor runs on over the node each names). Bot duels, 24 of 120 s from the spawns with both kept alive: 4 of 48 bots spent time inside a trap before (358 s in all — the terrace's trough twice for 118 s, a street hedge for 61 s), none now; the longest stay in one place out of the other's sight went from 11 s to 3 s. Tests 212 pass. `client/public/maps/town_data.json`, the map's nav file, `client/src/main.js` (the tag).
