**The ally tactic: FORWARD and BACK OFF, called to the bot teammate from a button by the stick** (b1105gq and b1105gr, 2026-11-12, PT then 9.7 with build b1105gr, online and offline; user: "i want to make a in-game command that makes ally BOT moving forward/backward. The idea is a small button at the up left corner of the joystick. By tapping it, 2 pop up buttons will appear, one would say smth like 'forward' and the other would say smth like 'back off'. The goal is to ask ally BOT to stay close or away from enemies.", then: 'change the effect to "hide 10 sec" and "lockrange -30 for 10sec"', "Remove these command buttons in command mode", "If the screen is big, place it nearer to the joystick", and "when there is either command in effect, make the command button itself glowing as well") — a 30 px button over the stick's left edge opens two pills, FORWARD and BACK OFF; a pick is a call to the bot on the player's side and stands 10 s (`ALLY_TACTIC_MS`; 20 s since b1105io, 40 s since b1105jm, and no time limit since b1105jn, below). **FORWARD** moves the bot's range band — the distance it keeps to its target, centred on its lock range with edges ±7 — 30 nearer (`ALLY_TACTIC_PUSH`), taken off after the shotgun rule so the call always moves the unit in from where it stood. Only where the bot wants to stand moves: the unit's lock range itself, which the red lock reads, is not touched (the homing it used to feed is off: both of its rates are 0), so a pushed bot stands inside its lock. **BACK OFF** is command mode's Hide stance for those 10 s: out of every enemy's sight, firing back only at what it can see from where it stands. While a call stands the button itself is lit — yellow, with a pulsing halo — and so is the call's pill the next time the two are opened; a tap on the lit pill takes the call back at once, a tap on the other replaces it at once; the pills close on a pick, on a second tap of the button, or 3 s after they opened. The button shows in the shooter view of a 2v2 whose teammate is a bot (offline always; online a seat a bot plays — one that was a bot at the match's start, until b1105iq; at any moment since, below) and stands — not in 1v1, not beside a human teammate, not in Sudden Death (that brain takes no tactic), not on the Shooting Range, and not in command mode, where it hides with the stick. In spectator mode (the Practice toggle, an online spectator) it shows and does not answer. With the player's own unit down and the teammate fighting on, the calls still work. How: `shared/src/sim/tactic.js` keeps the call on the unit's command entry (`tactic` { kind, until }, never on the fighter, which is serialized into every snapshot); the brain (`nbrain.js`) reads it each tick, moves the band for a push and treats a hide as the stance without writing the entry's own hide flag, so a commander's or a reload's hide and the call never undo each other. The band is the one Pursue, Engage and the Maze's firing-position search already read, and a route under way is planned again the tick a call lands, is taken back or runs out. A call dies with the unit it was made to. Online the page sends `ally:tactic` { slot, kind | null } — what the player meant, so a call that ran out on the way is not set anew by its own take-back; the server takes it from a seated player who is not in command mode, for a seat of the same side that was a bot at the match's start and still is (any seat of the same side that a bot plays, since b1105iq, below), outside Sudden Death, two calls to one unit at least 500 ms apart (a take-back is never held up), and answers nothing: what stands reaches the side with its snapshots (`commands[slot].tactic`, the end in the server's clock), so the lit button and the lit pill are the server's word. Placement: everything is measured off the stick (left 28, bottom 28; 140 across, 120 on a screen up to 720 px wide), the button 18 px over the stick's box and the lower pill 4 px over its ring, the same on a 606 × 327 phone and a 1920 × 1080 screen; a screen too short for the two rows (under 322 px high, under 380 on a wide one) lays button, FORWARD and BACK OFF in one row over the stick. In 9.7 every file takes PT's own change to the line (a three-way merge with no conflict). Checked here. The shared tests: six new (`shared/test/tactic.test.js` — the call on the table; FORWARD on Plain Field held at 73 to 87 with the lock on and back at the band after; the take-back; BACK OFF on Streets hidden from both enemies to the call's end; a standing Hide order outliving a call; a Maze route on Compound planned again on a call and on its take-back) and the 200 there were, the brain's equivalence references among them, unchanged: 206 of 206. The built page, offline, headless at 13 frames a second, the two enemies' bodies held still by the check: the teammate's distance to the nearer one 101.7 to 109.0 before a FORWARD, 64.5 to 75.5 under it, 99.5 to 107.9 after it ran out; under a BACK OFF on Streets its hide scratch set on every second of the call and cleared after; the taps — open, a call, the lit pill on re-opening, the take-back, the other call over the standing one, the 3 s close; the button lit under FORWARD with the two closed and open, under BACK OFF from the next frame drawn, and not lit with no call, after the take-back and after a call ran out; shown and answering in 2v2 and Trio 2v2, shown and not answering in spectator mode, absent in 1v1, hidden in Sudden Death, in the command view (a standing call ending there) and with the teammate down; a Trio teammate that fell with a call standing came back with none. Online, a 2v2 Duel against a test copy of this tree's server with the two enemies pinned: the teammate (lock 200) 195.2 to 199.6 from its target before the call, 163.7 to 169.4 under it, 194.5 to 200.0 after; the button lit and the lit pill from the echo, 5,894 ms left; the take-back; BACK OFF with the hide scratch on all 34 samples; FORWARD over a standing BACK OFF; calls to an enemy seat, to the caller's own, of an unknown kind and with no seat left nothing standing, and of two calls 120 ms apart the second was not taken. The button's box against the fullscreen button, the chips, the BOOST sticker and the stick's ring at 702 × 373, 606 × 327, 780 × 360, 844 × 390, 955 × 488, 1280 × 720 and 1920 × 1080: nothing touches. No page errors. Not tried on a phone. `shared/src/sim/tactic.js` (new), `constants.js`, `command.js`, `nbrain.js`, `index.js`; `shared/test/tactic.test.js` (new); `server/src/index.js`; `client/src/main.js`, `style.css`, `online/connection.js`.

**The ending camera waits for a tap at its end** (b1105gy, 2026-11-12, PT then 9.7, online and offline; user: "if the player doesn't skip, make the screen stops at the end of winner camera. It won't move on to the team ending screen unless player taps again. If the player taps skips anytime before the end of winner camera, the screen skips to the team ending screen.") — the end page no longer comes by itself. When the last shot has run its length everything stays as it is — the camera, the unit on its motion's last frame, the call, the TAP TO SKIP hint — until a tap or a key press, which goes to the end page as a skip does (`finale.held`, `updateFinale`). A tap or a key press at any moment before, once the skip is armed (1 s, b1105gx), goes there too, as before. The last shot is the winner camera; where there is none (no shooter known; until b1105in, below, the final blow's unit down with a teammate standing too) the K.O. shot holds on the fallen unit the same way — this side's reading, the message naming the winner camera alone: one rule for every ending, and no end page arriving under a finger that meant to end the hold. Online the lobby moving on still ends the finale (a rematch begun, the room left), and the end page a held player comes to is drawn from the lobby's state of that moment; while a player holds, the end page is not up for them, so the other side's rematch request is not seen until they tap. The hint's words are unchanged. In 9.7 `main.js` takes PT's own change (a three-way merge with no conflict). **The game-start intro** (b1105gz, 2026-11-12, PT; 9.7 2026-11-13 with build b1105hg, online and offline; user: "I want to make a game start intro. … When the game starts, the camera zooms in to the slightly high angle, three-quarter view of team1, sticks for a very very short period of time, then does the same to team2, then the player sees the normal playing pov (either shooter or command). … Either 1V1 or 2V2, the camera should be able to include all members in the team. … a HUD "Get Ready" … a HUD "Start" … After this HUD disappear, the normal HUD finally appears. … Only the "Pause" HUD will be shown and functioning during these beginning cameras. … Beginning cameras will be unskipable, online and offline. … Remove the invincibility for all unit during their first spawn in the game", then "in sudden death mode, the SD banner shows up after the "START" disappear. Also, for "START" HUD, i want it has a cap-and-bottom-line feel and swipes out instead of simply disappear."; the looks picked from the design canvas's round 14: GET READY its band B, START its lettering A between two bars) — every match but the Shooting Range's opens on it. **The sequence**: team 1's shot (the camera pushes in on the team from its front three-quarter, a little above, 0.5 s, and stands 0.5 s), a cut, team 2's shot the same, a cut to the viewer's own view (the chase camera, the free pov, the command view, a spectator's), START over it for 0.8 s, then the match's HUD and the match: 2.8 s. Team 1 then team 2 on every screen (online the server's seats p1 and p3, then p2 and p4; offline the player's side is team 1); a cut between the teams, no flight; Trio shows the first units; every match and rematch. **Its lengths are three shared constants**, `INTRO_PUSH_MS`, `INTRO_STAND_MS`, `INTRO_START_MS` in `shared/src/sim/constants.js` — the one place to tune them (user: "you need to make fast response when we start tuning the timing duration later"): the client's sequence and the server's hold both read them. **GET READY** rides the two team shots as a band along the screen's foot: the team's sticker (TEAM 1 / TEAM 2), the words, and the team's units as chips — face, name and the side word the HP chips give them, in their colours (the seat's own online; no side word for a spectator); a band too long for the screen is set a size smaller, never a cut name. **START** is the ending camera's lettering between a bar over its caps and a bar under its foot; it pops in, stands, and leaves in a swipe over its last 0.26 s — the bars part, one to each side, and the word is wiped away between them. Both are moved by the intro's own clock (`updateIntro`), so a paused intro holds them still. **The match waits for it** ("START 顯示時角色能不能動？… 我建議比賽在 START 掃出、HUD 出現的那一刻才開始。" — "As you suggested."): through the whole of it nobody moves, fires, thinks or can be hit. Offline the sim is not run (`state.running` false; the frame loop's intro branch keeps the picture live) and the opening stamps — the guns' clocks, every bot's first round 650 ms on — are set again as it ends (`stampMatchOpen`). Online the server holds every match for the intro's length (`introUntil`, sent in `match:start` and every snapshot; `holdUntil` is that plus Sudden Death's banner), the clients mute their input frames as they did for the banner's hold, and each client fits the sequence to what is left of the server's time: on time it plays at its own pace; after a stall — its map still building, a long first frame — a little faster (1.6 times at most), and past that it is joined further on, so START leaves as the hold ends on every screen (THE CLOCK ONLINE; later snapshots can only tighten the estimate of that moment, never loosen it). A server that sends no `introUntil` — one older than this build — gets no intro and plays as before. In **Sudden Death** the banner now comes after START (its own 2.3 s, the HUD in behind it), then the match. **Only PAUSE**: the match's HUD — the chips, the stick, the buttons, the boost, the kill feed, the crosshair — the command layer, the fullscreen button, the bars over the heads, the lock, the arrows and the edge triangles stay away until START is gone (`html.intro-on`, style.css); PAUSE alone shows and works, offline, where there is one: its menu opens over the intro and the intro's clock stands while it is up; New Game from there ends the intro with its match. Online there is no PAUSE, so nothing but the band and START is on the screen. Nothing skips the intro. **No spawn protection at the start**: the white light and its three immune seconds are gone from every unit's first spawn — `createMatchState` (the shared sim), the server's Sudden Death stamp, `startMatch` and the banner's end here; a Trio roster unit coming on mid-match keeps its own (`respawnFighterNext`, `respawnSlotMech`). A first-life unit's `invulnerableUntil` is 0, which still tells the bots' reference a first life from a later one. With the match held through the intro nobody can be hit before it is released; after it, units in sight of each other can be hit from the first moment — on Plain Field the teams stand 48 apart in the open, and in Sudden Death there the Sudden Death brain, which used to hold its fire on an immune target for three seconds, now fires as soon as its own reaction allows. **A team's shot** (`introTeamShot`): the camera 16 out (11 for a lone unit) from the middle of the team along the line to the other team's middle turned 35° — or the first of a fan of angles from which every unit of the team is in sight (the ending cameras' own sight rule) and stands whole in the picture, its feet clear of the band; none such, the first that sees them all; then nearer —, 6.5 above the units' roots, the whole moved along the camera's right so the team stands right of the middle, as the canvas's frames had it. The push in is along the camera's own line from 1.5 times as far (nearer where that start is blocked; the lens alone where all are). Over the 19 maps, 2v2 and duel, every team's shot found an angle that sees its whole team. The units are turned to the other team's middle and held at ease. **What stands in the way**: play's own fade keeps EVERY unit of the team in view through the shot, and goes to its ends at once on a cut (`wallFadeSnap`) instead of easing; on Town, where play fades nothing, the ending cameras' see-through window opens over each unit of the team that something hides — the window is now TWO windows (a second set of uniforms; one each, not one wide one: each is cut before its own unit's upright plane and above its own floor), and the twins a shot will need are made as the shot is set, for hidden units alone (`seeWarmFor`), since the slow walk that makes them has not run yet in a match's first second. At Town's own spawns nothing hides a team from its shot, so the window stays shut there. Also: a rematch online no longer starts with the own unit's slide of a unit or so (the smoothing offset left from the last match's last correction is cleared with the rest of the prediction's bookkeeping; the intro's camera is on the unit from the first frame). Two tests follow the new opening: the sniper's charge test accepts the round landing in the tick it is fired (its target is no longer immune), and the Sudden Death brain's equivalence run on Plain Field sets the three immune seconds itself (without them the two 1 HP bots end the match in 23 ticks). Checked headless on the built PT. The shared suite: 206 of 206. Offline, the page's clock stepped 1/60 s for every frame drawn, on Plain Field: a 2v2 — team 1's shot from 0 to 1.0 s (the camera 8.2 units in over the first 0.5 s, then not a millimetre), team 2's from 1.0 to 2.0 s, START from 2.0 to 2.8 s with its swipe from 2.55 s, the sim running and the HUD up in the frame after 2.8 s; through all of it the HP chips, the stick and the fullscreen button hidden and PAUSE shown, no bar over a head, no lock, no arrow, no spawn light and `invulnerableUntil` 0 on every unit, every unit on its standing clip and turned exactly to the other team, nobody moved, both units of each team inside the frame, every bot's first round 650 ms after the release; a duel the same with one unit a shot; Sudden Death the same and then the banner, the match after it; command mode the same, START over the command view and its cards after; PAUSE tapped 0.6 s in: its menu up, the intro's clock still for the 1.5 s it was up, Resume, and the intro ran its remaining 2.2 s; New Game from that menu: the lobby, nothing of the intro left, and a new match's intro from its start; Trio as the 2v2; the Shooting Range no intro, running at once, no light. On all 19 maps, 2v2 and duel, each team's shot found an angle that sees its whole team (the first angle, or another of the fan where that one is blocked or would stand a unit under the band), and in the 2v2 pictures of all 19 every unit stands whole above the band. Town's window: with team 2 stood behind a hedge and the shot forced to its far side, both windows opened (two units, two cuts in the hedge, the pair of pictures with the window off showing them hidden to the waist), eleven twins made as the shot was set and no shader error; a finale stop with the gate off still opened its one window. Online against a bot, on a test copy of this tree's server, a forward key held from before the start: the unit did not move through the intro and moved once it was over, the HUD away meanwhile and back after, no light, the intro ending within a frame of the server's release (37 ms before it in the last run; a page that came to the match 0.2 s late played every stage in the time that was left, and one that stalled a second and a half on its first picture was joined on at team 2's shot); Sudden Death online: the banner for the two seconds of hold that remained, no movement under it; a rematch: the intro again, whole, and the unit still through it. Not tried: two human players at once, a phone's own timing, the free pov, a spectator, and the command view online. `shared/src/sim/constants.js`, `shared/src/sim/state.js`, `server/src/index.js`, `client/src/main.js`, `client/src/style.css`; tests `shared/test/sim.test.js`, `shared/test/sdbrain_equiv.test.js`.

**The map loaded before the intro's camera runs; the push in 1 s** (b1105ha, 2026-11-12, and b1105hb, 2026-11-13, PT; 9.7 2026-11-13 with build b1105hg; user: "can you make sure the map is loaded before the camera starts running? currently there are sometimes where player only sees units but no background map (still loading). I can live with longer stating time before game. We might add a loading screen in the future." and "The zoom in is too fast, make zoon in 1sec."; a third ask, START half a second longer, was withdrawn in the same breath: "oh wait don't change the START HUD", "just the first two") — **The map first.** A match used to be set up as soon as its map's data file was in, and the map's model — megabytes, fetched from the map's pick on — came into the scene whenever it landed: a quick GO, or All random's, opened the game-start intro on units standing in an empty scene. Now `startMatch` waits for the model too (`mapReadyFor`: a hand-made map is always ready; a modelled one once its data file is in and its model has loaded): it puts itself off and comes back when the model is here. Meanwhile the screen is covered — the online page's own "Match starting…" card on a solid ground (`matchStartCover`, `.online-overlay.solid`): the menus are gone by then and the scene behind holds the last match's leftovers; this is the place a loading screen would take, and since b1105hd THE LOADING SCREEN, below, has taken it — and the cover stays up until the match's first frame has been drawn, the frame that uploads the model, so nothing shows between the menu and the intro's first shot with its map. However long the model takes, the match waits: the start is later by the load's length and no more. A model that FAILS to load is not waited for (`loadMapModel` now tells of a failure): the match starts without it, as it always could, and the next arena build asks again; a data file that fails puts the lobby back (it used to leave an empty screen). A later start, or one that goes through, voids a start still waiting. Online the server's clock runs whatever a page has loaded, so there the intro is not begun, and the picture stays covered, until the map is here — then the intro is fitted to what is left of the server's time (THE CLOCK ONLINE: a little faster, or joined further on) — or until that time has run out, when play begins as it used to on whatever has loaded. The server does not wait for a page's map; making it wait would be a step of its own. **The push in is 1 s** (`INTRO_PUSH_MS` 1000, 500 until now): each team's shot is 1.5 s — the push, then the 0.5 s stand as before — and the intro 3.8 s (2.8 until now; in Sudden Death the banner's 2.3 s follows as before). The push's easing is unchanged (out: quick off the cut, slowing into the stand), START's 0.8 s and its swipe too. The server's hold follows the same constant. Checked headless on the built PT. Offline, the model of Town held back by the page's own server until 6 s after the match was asked for from the lobby: the cover — "Match starting…" on a solid ground — from the first frame to the model's arrival, no unit of the match in the scene meanwhile, the intro's first frame 6.6 s in with all 55 of the map's meshes in the scene and the cover gone in that same frame; not one frame with the intro running, or the match's units shown, before the model was in. The model's request made to fail instead (after 3 s): the cover off and the match started without the model, its intro and all, the next build asking once more and no loop. The lengths, the page's clock stepped 1/60 s a frame drawn, on Plain Field: a 2v2 — team 1's shot from 0 to 1.5 s (the camera 8.6 units in over the first 1.0 s, then still), team 2's from 1.5 to 3.0 s, START from 3.0 to 3.8 s with its swipe from 3.55 s, the sim and the HUD in the frame after 3.8 s, the HUD away but for PAUSE until then, no light, nobody moved; PAUSE 0.6 s in held the clock for the 1.5 s it was up; Sudden Death the same and then the banner. Online against a bot on a test copy of this tree's server, Town's model released 1.5 s after the start: the picture covered until the model was in (2.3 s on that page), the intro begun then with 1.4 s of the server's time left and joined at team 1's stand, ended 93 ms before the server's release, the unit not moved through it; no frame with the intro running before the map, none with a unit shown uncovered before it. The shared suite: 206 of 206. Not tried: a phone's own load and frame times, two human players at once, a data file that fails (the lobby put back), and a model that arrives after the server's intro time online. **A start that waits clears the menus itself** (b1105hb, 2026-11-13, found in the hand-over's own check): the lobby's GO!! and the unit pickers clear the page before they ask for the match, but the end page's REMATCH and the fallback map picker ask with their page still up, and `startMatch` cleared it only when it set the match up — a start that waited for its map left that page standing OVER the cover (a menu is the upper layer), its buttons live, for the whole wait: nothing seemed to happen, and Select Unit tapped meanwhile opened the lobby with the start still to come. The wait now clears the menus as it puts the cover up, as the set-up would a moment later. A REMATCH meets it only when its map's model is not in — a load that failed and was asked for again. Checked headless: the match asked for with the lobby still on the screen and Town's model held 4 s — no menu in any frame after, the cover from the first frame to the model's arrival, no unit of the match in the scene meanwhile, the intro's first frame with all 55 meshes; the 2v2's lengths unchanged (0 to 1.5 s, 1.5 to 3.0 s, START to 3.8 s). Not tried: the end page's own REMATCH with its model still on the way. `shared/src/sim/constants.js`, `client/src/main.js`, `client/src/style.css`.

**The intro's team shots show the units' faces** (b1105hc, 2026-11-13, PT; 9.7 the same day with build b1105hg; user, with a picture of Dust's 2v2 where team 1's shot looked at Haruka's back: "show the unit's face in the cameras"). Through the whole intro every unit stood turned to the middle of the other team, and a team's shot took the first angle of its fan from which the whole team is in sight and whole in the picture: 35 degrees off the team's line at best. That showed a face only when things went well. A spawn with a wall before it leaves the shot no angle but one beside or behind the team (Dust at the user's screen, Station on any), and the units then showed the camera a side or a back; and from the first angle too the further of a team's two units, 12 apart, saw the camera from 40 to 65 degrees round. Now a unit in its OWN team's shot turns toward where that shot's camera stands, the short way round, until the camera is within `INTRO.faceMax` of its heading (15 degrees), and no further; a unit the camera already sees from inside that keeps the other team's line exactly. Each unit turns by its own line to the camera, so a team's two units end turned in toward it together. The turn belongs to the shot: it is set on the cut's own frame (`introFaceNow`, from `introEnter`, before the see-through window reads where the bodies are) and held by the model's frame (`introFacing`, read in `updateUnitModelState`); in the other team's shot and under START every unit is back on the other team's line, and since the shots are cuts no unit is ever seen turning. The camera is not moved: the pick of its angle, its distance, the push in and the frame's fit are as they were, and the heading is the model's alone (the sim's own yaw is not touched), so the server knows nothing of it and online needs no new message. 15 and not more: the shot looks down on the units from a little above, and at 40, as first tried, a unit under a helmet or a cap still showed a profile and one eye. Checked headless on the built PT. Every map, 2v2 and duel, each team's shot held at its stand (19 maps, 38 matches, 76 shots, 114 units): before this build 2 of the units showed the camera their backs (over 90 degrees round: Station's 2v2), 20 stood side on (60 to 90) and 17 more between 40 and 60; now every one of the 114 is within 15 degrees of the camera, 95 of them turned for it (the largest turn 109 degrees), the other 19 on the other team's line untouched; in the other team's shot and under START not one unit is off the other team's line; every shot sees its whole team and holds it whole above the band, as it did. The user's own picture again, Dust's 2v2 with Minori and Haruka at their screen's shape: before, team 1's shot on Haruka's back and Minori's side, team 2's on two profiles; now all four faces. The limit itself, the same Dust shot at 40, 30, 20, 10 and 0 degrees: at 40 a unit under a helmet or a cap still showed a profile and one eye from the shot's height, from 20 down both eyes; a change of the number in the console reaches the units the next frame. The intro's lengths, the page's clock stepped 1/60 s a frame: a 2v2 as before (0 to 1.5 s, 1.5 to 3.0 s, START to 3.8 s, the sim and the HUD the frame after, nobody moved, every unit on its standing clip); Sudden Death the same and then its banner. Online against a bot on a test copy of this tree's server, a Duel on Plain Field: the unit in view turned 6.7 degrees in its own shot and stood on the other team's line in the other's and under START, the intro ended 39 ms before the server's release, the unit not moved through it. Town's windows, team 2 stood behind each of three low hedges in turn and the shot forced to the hedge's far side, this build and the one before side by side: no shader error in either, and a window opens in every case — but at the two hedges where the team now turns round to the camera it opens over one unit of the two, where the build before opened both (at the third, where the units keep the other team's line, one of two in both builds). The hedge reaches the units' knees; the gate asks only after bones 0.8 or more over the floor, one hidden bone opening the window, and a unit turned round carries the low bones of its back and hair to the side away from the hedge, clear of it. The gate's rule is as it was; nothing hides a team at Town's own spawns. Not tried: a phone, two human players at once, Trio, the command view, a spectator. `client/src/main.js`.

**The loading screen; everything a match needs loaded under it** (b1105hd, 2026-11-13, PT; 9.7 the same day with build b1105hg; user, of the design canvas's round 15: "Use option A and the shown moving path. But make the image hovers from right to left at first instead of left to right."; of a white rim about the pair on the dark ground: "Yes."; and: "While we make this loading screen, might as well use this time to load everything the game would need, the weapon image for killing HUD, BOT pathfinder etc."; then, shown that a long map name reaches under the pair's flight and asked which goes over which: "Use 字在Q版上面") — **Every start goes through it.** `startMatch` puts the screen up first and only then gathers the match and sets it up (`startMatchNow`, the old `startMatch`'s body); the match's first frame is drawn under the screen, and the screen comes off with that picture. Until this build a match waited for its map alone (b1105ha) under the online page's "Match starting…" card, and the set-up itself ran on the menu's last picture: the page stood frozen on the lobby for as long as the arena, the route grid and the first frame's upload took. **What is waited for** (`matchLoadBegin`; `matchNeeds`, `matchNeedsOnline`): the map's data file, its model, and its route grid's file — the bots' pathfinder: with the file in hand the grid is read at the set-up, under the screen, where a young fetch used to leave it to the frame the bots first thought in; every unit the match fields, a Trio roster whole — its model, the clip files it borrows (the climb, a dodge, a jump), the kill feed's pictures of it (its weapon's sticker and its face, drawn ahead: `warmKillArt` returns a promise now) and the portrait of the bar over its head; and the frame that puts all of it on the graphics card — the match's first frame is drawn once with nothing culled (`matchWarmDraw`), so what stands outside the first shot is uploaded too and the intro's later cuts find it there. Until this build the units' models, their clips, the grid's file and the kill feed's pictures were asked for while the match was already on the screen: the intro could open on a unit's stand-in card, and the kill feed's pictures were drawn in the frame the HUD came in, the frame play began. A file that fails is not waited for twice: the match starts without it, as it always could (`loadUnitModel` tells of a failure now, as `loadMapModel` has since b1105ha); the map's data file alone is needed, and its failure puts the lobby back as before. **The screen** (`showLoadingScreen`; `style.css` `.ld`): round 15's sample A — the ink ground with its three blobs, the MAP sticker and the map's name in the ending camera's lettering, NOW LOADING and a thin bar along the foot — and the pair cut out of the user's picture, Aris holding Kei (`client/public/ui/loading_pair.png`, 134 KB, the white rim baked in; asked for as the page boots), flying a figure of eight over the rest of the screen, 8 s a round, three squares of the halo's mint after them. The path is the sample's, begun at its right end: the pair flies from the right to the left first. The name, the words and the bar are the layer above the pair: any name longer than TOWN reaches under the flight's lower left turn, and there the pair passes behind the letters (the user's pick of the two orders), the name always whole. A long name is set smaller, to six tenths of the screen's width (SHOOTING RANGE 55 px tall where TOWN is 84, on an 844 x 390 screen); a screen held upright sizes the whole by its width. Every motion is a CSS animation of transform or opacity, so it does not wait on the page's own work. The bar is the files' own progress — each thing a weight (`LOADING.weights`), a model's share by its bytes where the browser tells the file's size — standing at nine tenths when every file is in and running on through the set-up. The ground is up on the frame after the tap; the content comes in 150 ms later (`LOADING.showAfterMs`) and, once it has begun to show, stays 450 ms at the least (`minMs`): a start with everything in hand on a quick machine is a cut through ink, and no start shows the screen for a blink. The screen takes every tap, PAUSE's menu does not open under it, and the fullscreen button is hidden while it is up. **The match under it.** Offline the intro enters its first shot — the camera placed, the picture drawn under the screen — and its clock stands until the screen is off (`updateIntro`), so the intro is seen from its first moment and is as long as it was. While files are on their way nothing is drawn under the screen. The lobby (`showSelectMenu`), or a later start, voids a start still waiting; a start asked for with a match still running stops that match at once. **Online** (`runOnlineMatchFrame`) the server's clock runs whatever this page has loaded: the screen stays up, and the intro is not begun, until the same things are in — then the intro is fitted to what is left of the server's time, as since b1105ha — or until that time has run out, when play begins on whatever has loaded. No message is added and the server is untouched. Known costs: a start is later by its loads' length, the units' now as the map's since b1105ha; a start with everything in hand whose set-up takes longer than 150 ms shows the screen for 0.6 s at the least, where it used to freeze on the menu for the set-up's own length; the one frame drawn with nothing culled is longer than a plain first frame; online, a page whose units arrive after the server's 3.8 s has seen the screen in the intro's place, where it used to see the intro with stand-in cards. Checked headless on the PT site's build at b1105hd (a software renderer: its frame times are not a device's, the order of things is). Town 2v2 from a lobby that had loaded nothing of it, every file 400 ms late: the screen from the frame after the ask (17 ms) to the match's first drawn frame; under it 17 requests — the map's three files, four models, the three clip files, four weapon pictures, three portraits (the fourth was the lobby's) — and none after it; at the first frame without it the intro at its first moment (0 ms), 4 of 4 units on their models, the map's 55 meshes, the grid read from its file (50 ms), the four units' kill feed pictures drawn; then the intro's stages by the clock, 0 to 1.5 s, 1.5 to 3.0 s, START to 3.8 s. The build before, the same way: the intro's first frame with 0 of 4 models and 16 of its frames before all four stood, and 8 of its 16 requests after the cover was off — two portraits, two clip files, and the four weapon pictures 7.1 s in, as the HUD came. The same start again, everything cached: no request, the screen for the set-up's length. PAUSE's key and button under the screen: no menu. A unit's model failing, the map's model failing: the match starts without it; the data file failing: the lobby back. Trio 1v1 with two rosters of three: six models and six units' pictures under the screen, 24 requests, none after. The Shooting Range (no intro): the screen off with the first frame. Streets, a hand-made map: as Town, less the map's files. Online against a bot on a test copy of the server: Town with its model held 1.5 s — the screen from 0.17 to 1.69 s, the intro begun with 1.86 s of the server's time left, its three stages, START gone 0.1 s before the release; Plain Field with both units' model held 1.5 s — the screen to 1.68 s, the intro begun with 1.99 s left, no frame of it without the models. The pair's place sampled over 9 s on an 844 x 390 screen: from the right end (its middle at x 765) to the left end (x 374) in the first 4.5 s, down then up on the way, and back the same way mirrored — the sample's figure of eight. Looked at on 844 x 390, 667 x 375, 1024 x 768, 1600 x 900 and 390 x 844, with six map names. Not tried: a phone or any real graphics card (whether the pair keeps flying through the set-up's own frames there), two human players online, a spectator, the command view. `client/src/main.js`, `client/src/style.css`, `client/public/ui/loading_pair.png`.

**The winner camera: the unit at ease after its motion** (b1105he, 2026-11-13, PT; 9.7 the same day with build b1105hg; user: "In the winner camera, after the reload motion is completed, loop the idle motion every unit uses for their stand motions. I will provide special motion for ARIS/HARUKA/HOSHINO to use in this situation, but for now use their standing motion as well.") — The final blow's unit played its victory motion, its reload clip, once and then stood frozen on that motion's last frame: through the closing hold and, since the frame stays up until a tap (b1105gy), for as long as the player looked. Now, the pass over, the unit goes back to its STAND clip, looping, until the tap (`finalePoseFor`): at ease as it stood through the opening hold, and as every other living unit stands through the finale. The change of clip is the one play makes whenever a reload ends: the model's own 0.12 s blend, a cut for the model that cuts (Hoshino). The pass is over when the rig's own clip says so — started by this finale and stopped on its last frame — not by the clock: on a device slower than 30 frames a second the clips run behind the clock (the frame loop's step is capped at 1/30 s), and the motion still plays whole. Every unit by the one rule; Aris, Haruka and Hoshino stand on their stand clip too until their own motion for this moment comes. Nothing of the timing moves: the opening hold (0.1 s), the motion at its own speed, shot 2's length, the moment the skip is armed; a unit that lies fallen through shot 2 (EVERY UNIT DOWN) lies still, and shot 1 is untouched. The pose is this page's alone: online needs no new message, and the server is untouched. Checked headless on the PT site's build at b1105he on a 60 fps clock, 8 s of shot 2 with nobody tapping: Saori — at ease 0.12 s, the reload once (1.80 s, to its last frame), then the stand clip from 1.92 s, its time running at its own speed and going round (three times in 6.1 s; a 1.6 s clip), the blend over in 7 frames; Haruka — once (2.00 s), then the stand clip from 2.12 s; Aris — once (2.20 s), then from 2.32 s; Hoshino — once (1.53 s), then the stand clip at once, no blend; in each the finale still up and held, the call and the hint in place, and one tap brings the end page. On a 10 fps clock (Saori, Haruka): the motion to its last frame before the stand clip begins, 5.5 and 6.1 s after the camera arrived. Iori on the final build: once (2.50 s), then the stand clip from 2.62 s. Online against a bot on a test copy of the server (Saori): the stand clip already going round as the hold begins (0.62 s into it, 2.52 s after the camera arrived) and 3 s on (the clip's time 3 s further round its 1.6 s), the camera unmoved, the call and the hint in place, no end page; one tap, the end page. Not tried: a phone, the roster unit standing in (WINNER), the command view. `client/src/main.js`.

**A map's objects found again where pieces were set beside its root: Neon's and District's things fade one at a time** (b1105hf, 2026-11-13, PT; 9.7 the same day with build b1105hg; user, at Neon's x -21.19 z 55.74 with the paved walkway outlined in a screenshot: "the walkway in Neon should not go transparent.") — On a map that fades by object (`fadeNodes`: Neon, District, Warehouse, Compound) a thing is one of the model's own objects, and it alone goes translucent while it hides the player's unit from the camera. An object was found as "the highest ancestor of the mesh that has siblings": right while a model had one top node, wrapped in its chain of single-child nodes. Then pieces were set BESIDE that top node — Neon's three bridge partitions, 18 meshes (b1105fq, 2026-11-10), District's two door frames, 14 — and the model's root had siblings: the root itself became the one object, and the whole map a single thing. From then on, whenever any face of the map stood between the camera and the player, EVERYTHING faded together to a tenth: every building, and with them the raised pavements of the ground's own tiles (the faces over 0.1 m; the road, lower, stayed) — the walkway going transparent under the player's feet. Now an object is found inside each of the scene's top nodes on its own (`splitFadeParts` `objOf`): down from the top node through the single-child wrappers, the first node with several children is the one whose children are the objects; a top node that never branches, a piece set beside the model, is an object itself. Over the served models' own node trees the old rule made Neon 19 objects, one of them 271 of its 289 meshes, and District 15, one of them 1366 of 1380; the new rule makes them 289 and 1380, one mesh each. The seven other modelled maps have a single top node and group exactly as before (Warehouse 45 objects, Compound 116, Spacecraft 92, Town 2, Dust 9, Hospital 4, the Backrooms 6). In a match: Neon registered 278 fade meshes in 16 groups, one group holding 263 of them, and now registers 248 in 247 groups (the largest 2); District 695 in ONE group, now 633 in 348 (the largest 12); Warehouse (66 in 42) and Compound (108 in 108) are unchanged. Neon's ground tiles are no things at all again (0.32 m at their highest): they are not registered and never fade. Neon's `fadeForce` objects — the scaffold decks, the catwalk's grating — are found by their names again, and fade alone as b1105bk meant. District's command view fades nothing either way (`cmdNoFade`, b1105dy). Checked headless on the PT site's build at the user's spot on Neon, free pov, the camera turned round the compass in 15 degree steps: at the headings where the building south of the walkway stands between the camera and the player (315 to 75 degrees) the build before it faded 263 meshes of 262 objects, the four ground tiles about the spot among them; b1105hf fades 2 — that building and the object against it — and the walkway stays solid; at the other headings neither build fades anything. Not tried: a District spot with a building in the camera's way (two picked by guesswork had none), the command view, online. `client/src/main.js`. This tree carried the old rule and the same two models until build b1105hg.

**The winner camera: Aris and Hoshino hold their battle stance after the motion** (b1105hg, 2026-11-13, PT then 9.7; user, with the two units' model files: "these are the files to use to update the winner camera ending loop. Only Aris and Hoshino need update. Use "01_Normal_Idle" from Hoshino glb and "Normal_Idle" from Aris glb as their ending loops after the reload motion in winner camera.") — The motion b1105he said was to come, for two of its three units. A model may name the loop its unit holds once its victory motion is over (`UNIT_MODELS` `victoryIdle`): Aris's is `Normal_Idle` and Hoshino's `01_Normal_Idle`, each at its own speed. `finalePoseFor` asks for it for the final blow's unit in shot 2, from the end of the motion's one pass for as long as the frame stays up; a model that names none is given its stand clip there, as at b1105he (Haruka, whose stand clip is her Normal_Idle already, and every other unit). Only that unit and only then: through the take and the opening hold it stands on its stand clip as before, and a teammate in the frame, Aris or Hoshino too, keeps the stand clip. **No file is added or changed**: the two files sent are, byte for byte, the models both trees already serve (`client/public/models/aris.glb`, 10,569,384 bytes; `hoshino.glb`, 12,494,116), and the clips were in them — Aris's ground walk holds the same clip at half speed, one mixer action under two keys. **What changes to the eye**: these two stand on Formation_Idle, which lowers the weapon, so the unit left its firing stance the moment its reload ended; now it stays in it, the weapon up. Hoshino's model cuts between clips (`fade: 0`): the cut to Formation_Idle turned her right hand 99 degrees and moved her gun 0.21 of the model's units in one frame; the cut to 01_Normal_Idle turns no part more than 5 degrees (a skirt bone) and moves none more than 0.008. Aris's model blends over 7 frames: the largest turn of a part in one frame of the blend was 11 degrees (her right hand), and is 0.5. The loops' seams, each time round: 0.2 degrees at the most for Hoshino (2.0 s a round), 1.2 on Aris's necktie (1.73 s a round). Nothing of the timing moves: the opening hold, the motion at its own speed, shot 2's length, the moment the skip is armed, THE HOLD AT THE END. Checked headless on the PT site's build at b1105hg, offline Duel on Plain Field, a 60 fps clock, 8 s of shot 2 with nobody tapping: Hoshino — at ease 0.1 s, her reload once to its last frame (1.53 s), then 01_Normal_Idle from 1.63 s, three times round in the 6.4 s watched; Aris — at ease 0.1 s, her reload once (2.2 s), then Normal_Idle from 2.3 s, three times round in 5.7 s; both with the finale up and held (from 2.25 s and 2.92 s), then one tap and the end page. Saori and Haruka go back to their stand clip, as before. On a 10 fps clock (the clips run behind it, the frame's step being capped) the reload still finishes before the loop begins: Hoshino's at 4.6 s, Aris's at 6.6 s. In a 2v2 with Hoshino's the final blow and Aris beside her, Hoshino stands, reloads once and loops 01_Normal_Idle from 1.65 s while Aris stays on her stand clip through all 300 frames watched; with Aris's the final blow she loops Normal_Idle from 2.32 s and her teammate stands. Online, on the 9.7 build of the same lines against a test copy of its server, Hoshino the host against a bot: as the hold begins (2.25 s after the camera arrived) she is on 01_Normal_Idle, 0.63 s into its 2 s, and 3 s later 1.63 s into it — three seconds further round — the call and the hint in place and no end page; one tap, the end page. Not tried: a phone, the roster unit standing in (WINNER), the command view. `client/src/main.js`.

**The game-start intro, the loading screen, the winner camera's idle and the fade by object, here from PT** (9.7 build b1105hg, 2026-11-13; user: "After this. Update all new added/fixed features to 9.7.") — The eight builds above (b1105gz to b1105hg) reached this tree in one step. Seven files were merged three ways against PT at b1105gy — `client/src/main.js`, `client/src/style.css`, `server/src/index.js`, `shared/src/sim/constants.js`, `shared/src/sim/state.js`, `shared/test/sim.test.js`, `shared/test/sdbrain_equiv.test.js` — and one was added, `client/public/ui/loading_pair.png`; the server and the built style sheet come out equal to PT's. Five places in `main.js` were settled by hand, each for what this tree does not carry: the PT site's two imports and its photo mode (its block, its branch in the frame loop, its test in the draw) are left out, and this tree's own note on the finale stays beside PT's two new ones. One name was rewritten: the loading screen asks whether a unit is drawn as a 3D character by this tree's own test (PT's `unitHasModel` belongs to a change this tree does not have). The models are asked for as `.glb`, as before. **One thing differs from PT under the loading screen**: this tree still asks for the 2D pose art of a unit that has a 3D character (PT does not), where the unit is built; the screen does not wait for those pictures — the model is up, they are never shown — so a start may send requests for them after the screen is off (8 in the check below, two units' four poses, Iori's four answered 404 as before this build). Checked headless on this tree's build (a software renderer: the order of things, not a device's times). Offline, Town 2v2 from a lobby that had loaded nothing of it, every file 400 ms late: the loading screen from the frame after the ask to the match's first drawn frame, no menu under it; at the first frame without it the intro at its first moment (0 ms), 4 of 4 units on their models, the map's 55 meshes, the route grid read from its file, the four units' kill feed pictures; the intro's stages by the clock, 0 to 1.5 s, 1.5 to 3.0 s, START to 3.7 s; the same start again, everything cached: nothing asked for but Iori's four missing poses, and nothing after the screen. Sudden Death on Plain Field, Kei against Aru: the screen, the intro whole, nothing asked for after the screen. The winner camera on a 60 fps clock, 7 s of shot 2 with nobody tapping: Hoshino stands 0.1 s, reloads once (1.53 s) and loops 01_Normal_Idle from 1.65 s; Aris reloads once (2.2 s) and loops Normal_Idle from 2.32 s; Saori goes back to her stand clip; in a 2v2 Hoshino's final blow loops while Aris beside her keeps the stand clip; each held, then one tap and the end page. Neon at the reported spot (x -21.19, z 55.74), free pov, the camera turned round the compass in 15 degree steps: 2 things faded at the most — the building south of the walkway and the object against it — at the headings where the building stands in the way, none from 150 to 300 degrees, never the map; the fade things a match registers — Neon 248 in 247 groups, District 633 in 348, Warehouse 66 in 42, Compound 108 in 108 — equal PT's. Online against a bot on a test copy of this tree's server: Town with its model held 1.5 s — the screen from 0.30 to 1.71 s, the intro begun with 1.86 s of the server's time left, its three stages and START, the unit still through the hold and free after it; Sudden Death the same way — the intro begun with 1.89 s left, then the banner for 1.6 s with the unit still, then play; Hoshino's final blow — on 01_Normal_Idle as the hold begins and three seconds further round it 3 s later, no end page until the one tap. No page errors in any run. The suite: 206 tests pass. Not tried: a phone, two human players at once, a spectator, the command view, Trio. `client/src/main.js`, `client/src/style.css`, `client/public/ui/loading_pair.png`, `server/src/index.js`, `shared/src/sim/constants.js`, `shared/src/sim/state.js`, `shared/test/sim.test.js`, `shared/test/sdbrain_equiv.test.js`.

**The loading wait: online, a match's clock starts when every player has loaded** (b1105hh, 2026-11-13, PT and 9.7, online; user, asked whether the online server should wait for every player's files before it starts its clock: "Yes."; of how long at the most: "up to 30 sec (player would standby in loading screen, correct?)"; of where: "PT and 9.7") — Until this build the server began a match's hold — the game-start intro — the moment the host started the match, whatever the players' pages had loaded. A page still loading saw its loading screen in the intro's place, and one that needed longer than the hold came into a match already under way: its unit stood on its spawn to be shot at, and its page went on fetching and decoding files through its first seconds of play. **Now the hold begins when every player's page has said its match's things are in hand, and 30 s after the start at the latest** (`constants.js` `LOAD_WAIT_MAX_MS`, the one place to tune it). Until then every page stays on its loading screen — the pages that are done too — and no intro begins; then every screen plays the whole intro together and the match starts for all at once. **The rule** is `shared/src/sim/loadwait.js`, and the server keeps one wait a match (`index.js` `startMatchFor`). Waited for: the seats whose pages said, as they connected, that they report their loading (the handshake's `auth.loadWait`, `connection.js`). Not waited for: a bot's seat, a spectator, and a page older than this build — it would never report, and every match with it in the room would wait the whole 30 s. A page says `match:loaded` once a match (`main.js` `runOnlineMatchFrame`, `connection.js` `sendLoaded`) when every file is in or has failed and the match's picture has been drawn once under the screen with nothing culled, so its upload to the graphics card is done before it reports and not in the intro's first frame; the word carries the match's start time, so one about the match before cannot count for a rematch. A player who leaves is no longer waited for. When the wait is over (`tickLobby`, `releaseLoadWait`) the server stamps the hold from that moment — `introUntil`, `holdUntil`, every bot's first round 650 ms past it — and the pages read it off their next snapshot; `loadUntil`, new in `match:start` and in every snapshot, is the wait's latest end while it runs and 0 after. With the time run out the match starts on whatever each page has, as every match did until this build. **An older build on either side keeps working**: a page of this build against an older server hears no `loadUntil`, says nothing and plays as before; an older page against this server is not waited for, and while others still load it stands on its intro's first frame until the clock starts. Known costs: every online start is later by a round trip to the server and the one frame drawn before the report; a match starts as late as its slowest player, 30 s at the most — a player whose tab is in the background at the start loads nothing and holds the room for all of it; the screen's words stay NOW LOADING while a page that is done waits for the others. Checked headless on the PT site's build, two pages in one room on a test copy of the server, each page's files held back by its own file server (a software renderer on one machine: no network delay, and the order of things rather than a device's times). The second player's unit models 6 s late: the first page had its files at 2.0 s and stood by on its loading screen for 4.5 s; the server's wait was over 6.45 s after the start; both intros began 4 ms apart, each at its first moment with 3.78 s of the hold left, and ended 32 ms apart; neither unit moved while held (a forward key down throughout) and both ran after it. The wait's time running out (a test server set to 5 s, the second page needing 12): over at 5.01 s, the first page's intro whole, the second on its loading screen until the hold ran out and then in the match without an intro, as before this build. A 2v2 with the second player gone 2.5 s into the wait: over as soon as the first page had loaded (4.3 s), its intro whole, the leaver's unit a bot. An older page in the room, slow: the server waited for the other seat alone (2.9 s) and that page's intro was whole; the older page came in as it did before. An older page in the room, fast, beside a page of this build 6 s late: the older page stood on its intro's first frame for 5.6 s and the two intros ended 52 ms apart. One page against a bot, Town's model held 1.5 s: the intro from its first moment with 3.76 s left, where the build before joined it 0.43 s in with 1.86 s left; Sudden Death the same, the banner after it for 2.1 s, the unit still. A page of this build against the server of the build before: as before, the intro joined 0.35 s in with 1.88 s left, nothing waited for. The ending camera online: unchanged. No page errors in any run. Tests: `shared/test/loadwait.test.js`, six; the suite, 212, passes. On this tree's build and its own server, the two main checks again: two pages in one room with the second's unit models 6 s late — the first stood by on its loading screen for 4.4 s, the wait was over after 6.38 s, both intros began 21 ms apart, each at its first moment with 3.78 s left; one page against a bot with Town's model held 1.5 s — the intro whole, 3.76 s left. The suite here, 212, passes. Not tried: a phone, a real network's delay, more than two human players, a spectator, the command view, Trio. `shared/src/sim/loadwait.js`, `shared/src/sim/constants.js`, `server/src/index.js`, `client/src/online/connection.js`, `client/src/main.js`; test `shared/test/loadwait.test.js`.

**The winner camera: Kurumi holds her battle stance after the motion** (b1105hi, 2026-11-13, PT and 9.7; user, with her model file: "Use motion "Normal01_Idle" as the looping motion after the winner reload motion for Kurumi."; "PT and 9.7") — THE ENDING LOOP (b1105hg) for a third unit. Kurumi's model names it (`UNIT_MODELS` `victoryIdle`: `Normal01_Idle`, at its own speed), and once her reload motion's one pass is over in the winner camera she loops it — her shield up before her — where she went back to her stand clip, Formation_Idle, and stood the shield at her side with her gun lowered. Normal01_Idle is her first motion set's battle stance, and her reload is that set's (Normal01_Reload) and ends in it. Nothing else moves: the loop is for the final blow's unit alone and only after the motion; through the take and the opening hold she stands on her stand clip; a teammate in the frame keeps the stand clip; the timing is as it was. **No file is added or changed**: the file sent is, byte for byte, the model both trees already serve (`client/public/models/kurumi`, 8,513,620 bytes), and the clip was in it. Her model blends between clips (7 frames): the largest turn of a part in one frame of the blend was 13 degrees (her weapon, which also moved 0.04 of the model's units a frame) and is 1 degree (a skirt bone). The loop is 2.0 s a round; its seam turns no part more than 0.5 degrees. Checked headless on the PT site's build at b1105hi, offline Duel on Plain Field, a 60 fps clock, 8 s of shot 2 with nobody tapping: Kurumi — at ease 0.1 s, her reload once to its last frame (2.8 s), then Normal01_Idle from 2.9 s, twice round in the 5.1 s watched, the finale up and held from 3.52 s, then one tap and the end page. Saori goes back to her stand clip and Hoshino to her own loop, as before. On a 10 fps clock her reload still finishes (8.4 s) before the loop begins. In a 2v2 with Kurumi's the final blow and Aris beside her, Kurumi loops from 2.92 s and Aris stays on her stand clip through all 300 frames watched. On this tree's build: offline, Kurumi reloads once (2.8 s) and loops Normal01_Idle from 2.92 s, the finale held from 3.52 s, one tap and the end page; online against a bot on a test copy of this tree's server, Kurumi the host — on Normal01_Idle as the hold begins, 0.62 s into its 2 s, and three seconds further round it 3 s later, no end page until the one tap. **The Guide: the point of view switch and the other basics; this README's stale lines** (b1105hj, 2026-11-13, PT and 9.7; user, after a check of this README against the code: "update readme. Also update the in game Guide for basic features (pov switch etc.). But keep the guide very easy and concise to read.") — **The Guide** (the main menu's `?`, `showGuidePopup`) gains eight lines and two headings — 40 lines under eight headings where it had 32 under six. *Point of view*, after Controls: switch any time by two quick taps on the empty screen or `I`; Angled, the shots aim at the locked enemy by themselves; Free, the player aims the crosshair and the shots go where it points — a swipe on the empty screen or a drag on the fire button, on a PC the mouse and its left button. *On some maps*, after Fighting: a ladder (jump at it to get on, up or down, jump again to let go) and a transporter (stand in the glowing circle, hold jump a second). Under *Fighting*: the triangle on the screen's edge, and the ally tactic — the small arrows button at the top of the screen (it stands by the fullscreen button or, on a small wide screen, right of PAUSE), FORWARD or BACK OFF, 10 seconds (20 since b1105io, 40 since b1105jm, and no length since b1105jn, below). Under *How to win*: the ending camera, and that a tap moves on. The PC keys' line calls `I` the point of view (the free camera before). One line was wrong and is put right: a Trio's next unit "starts at the spawn point" — ten maps pick the place — reads "comes in". No other line of the Guide is touched and no style: the card, its headings and its two highlighted lines are as they were. **This README**, the places the check found: under Modes, where a Trio's next unit stands, and a new paragraph, *A match's course* (the loading screen, the intro, the ending cameras and their tap); under Offline, the Guide's line — Server Boot-up left the main menu at b1105o; under Sudden Death, the sniper rifles — three, Hiyori among them, left out of the Random cards and the online bots' defaults alone, where the line had two out of the pickers; the Controls table — the arrow keys, `Shift`, `Q`, `E`, `I` and `V`, the phone's pov switch and the ally tactic, the transporter's hold, and a row for the ending cameras; four bullets under HUD & unit displays — the kill feed, the intro, the ending cameras, the loading screen; under Maps, the ten maps that name spawn places; and the Pre-game loading note under Implementation notes. Each was read against the code as it stands (`main.js`'s key handler and pickers, `server/src/index.js` `sdUnitKey`, `arena.js` `ARENA_SPAWNS`, `spawn.js`, `pads.js`, `constants.js`), not against the older entries. Checked headless on this tree's built page at b1105hj: the Guide opened from the main menu on an 844 x 390 screen and a 1280 x 720 one — the card whole inside the screen, scrolling down and not sideways, the new headings and lines in their places, the close button closing it, no page error; the whole card photographed at 560 px wide. The suite here, 212, passes. Not tried: a phone. `client/src/main.js`; this file.

**The game's sounds and its music** (b1105hk, 2026-11-13, PT and 9.7; user, after the series of recordings set by ear on the listening site: "Implement in PT and 9.7"; then "the end game bgm and the main menu bgm need the soft switch too" and, of the game-start intro, "play the killing HUD effect at each team's beginning ... and when the "START" HUD shows up, play the previously removed "head shot killing HUD"") — The game had no sound; now every sound settled on the listening site plays at the level it has there and the ten tracks of music play by place: the chapter *Sounds & music* says what plays when. New: `client/src/sfx.js` (the engine and the music player), `client/src/sfx_data.js` (the table: 51 sounds in 40 files and 10 tracks, with their levels, written by the site's tool), `client/public/sfx` (842 kB) and `client/public/bgm` (30.2 MB, the tracks as uploaded). `main.js` gains the hooks: each unit's sounds on its row as `sfx`; a pull at `spawnProjectiles`, at the own predicted round online and at the server's `fired` reports; a reload's start in `tickAmmo` and off the mirrored state online; what a unit's state tells read every frame (`updateUnitSfx`: a charge's start and end, the full beam, the round's wait, a unit down); the player's own steps, dodge, jump and landing (`updateMoveSfx`, with `strideFootfalls` reading the model's stride); the hit hint and the being-hit sound where the HIT X is noted; the kill sound with the kill feed's entries; the intro's sounds; one listener for every button's sound and the Music button; the music's place said where each page and each part of a match begins. `connection.js` queues the `fired` events as it does the hits; `style.css` has the Guide's Music button. The server and the shared rules are as they were. Decisions taken here and open to change: the movement sounds are the player's own unit's alone, in the shooter's view; a gun is heard at one level wherever its unit stands; Aris, hovering, has no steps; the steps are evenly spaced on each unit's stride and do not follow an uneven pair of feet exactly. Checked headless on this line's build: the first press opens the audio with all 51 sounds decoded and a menu track playing round its own first and last sound, every file fetched under its own extension with no 404; buttons, cards and small controls each sound their own; the character menu and back cross-fade; the Guide's Music button turns the music off and on and the choice is kept; a duel on Plain Field: the loading screen keeps the menu's music with the battle's track ready, the track cuts in as the screen lifts with the intro's three sounds 1.5 s apart, a jump and its landing, a dodge, 30 own shots with the reload sound 0.5 s into the reload, the enemy's shots and rounds, the hit hint and the being-hit sound, no kill sound for the knockout that ended the match, the ending's track with the ending camera and a cross-fade back to a menu track; no page error. Online, two pages in one room on a test copy of this line's server (removed after): Saori's 74 shots heard on her own page at the trigger and 74 on the other about 50 ms after, Hiyori's 4 and 4 with her bolt after the three that left a round, each side's reload once on both pages, the scope on her own page only, the music of each place through the menus, no kill sound for the staged knockout that ended the match and the ending's track on both pages. The steps' timing, each unit's guns in play, the 2v2 knockouts and the Pause menu were checked on the PT build of the same code (its README has the figures). `npm test`: 212 of 212. Not tried: a phone; nothing was judged by ear here. `client/src/sfx.js`, `client/src/sfx_data.js`, `client/src/main.js`, `client/src/online/connection.js`, `client/src/style.css`, `client/public/sfx`, `client/public/bgm`.

**A unit's bolt, reload and scope are heard by its own player alone** (b1105hl, 2026-11-14, PT and 9.7; user: "only the user unit itself should hear its own bolt sound/reload/scope sound; other unit would only hear gunshot sound from others.") — Since b1105hk every unit sounded every sound of its gun for everyone. Now a unit that is not the player's own is heard by its shots and its beams and by nothing else: no bolt (Hiyori's, Iori's), no reload (a magazine's, a machine gun's three passes, Kei's recharge), no round going in (Hoshino, Haruka, Aris) and no charge of Kei's; the scope was the player's alone already. One test says whose unit it is (`sfxOwn` in `main.js`): the unit this player plays. A teammate's and an enemy's are not it; a commander's own unit is; a viewer who plays no unit, in either spectator view, has none, and hears shots and beams only. The movement sounds, the player's own unit's from the start, ask the same test now: a spectator heard the watched match's first seat walk, whoever was watched, and no longer does. What the player's own unit sounds is as it was, and so are the hit hint, the being-hit sound, the kill sound and the music; the server and the shared rules are not touched. Checked headless on the PT build. Eight offline duels against a fighting bot, the two units of each with no gun sound in common so that every sound says whose it is, both kept on their feet with magazines of three rounds for 18 s: the bot's shots and beams sounded every time (8 to 48 a duel), and of its 27 reloads, its 30 rounds going in, its 26 charges and its bolts not one sounded; the player's own unit sounded all of its own (Iori's 14 bolts and 4 reloads, Hiyori's 6 scopes, 4 bolts and 2 reloads, Hina's 4 reloads of three passes, Kei's 5 charges and her recharge, Hoshino's and Aris's 17 rounds each). The same probe on b1105hk heard the bot's bolt 7 times, its reloads 6, Kei's charge 10 and the round going in 20 in three of those duels, so it does tell the two builds apart. The spectator's view, two duels: shots alone from both units, and no step in 12 s while the first seat ran 237 u; the player's own unit walking, sprinting and jumping still sounds its steps, its jump and its landing. Online, two pages in one room on a test copy of the server (removed after): Iori against Hiyori, each page heard its own bolts and reloads (8 and 2; 3 and 1, with 4 scopes) and of the other unit its shots alone, the same count on both pages (10 and 4); Kei against Hoshino, Kei's charge on Kei's page alone (4), the round going in on Hoshino's alone (7), the beams and the shots on both. `client/src/main.js`.

**Kei's charge is heard by everyone again** (b1105hm, 2026-11-14, PT and 9.7; user, of b1105hl: "kei is an exception, others can hear her charging sound, but still can't hear her reload sound.") — b1105hl made every sound of a gun but its shots and beams the unit's own player's alone, and Kei's charge with them. The charge is the one exception now: it sounds for whoever holds Kei — the player, a bot, another player — and in a spectator's view too, from the charge's start, cut when a sprint cancels the charge, as before b1105hl. Her recharge stays her own player's alone, as every reload, and nothing else of b1105hl changes. Checked headless on the PT build: Saori against a bot Kei, the bot's 7 charges all heard and its 2 recharges not; Kei as the player's own unit, her 5 charges and her recharge heard; the spectator's view, Kei's 7 charges heard and her 2 recharges not. Online, two pages in one room on a test copy of the server (removed after), Kei against Hoshino: Kei's 7 charges on both pages (the other page about 60 ms after her own), her recharge on her own page alone, the round going in on Hoshino's page alone (14), the beams (7) and the shots (14) on both. `client/src/main.js`.

**Seven units' gunshots are recordings of their own guns** (b1105hn, 2026-11-14, PT and 9.7; user, with eight more recordings — MPX, AK, M4, HK, FAMAS, PPSh-41, M14, UMP-45: "These are the new sounds to be update. Crop them and list them out in sound test site.", "if you're not sure which one is the correct sound. list them out and i'll pick it later."; then, a row circled in each of eight screenshots of the site: "here. keep these options on sound test site, we might need them later.") — Saori (M4), Asuna (FAMAS), Subaru (HK), Minori (AK), Marina (PPSh-41), Yuuka (MPX) and Kurumi (UMP-45) fire the gunshot cut from the recording of their own gun and picked on the listening site, each a file of its own. The engine's limit of eight voices of one file fades the oldest tail in a long burst of Saori's, Subaru's and Marina's shots: that takes under 0.25 % of the burst's energy and changes its loudest 400 ms by 0.01 dB at the most against the site, which plays every tail to its end. `client/src/sfx_data.js`, `client/public/sfx`, `client/src/main.js`.

**Fubuki's gunshot is the M14 recording's** (b1105ho, 2026-11-14, PT and 9.7; user, asked whose the picked M14 gunshot is and what the HK's reload should be: "M14 for Fubuki, use M4 reload sound for HK.") — Fubuki's Ruger Mini-14 fires the gunshot cut from the M14 recording, picked on the listening site with the seven of b1105hn, in place of the Mini-14 recording's, which stays on the site. **Hina's gunshot is an MG42 recording's, with the site's echo** (b1105hp, 2026-11-14, PT and 9.7; user, with the upload "MG42._ASMR.mp3": "Crop this for MG42 gunshot. List it in sound test site"; then, a screenshot of the cut's two echo levers at 1.00 and medium: "Use A with these settings for Hina MG42") — Hina's MG42 fires one shot cut from an MG42 recording with the listening site's echo baked into it at amount 1.0, medium length, the setting her made rifle had; the made rifle stays on the site. The recording holds no shot fired alone and a very loud echo two to three seconds long after every burst, so the shot is the last of a three-shot burst, the 80 ms before the first reflection comes back, and its echo is the site's, not the recording's. **Fubuki's gunshot in two parts: her gun's metal sound is her own player's alone** (b1105hw, 2026-11-14, PT and 9.7, online and offline; user: "There's a high pitch metal sound in Fubuki gun shot sound. Can you make that metal sound only be heard by Fubuki itself, others would only hear its gunshot without metal sound.") — The M14 recording that is Fubuki's shot has her gun's action in it, 0.15 s after the shot starts: a few clicks and a ring of narrow tones, the loudest at 9.1 kHz and 24 dB over the sound round it, gone by 0.3 s. The recording is two files now that add up to it sample for sample: the shot without the action's sound, which everyone hears, and the action's sound alone, which is added for the player whose unit Fubuki is — the rule a bolt, a reload and a scope have (a bot's Fubuki, a teammate's, another player's and a spectator's view have the shot alone). Her own player hears the recording as it was. The split is made on the sound's spectrum between 0.14 and 0.32 s, over 0.8 kHz: whatever stands over the level the shot alone would have there — the line through its level before the action and after it — is taken out, and what is taken out is the second file; under 0.8 kHz the action cannot be told from the shot and nothing is touched. `sfx.js` adds the two into one voice for the own player (two voices can start a block of the audio apart, and the halves out of step lose up to 2.5 dB where they overlap); the unit's row of the table names the second (`shotOwn`). On the listening site the metal sound has a slider of its own, and Fubuki has a row for what the others hear. Checked headless on the PT build: 52 sounds in 43 files fetched and decoded; the pair's voice is the shot's buffer plus the metal sound's to the last figure, the metal sound 0.13 to 0.33 s after the play's start; in offline duels Fubuki the player's fired every shot with the metal sound (21 of 21), Fubuki the bot's and Fubuki watched by a spectator none (0 of 19 each); online, two players in one room, her own page had it on all 17 of her shots and the other page on none. `client/src/sfx.js`, `client/src/main.js`, `client/src/sfx_data.js`, `client/public/sfx` (two in, one out: 43 files, 949 kB).

**Hoshino's profile picture has its white sleeves again** (b1105ib and b1105ic, 2026-11-15, PT and 9.7; user, with a picture of the unit picker's full photo of her, the upper arms circled: "Hoshino Profile image's arms/sleeves are overcropped that they become transparent. Should have been white clothes.") — the cut-out that made `units/hoshino_profile.png` (a render of her model, 596 x 596) had taken four white parts of her for background: her two sleeves, which lie in front of the dark backpack (1815 and 537 pixels, clear in their middles with a soft rim), the end of her left sleeve at the figure's right edge and a small white piece under the gun's stock (each bitten in its middle). The photo's class colour showed through them. The file is repaired, not drawn anew: in those four places the alpha is raised (the sleeves' closed-in patches to full; the two bites by a 9 x 9 closing, which leaves the figure's own soft edge as it runs beside them) and white is put behind what was left, so a pixel that kept most of itself keeps its colour and a cleared one is the flat white the render gives her collar and socks. 2,573 of 355,216 pixels changed, none outside those places; the halo's open middle stays open. Her thumbnail is another render and had its sleeves. Checked: no closed-in transparent patch is left in her arms; headless on the PT build the picker's photo shows both sleeves white. From b1105ic the repaired file has a name of its own, `client/public/units/hoshino_profile_v2.png`, so no browser keeps the picture it had under the old name (the game asks for these pictures by name alone): `UNIT_PROFILE_ART` and `profileFile` in `client/src/main.js` name it for the picker's full photo and the floating profile card; every other unit's art is `units/<key>_profile.png` as before, and the old file is gone. `client/public/units/hoshino_profile_v2.png`, `client/src/main.js` (the name table, the note and the tag).

**A spectator hears the steps of the unit they watch** (b1105id, 2026-11-15, PT and 9.7; user: "and i don't think i can hear the footsteps in spectator mode, which i should have could.") — b1105hl made every sound that is a unit's own its player's alone and, along with the gun's sounds the user had named, silenced a unit's steps for a viewer who plays no unit; before it a spectator heard the watched match's first seat walking, whoever was watched. Now a viewer who plays no unit — the Practice row's Spectator, whose **TARGET** picks the unit, and an online room's spectator, whose view is the match's first seat and its teammate once that seat is down — hears the steps, walking and sprinting, of the unit the camera follows, as a player hears their own unit's, and they change with the watched unit (`updateMoveSfx` reads `cameraFocusMech()` for such a viewer). Nothing else that is a unit's own sounds for a spectator: not its dodge, its jump or its landing, and of its gun the shots alone, as before. A player's own sounds are as they were. Checked headless on the PT build and the 9.7 build, offline, two bots in a 1v1, one that walks (Saori) and one that hovers and has no steps (Aris), in either seat: watching Saori, 13 to 17 steps in 8 s; watching Aris, none, though Saori walked on; a leap made to happen on the watched unit sounded no jump and no landing, where the same on a player's own unit sounded both and that unit's dodge by the keys sounded its dodge. The build before it, in the same view: no steps in 6 s with Saori on her feet for 5.6. A played match's steps, sprint, jump, landing and dodge sound as before. No bot dodges, and a bot jumps in Sudden Death alone, so of a watched bot's movement the steps are what there is to hear. The online spectator's view was not run. `client/src/main.js`.

**Maki's profile picture has her gun's white parts and her temple again** (b1105ie, 2026-11-15, PT and 9.7; user, shown the half-clear panel of the gun in her full photo: "Fix maki profile image crop as well.") — the cut-out that made `units/maki_profile.png` (a render of her model, 601 x 601) had taken four parts of her for background: her gun's white receiver panel (1,873 pixels, half of it clear, the photo's class colour showing through), the top band and the end of the gun's slotted white plate (394), the white pin of the bipod's bracket (68), and a patch of her temple's skin between two locks of hair above her far eye (138). The file is repaired as Hoshino's was (b1105ib): in those four places the alpha is raised to full and the part's own colour is put behind what was left of each pixel — white for the gun's parts, whose remains are neutral, and the face's flat skin colour (255, 224, 220) for the temple, whose remains are that colour. 2,473 of 361,201 pixels changed, none outside those places; the open space between the barrel and the bipod is a real gap and stays open. The panel's cleared half is flat white: the rivets it had there are not drawn again. Her thumbnail (`maki_profile_thumbnail.png`) is that picture's own 400 px square at (110, -6), pixel for pixel, and showed the panel and the temple the same wherever it is used (the picker's side card and tile, the lobby's cards, the portraits in a match): it is cut again from the repaired picture, 2,011 of 160,000 pixels changed. Both files have names of their own, `units/maki_profile_v2.png` and `units/maki_profile_thumbnail_v2.png`, so no browser keeps the pictures it had (as b1105ic for Hoshino): `UNIT_PROFILE_ART` names the first; a like table, `UNIT_PROFILE_THUMB`, with `profileThumbFile`, names the second for the 1v1 Duel's lobby card, the profile folder's side card and, through `thumbFile`, every other user of a thumbnail; the old files are gone.
