Checked: no hole is left in either picture but the real gap by the bipod; headless on the PT build and the 9.7 build the picker's full photo, its side card and its tile hold the new files, the panel white, and a match played as Maki draws her portrait from the new thumbnail with no request failing. `client/public/units/maki_profile_v2.png`, `client/public/units/maki_profile_thumbnail_v2.png`, `client/src/main.js`.

**A spectator hears the watched unit's dodge, jump and landing too** (b1105if, 2026-11-15, PT and 9.7; user, asked in b1105id's report whether the dodge, the jump and the landing should follow the watched unit as its steps do: "yes") — b1105id gave a viewer who plays no unit the steps of the unit their camera follows and left that unit's dodge, jump and landing unheard. They sound now as well, as a player hears their own unit's, and change with the watched unit; no other unit's movement is heard, and of a gun the shots alone, as before (`updateMoveSfx`: the three gates b1105id had set are gone). Checked headless on the PT build and the 9.7 build, offline: in a spectated Sudden Death duel on Factory every dodge of the watched bot sounded and none of the other bot's (PT build: 14 of the watched bot's, 14 heard, the other bot's 14 unheard, in 40 s; 9.7 build: 10, 10 and 10); a dodge and a leap staged on the unit in view sounded the dodge, the jump and the landing, and the same staged on the unit not in view sounded nothing, in either seat; the steps as in b1105id; in a played match the player's own unit's sounds as before and the bot's movement unheard. The online spectator's view was not run. b1105id's entry says no bot dodges: that was wrong, the bots dodge in Sudden Death. `client/src/main.js`.

**A spectator hears the watched unit's bolt and the metal part of its shot** (b1105ig, 2026-11-15, PT and 9.7; user: "spectator cannot hear the bolt sound and metal sound when the spectating target is iori and fubuki, which they should have could.") — for a viewer who plays no unit, the unit their camera follows sounds its shot whole, as its own player hears it: the bolt after a shot that leaves rounds (Iori's, Hiyori's) and the part of the shot's recording that is its own player's (the metal sound of Fubuki's gun, b1105hw). No other unit's bolt or metal part is heard, and a bolt begun for a unit is stopped when the view leaves it. A reload, the round going in and the scope stay the player's own unit's alone, as b1105hl has it. `sfxShotWhole` says whose shot is whole; the watched unit is the one the frame's HUD pass found (`sfxViewMech`), because a bot's shot is sounded inside the bot's turn, where `runBotAIForMech` has `state.player` and `state.enemy` standing in for the bot's target and the bot: asked there, the first seat's bot had its bolt while the second seat was watched. Checked headless on the PT build and the 9.7 build, offline, spectated duels: Iori against Fubuki — watching Iori, a bolt after each of her shots that left rounds (13 in 16 s) and Fubuki's shots bare; watching Fubuki, every shot of hers with its metal part (23 of 23 on the PT build, 24 of 24 on 9.7's) and no bolt of Iori's; the view moved off Iori right after a shot, her bolt on its way was gone. Hiyori against Saori — her bolt when she is watched (5 in 16 s), none when Saori is. No reload, scope or round sounded for the spectator. A played match as before: the player's own unit's bolt, reload, scope and metal part, the bot's shots bare. The online spectator's view was not run. `client/src/main.js`.

**A spectator hears the watched unit's reload, round, scope and Kei's recharge too** (b1105ih, 2026-11-15, PT and 9.7; user, asked in b1105ig's report whether the reload, the round going in (Hoshino, Haruka, Aris), the scope (Aru, Hiyori) and Kei's recharge should follow the watched unit as well: "YES") — with this a viewer who plays no unit hears the unit their camera follows as its own player does: every sound of a gun that is its own player's alone (b1105hl) sounds for the watched unit, and for no other. `sfxShotWhole` of b1105ig is `sfxAsOwn` and gates the reload, the scope and the round as it did the bolt and the metal part; a reload or a round's sound begun for a unit is stopped when the view leaves it, as its bolt is. A view that comes to a unit in the middle of its reload does not take that reload's sound up part-way. Checked headless on the PT build and the 9.7 build, offline, four spectated duels whose two units' own-player sounds have different names, each seat watched in turn for 18 s: Iori against Hiyori (watching Iori, her bolt and every reload of hers and nothing of Hiyori's; watching Hiyori, her bolt, every reload and a scope for each of her 7 charges, and nothing of Iori's); Hoshino against Kei (the round going in when Hoshino is watched; Kei's recharge when she is, 2 of 2); Fubuki against Maki (her metal part on every shot and her reload; Maki's machine gun reload when she is watched, 3 of 3); Aru against Aris (her scope and her reload; Aris's round). The view moved off Maki during her reload: the reload's sound was gone. A played match as before: the player's own unit's sounds all there, the bot's shots bare. The online spectator's view was not run. `client/src/main.js`.

**Dust lit as the other daylight maps: its materials are no longer full metal** (b1105ii, 2026-11-15, PT and 9.7; user: "does the Dust map has its own light source?", and, shown the map as it stood beside the same view with its materials' metal value at 0 and asked whether to make it so: "yes. PT and 9.7.") — Dust has no light of its own: every map is lit by the scene's two lights, an ambient light and one directional light that never moves, and a map only names the tone they take (`applyMapAmbience`); Dust takes the daylight tone, the same numbers as Airport's and Warehouse's. Its model carries no lamp, no glowing material and no stored light either. What made it dark was its materials: the file names no metal value for its nine materials, and glTF's own default is 1, full metal. A full-metal surface has no diffuse part, so the ambient light — most of a daylight map's light, and the scene has no environment map to stand in for it — never reached it; all it showed was the sheen of the one directional light. Every face turned from that light was pure black and the rest dim, shifting as the camera turned. It had been so since the map came in (2026-10-19): the card's photo shows it. A modelled map may now name the metal value its model's materials take, GLB_MAPS `metal`, and Dust names 0: `buildGlbArena` sets it on the model's materials as it makes an arena from it, before the fade splits its pieces off, so every piece wears it. The model's file is untouched. No other modelled map is full metal throughout, and none of them names the value. Checked headless on the PT build and the 9.7 build: all 775 of the map's meshes wear metal 0 (they wore 1); from the first spawn, through the game's own camera, the picture's mean brightness goes from 41 to 106 of 255 and its pure black from 15% to none; from four sides around that spawn 36–49 becomes 106–123, the numbers of the experiment the user was shown, to the digit; the scene's lights are the same two at the same strengths; Warehouse, Town and Spacecraft keep their own values. The card's picture (`maps/dust.png`) is still the old dark photo: six samples with the new look were taken for the user to pick from. `client/src/main.js`.

**Dust's card: the picture the user picked, under a new name** (b1105ij, 2026-11-15, PT and 9.7; user, with b1105ii's go-ahead: "And take some map info card image samples.", and, shown six of them, each in the real info card and the picker's tile: "option C for map info card image.") — Dust's card showed the map as it stood dark before b1105ii. Its picture is now sample C: the paved plateau at eye level, its crates and the houses beyond. It was taken on the b1105ii build as a card photo is (photo mode: the fog dropped, the units hidden, every faded wall solid, one 1024-px square frame), from a camera of its own at arena (46, 30, -50) looking to (40, 18, 20), its field 65 degrees, with the map's model and the lights alone in the frame — the first take of another sample had caught the cyan dots a unit's boost leaves on the ground, which hiding the units does not hide. It is stored without the renderer's alpha channel, which was opaque throughout: the same pixels, 1.23 MB for 1.92 (the photo before it was 1.43). The file has a new name, `maps/dust_v2.png`, so no browser keeps the old picture, and `maps/dust.png` is gone: a map's card may now stand under a file name of its own, `MAP_CARD_ART` with `mapCardFile`, as a unit's art does (`UNIT_PROFILE_ART`). The four places that named `maps/<key>.png` themselves — the lobby's map slot, the map's info card, the picker's tile, the end page's memo — ask `mapCardFile`; every other map's card is `maps/<key>.png` as before. Checked headless on the PT build and the 9.7 build: the picker's tile, the info card, the lobby's slot after CONFIRM and the end page's memo after a match on Dust each name `maps/dust_v2.png` and show it, 1024 x 1024; all 20 tiles of the picker load; nothing asks for `maps/dust.png`. `client/public/maps/dust_v2.png` (new), `client/public/maps/dust.png` (removed), `client/src/main.js`.

**A shot is heard from where it is fired: quieter with distance, louder in the ear on its side** (b1105ik, 2026-11-15, PT and 9.7, offline and online; user: "do we have the spatial gun fire feature that adjusts how the gun shots hear like according to units relative locations? i don't feel like. can we make it happen with out making online/offline games more lag?", and, of the way put to them — quieter with distance but never silent, louder in the ear on the shooter's side of the screen, one's own shots as they are, a spectator's and the command view's by the unit the camera follows, walls and front/back left for later: "As you suggested. PT and 9.7") — there was none: every shot played at its one level, the same in both ears, wherever it was fired. Now a unit's sounds that everyone hears — its shot, Kei's charge and her two beams — are heard from where the unit stands, by THE EAR: the unit the camera follows (`cameraFocusMech`: the player's own; a spectator's watched unit; the teammate while the player's unit is down; in the command view the player's own still). How far: full level out to 6 m, then down as (6 m / distance) to the power 0.6 — 3.6 dB for each doubling, gentler than open air's 6, so the ranges a match is fought at are told apart — and never under a quarter: 10 m 0.74, 20 m 0.49, 40 m 0.32, 60 m and beyond 0.25. Which side: the ear on the shooter's side of the screen keeps that level; the other is 10 dB under it for a shooter straight to the side, less by the sine of the bearing off the camera's line (5 dB at 30 degrees); nearer than 2 m the bearing counts for less, so a unit brushing past does not flip from ear to ear. Ahead and behind are not told apart and a wall between changes nothing: both were left for later. The ear's own unit's sounds play as they always did, and so do the sounds that are a unit's own player's alone (bolt, reload, round, scope), which only ever sound for it. The numbers are `SFX_SPACE` in `main.js`. How: `sfx.js` lets a play be given a PLACE, each ear's level; the game keeps one on each unit (`sfxAt`), tells it every frame from the HUD's pass and reads it when a sound of the unit starts, so a sound already ringing follows its unit and the camera. A place's voices go through five small nodes of its own (stereo input, splitter, a gain an ear, merger): the recording is never summed to one channel — most of the shots are two channels that differ, and their sum is up to 1.6 dB under either — so a shot from near and straight ahead is the recording as it was set on the listening site. A camera that locks onto its target keeps a duel's opponent near the middle of the screen, so in a 1v1 it is mostly the distance that is heard; the sides tell in a 2v2 and off the lock. Cost: nothing is sent or asked over the network for it and the server is as it was — a client knows where every unit is. Each frame the rule is worked out once for each unit, about a tenth of a millionth of a second each (measured: 77 to 94 billionths); the mixing is the browser's audio thread's, as every voice's is, where the engine's own graph worked out offline with 48 voices at once — the engine's cap — takes 15.2 ms for each second of sound before and 18.7 ms after, 1.5% and 1.9% of that thread's time. The time between frames in a 2v2 with three machine guns, in the headless browser's software drawing, was 84.7 and 76.5 ms before and 85.8 and 81.5 ms after: the two builds are no further apart than two runs of one build. Checked headless on the PT build and the 9.7 build. The rule's numbers off the game's own function. The engine's real output, read off the sounds' bus in each ear: a place told 1 and 0.316 leaves one ear as it was and the other 10.0 dB down, a place told 0.25 both ears 12.0 dB down, for a mono recording and for a two-channel one whose channels differ (the sniper's shot), to 0.2 dB. A spectated 2v2 of four bots, everyone kept on their feet, each of two seats watched for 14 s: every frame each unit's place against the rule worked out from where the units stood and how the camera lay — none off (a few a frame behind, where a unit or the camera jumped in one frame); a unit clearly to one side of the screen some 190 to 320 times a watch, the wrong ear louder never; the watched unit's sounds direct, every other unit's through its place (94 to 157 sounds a watch, none without). A played duel: the player's own shots direct (64 and 68), the bot's all through its place. The command view: the player's own unit the ear, the bot's shots through its place. Online, two pages in one room: each page hears its own shots as they are and the other player's through that unit's place (6 of 6, and 68 or 69 of as many). The played duels of the earlier sound checks as before, 0 wrong. Nobody listened: how it sounds is the user's to judge. `client/src/sfx.js`, `client/src/main.js`.

**The music at four fifths, Aris's shot a quarter louder, Town's homes swapped, and Dust's building with the painted door closed** (b1105il, 2026-11-15, PT and 9.7, online and offline; user: "1. decrease all BGM sounds by 20% (80% current volume) 2. dust x 48.28 z -19.19 feet -1.17 m (model) cam S 174° [arena x -6.1 y -2.25 z 51.2] build b1105ik 11-15 This is not an enterable area, the door at \"dust x 47.80 z -19.69 feet -1.14 m (model) cam SE 139° [arena x -8.0 y -2.13 z 49.3] build b1105ik 11-15\" is merely a decoration. 3. PT and 9.7", and, while that was in hand: "implement below also: 1. switch the initial spawn position between team1 and team2 in map Town. 2. increase Aris gunshot sound by 25%") — **The sounds.** Each of the ten tracks plays at 0.8 of the level it had, 1.9 dB down (the listening site's fold 11: its slider of all the music at 80, taken in — the menu's three at 0.15, 0.1932 and 0.13064, the character menu's at 0.12328, 0.1432 and 0.08832, the battle's at 0.12328, 0.1368 and 0.12472, the ending's at 0.10496), and Aris's gunshot at 1.25 of its level, 1.9 dB up (fold 12: her own slider at 125 — 0.39696 to 0.4962). Kei's cancelled-charge shot, which is the sound Aris had before her pick, is as it was. The table alone changed (`client/src/sfx_data.js`, written by the site's tool); no file did. **Town.** The two homes are swapped in the map's spawn row (`arena.js` `ARENA_SPAWNS`): team 1 — the offline player's side, the online host's — starts on the east terrace (units 168, 22; its floor 3.9 m up) and team 2 on the west terrace (−208, 10; 2.35 m up). A 2v2 mate stands beside its own home as before (`mateSpawnFor` asks the home, not the slot), and the six places of the Trio respawns' pool are the same six. The server and both clients read the one row; the intro's team shots and the ending's cameras take the units where they stand. **Dust.** The user's place is inside the building on the sloped west street, behind its arched double door. In the model the door is a picture on the building's street face (z −19.93, from x 41.30 to 53.46, down to −2.93 m), set 36 cm into it; the building is a shell with nothing behind the face. The reading had a wall over the door alone (three boxes from 1.5 and 2.0 m up) and east of it, none west of it, and the building's body behind (0.6 to 1.0 m back) stands from the street's zero — while the street here slopes from 2.8 m under that zero at its west end up to 0.1 m under it at its east. A box holds a body only while the body's centre is no more than 2 units under its bottom (`boxHoldsAt`), so on this street the body's centre is under every bottom there: it walked in under the door's lintel and along the hollow between the face and the body, where only the floor's end held it (the void's edge — the user's z −19.19), and jumping or sprinting, which that edge does not hold, it went on under the whole building and out the far side. **Eight boxes added to `dust_data.json`, twelve floors out and two cut**, the rest of the file's text as it was: the face as a wall from 3 m under the street, and the hollow behind it solid. In model metres — west of the door, x 41.40..44.65, z −19.90..−19.343, to the body's 9.5 m, and x 44.65..46.15, z −19.90..−19.15, to its 18 m; the door under its lintel, on the lintel's own line (z −19.90..−19.65), x 46.15..46.65 to 1.5 m and x 46.65..48.40 to 2.0 m; the 25 cm slit between the lintel and the wall east of it, x 48.40..48.655, to 18 m; behind the door, x 46.15..48.655, z −19.65..−19.15, to 18 m; the corridor behind the wall east of the door, z −19.90..−18.90, x 48.655..50.40 to 18 m and x 50.40..53.15 to that wall's 8 m with its top buffer. The floors that lay in there are out — the door sill's seven steep pieces and five strips of the street's plane — and the two strips that ran in from the street end at the face. A body, a round and a bot's sight stop at the face as at any wall. `shared/tools/maps/dust_door_fix.mjs` is the edit (a run on its own output changes nothing; `shared/tools/maps/README.md` lists it with the other hand edits, to be run again after a regeneration). The route grid rebuilt: 2,887 to 2,876 nodes, the 11 gone a row inside the wall (z −19.40, x 42.4 to 52.4, a metre apart: a bot could route there), 361 to 354 jump links, no node new. Checked on the old data and the new, on the PT build and the 9.7 build. The sim's own body walked south at the face from the street at every 5 cm of it (229 places, each plain, sprinting and jumping, 4 s): 420 of the 687 ended past the face before — a plain walk at the door 10 m into the building, a sprint 27 m on, out under its far side — none now, each held a radius off the face (z −20.19; at the wall east of the door −20.44, as before). The sim's reach flooded from a spawn over the whole map, walking and jumping: 102 cells (6.4 m²) no longer reached, one strip along the face, none gained, the map's three small pits as they were. In the built page (headless, offline, free pov, the forward key held from the street 1.6 m north of the door): the unit stopped at z −19.20 walking and −19.30 sprinting before, inside; at −20.19 both ways now, in both builds. Town in the built page, read during the intro: a 2v2 has the player at (168, 22), its mate at (168, 34), the enemy at (−208, 10) and its mate at (−208, 22), a 1v1 the same two homes; two pages in one room against a test copy of the server (1v1 on both builds, 2v2 on PT): the host at (168, 22), the guest at (−208, 10), each page seeing the other there. The sounds in the built page: the menu's track and the battle's track playing at the new levels read off the player (0.15 and 0.12328 in one run, 0.13064 and 0.12472 in the other, by the track drawn), Aris's shot at 0.4962 in the table and fired. The listening site's page with each fold: its 380 buttons tapped, none wrong; a browser holding the folded slider's old number shows it at 100 and keeps its other numbers. Shared suite 212 of 212 in both trees; no page errors. `client/src/sfx_data.js`, `shared/src/sim/arena.js`, `client/public/maps/dust_data.json`, `client/public/maps/dust_nav` (`.wasm` on PT, `.nav` on 9.7), `shared/tools/maps/dust_door_fix.mjs`, `shared/tools/maps/README.md`, `client/src/main.js` (the tag).

**A match's weight: one map in hand, the match's units alone, unit files with only the motions the game plays, and the loading screen doing the graphics card's work** (b1105im, 2026-11-15, PT and 9.8, online and offline; user, of a phone whose page died starting an offline 2v2 Trio in spectator mode after several maps had been looked at in the map picker: "選地圖時不預先載模型，確定後才載 / 刪掉角色模型裡沒用到的動作 / Implement these. BTW, we already made the loading screen, so try to make the best use out of it to make the games (online games especially) more light weight and less lag. I don't mind a longer waiting time in loading screen.") — Until this build nothing a page had loaded was ever let go: every map looked at in the picker and every unit ever picked stayed in memory and on the graphics card for as long as the page lived. **One map in hand** (`holdMap`, `trimMaps`, `mapModelKept` in `main.js`; `dropGeneratedData` in `arena.js`): a tap on a card in the map picker fetches nothing — it fetched the map's model, data and route grid, 30 to 160 MB of memory for a look — and the map confirmed in the picker, named by an online room or started is the one the page gets ready; every other map's model, data and route grid are let go, the graphics card's copies and the decoded pictures with them (`disposeModel`), a model that lands after its map was given up included. An arena goes with its match (the lobby takes it down: every start builds its own), and an arena of another map goes before the new map's files are asked for. Online the room gets its map ready for the host and the guests alike: a guest's used to wait for the match's start, and its page threw "arena data … not loaded" frame after frame until the data file landed. **The match's units** (`trimUnitModels`, `unitModelsOnField`): a unit's model is kept while a unit on the field wears it or a pick names it, and let go otherwise — when a roster is picked (every roll of the die used to leave its units' models in memory), when the lobby comes up, at a start, and online in the room and at each match's start. An offline start takes the last match down first, under the loading screen, before the new one's files are asked for (`startMatch`), so two matches' units never stand in memory together. **A model in hand** is its scene, its clips and its bounds: the loader's own record of the file — the file's bytes over again, its JSON, every picture as it came — is dropped once the model is made. **The units' files carry only the animations the game plays**: `shared/tools/anim/unit_anims.mjs` reads the names off `UNIT_MODELS` (each clip's `name`, or its `base` where the clip itself is borrowed from another unit's file) and `glb_keep_anims.mjs` writes a file with those alone — 24 to 57 animations a file before, seven to nine now, 131.8 MB of model files to 46.4; three's loader built a track for every channel of every animation, 2,900 to 8,300 a file. Nineteen files, under new names (`<name>_v2.glb`, `hiyori_v3.glb`; Minori's held nothing else); the meshes, the skins, the pictures and every kept animation are byte for byte what they were, checked accessor by accessor, and every unit's actions in the built game — clip, length, tracks, loop — are the same before and after. **The loading screen does the graphics card's work** (`matchWarmDraw`, `matchWarmSamples`, `pinPrograms`, `sharedGeo`): the frame drawn under the screen now also draws the roster units built ahead, one of each kind of round, trail and hit ring the match's units fire, and what the scene holds hidden — a unit's see-through outline, the bars and arrows over the units — and sends up every picture still queued; each of these used to reach the graphics card in the middle of play, a roster's second unit in the frame it spawned. The renderer threw a shader away when the last material using it went and compiled it again at the next shot (a round's five times and a hit ring's seven in a two and a half minute match): every program is now kept for the page's life. And a round, a pellet, a laser bolt and a hit ring wear one geometry a kind, where every shot built its own and sent it up, 1,200 to 1,300 of them in that match. **On the release line** (9.8) a unit with a 3D character also stops fetching its 2D pose art unless its model fails to load (`makeUnitSprite`, `warmUnitArt`): four to nine pictures a unit, 40 to 76 MB decoded for each of the twelve units with the full set, for a billboard nobody has seen since the loading screen has the model in hand first; the playtest site has loaded none since b1105k, and the files stay in the repository. **Measured** (headless, a phone-shaped page; the memory of all the browser's processes over the main menu's): six maps looked at and then a 2v2 Trio on Town in spectator mode, eight seconds in — +1,265 MB before and +549 MB now on the playtest build, +1,515 MB and +582 MB on 9.8; the script heap 357 MB to 105 (395 to 122). In 150 s of a 2v2 Trio of bots after the loading screen came off: 18 to 20 shader programs made before, none now; 10 to 24 textures sent up, 3 now; 1,229 to 1,335 geometries first drawn, 5 now; a frame's drawing at the 99th percentile 20 to 22 ms before and 6 ms now, the longest 32 to 40 ms and 7 ms (software rendering: the ratio, not the numbers). Online, two pages in a room: nothing made after the screen in a 2v2 on Compound, two textures at the most across a 2v2 Trio on Town with its respawns. **Known costs**: a start with other units reads their files again under the loading screen (from the browser's cache), and so does a map played earlier; the loading screen's last frame compiles some twenty shaders and sends every roster unit up — a third of a second on the test machine, as before, longer on a phone; a motion the table does not name is no longer in a unit's file (the full models are in the 9.7 branch); a shader, once compiled, is kept for the page's life, a few dozen in all. Checks: 214 tests, two new (`arena_drop.test.js`: a file map's data let go and read again builds the arena it built before; a map whose data is not a file is never let go); on both builds the page's own flows with what it holds read after each (`window.__mapLoad.held()`) — maps confirmed one after another, one while the last was still on its way; a match, the lobby, another match with other units, a knockout, the ending, REMATCH, a Trio on another map straight from a match — and online a 2v2 and a 2v2 Trio with staged knockouts and a REMATCH, no error in any.

**The winner camera on the teammate; the K.O. camera on the side that lost** (b1105in, 2026-11-15, PT and 9.8, online and offline; user: "in 2V2, when the last unit of losing team and the attacker from winning team die at the same time while there is other unit alive in winning team, the "WINNER" camera (not the "FINAL BLOW" camera doesn't seem to work.") — Two faults met in that ending. **No winner camera.** Shot 2 was for the final blow's unit standing, for the roster unit come on in its slot (Trio, b1105gh), or for that unit as it lies with every unit of the match down (b1105gg); with it down, nothing come on in its slot — a 2v2 without rosters, or its roster spent — and its teammate standing, the finale stopped on the K.O. shot, the rule the proto was given (b1105gb: the final blow's unit dead though its side won, skip it). A unit of its side that stands now takes shot 2 as the roster unit does: the take, its victory motion, the call **WINNER** (`finale.mate`, `startFinale` in `main.js`). Shot 2's unit, the first of these that applies: the final blow's unit standing (FINAL BLOW); the roster unit standing in its slot (WINNER); a unit of its side standing — the unit in its teammate's slot, that roster's next where the teammate fell in the same frame (WINNER); with every unit down, the final blow's unit as it lies (FINAL BLOW). **The K.O. camera on the winner's own unit.** The finale opened on the last knockout noted whose unit still lies on the field, and two units down in one frame are noted in the order their hits landed: with the winner's unit noted last, shot 1 read K.O. over a unit of the side that won, its shooter's side had nobody standing to show, and the end page then said YOU WIN. The knockout taken is now the losing side's — offline the win check's side (`endWithFinale`), online `match:end`'s winner, a team's letter in a 2v2 and a slot's id in a duel (`startOnlineFinale`) — and the last of all, as before, where that one is not found. An ending that leaves nobody standing is read the same way: the unit shot 1 shows is of the side the end page names the loser (a duel's double knockout, noted in either order, is K.O. over the enemy's unit and FINAL BLOW over the player's as it lies, under YOU WIN; it went by the order before). Client-side and after the match has stopped: nothing is sent to the server or asked of it, the server's code is untouched, and a match in play runs nothing new. Checked headless on both builds, the knockouts written into the match as real hits leave them (offline the kill log and HP 0; online HP 0 and the tick's hit event, on a test copy of the server). Before, on b1105im: offline a 2v2 with the player's unit down 0.6 s before its round ended the match and the ally standing — K.O. over the enemy, no shot 2; the two down in one frame, the enemy noted first — K.O. over the player's own unit, no shot 2, then YOU WIN; Trio with the player on its last unit, the same; online, two pages in a room, the two down in one tick — K.O. over the host's own unit on both pages, no shot 2, YOU WIN for the host. Now, offline on Plain Field, eleven 2v2 endings on each build: the ordinary win (FINAL BLOW over the player's unit, as it was); the round in the air and the one frame in either order (K.O. over the enemy, WINNER over the ally, YOU WIN); the one frame seen from the side that loses (K.O. over the player's unit, WINNER over ENEMY 2, YOU LOSE); every unit down in one frame (K.O. over the enemy, FINAL BLOW over the player's unit as it lies); Trio with the final blow's slot bringing on its next unit (WINNER over that unit, as b1105gh has it), with its roster spent (WINNER over the ally; with the round in the air too), with the ally down in the same frame and its next unit on (WINNER over that unit), and with the slot's last unit down after its predecessor's round was fired (WINNER over the ally). Six duel endings on each build, to see them stand: a win, a loss, both down in one frame in either order, and Trio with either side's next unit coming on (WINNER over it). Online, two pages in a room, the host and the guest reading the same two units under their own side words: nine 2v2 endings on the PT build — the win, the round in the air, the one tick in either order, the loss, every unit down, and Trio's three — and five of them on 9.8's. No page error and no server error in any. 214 tests pass, none new: the finale is the page's own code, which the suite does not load.

**The ally tactic stands 20 s** (b1105io, 2026-11-16, PT and 9.8, online and offline; user: "make the effeteness duration of "forward/back off" command from 10sec to 20sec. PT and 9.8") — FORWARD (the bot teammate's range band 30 nearer its target) and BACK OFF (the Hide stance) each stood 10 s from the call (b1105gq: 'change the effect to "hide 10 sec" and "lockrange -30 for 10sec"'); they stand 20 s (40 s since b1105jm, no time limit since b1105jn, below). The one number is `ALLY_TACTIC_MS` in `shared/src/sim/constants.js`, 10000 to 20000: the brain, the server and the page read it from there, so the call's end, the lit pill and the lit button move with it and no other code changed. Everything else about a call is as it was: the standing one tapped again is taken back at once, the other one replaces it at once and its own 20 s run from then, a call goes with the unit it was made to, and two calls to one unit stand 0.5 s apart at least. The Guide's line reads "The teammate does it for 20 seconds." **Online the length is the server's**: the server sets the call's end and the pages light what it echoes, so a match is on 20 s once its server runs this commit — whatever the page's build — and stays on 10 s until then. The tests held the 10 s by hand in six places (`shared/test/tactic.test.js`); they are written off the constant now, its value pinned once, with one line more: the call still stands at the moment the 10 s used to end. FORWARD on Plain Field keeps the bot within 70 to 90 of its target (the pushed band is 73 to 87) from 4 s after the call to the call's end at 20 s, inside the unit's lock, and back within 100 to 120 (its own band) from 5 s after; BACK OFF on Streets breaks both enemies' lines within 8 s of the call and is seen on no tick from then to the call's end. 214 tests pass. Checked headless on both builds. Offline, a 2v2 on Plain Field, the first call by the HUD's own taps: before (b1105in) FORWARD and BACK OFF were gone after 9.9 to 10.0 s; now after 19.9 s each, the call standing and the button lit 10.5 s in, the button unlit the frame the call ends, a call taken back 2 s in gone at once. Online, two pages in a room against a test copy of the server, the host calling FORWARD to its bot and the guest BACK OFF to its own: the echo within 0.2 s, 19.8 to 19.9 s left on it, the call and the lit button gone after 19.8 to 20.0 s, the other side's page told nothing of it — on the PT build and on 9.8's. And across versions, on 9.8's builds: this page against the server as it was before, 9.9 s — the server's word; the page of before against this server, 19.9 s. No page error and no server error in any.

**The Guide's line on the lock: red when that enemy fires, and no word of homing** (b1105ip, 2026-11-16, PT and 9.8; user: "and i notice that there are wrong descriptions in in-game Guide. Lock icon goes red when enemy fires at you, and there's no homing effect in game (all tuned to 0 degree). No need mentioning homing effect in Guide either.") — The Fighting list's first line read "Your unit locks on to an enemy by itself. When the enemy is close enough, the lock turns red and your shots follow them. From farther away, shots fly straight and miss more." Neither half is the game. The lock icon is green and turns red while its target fires — for 0.2 s after each of its shots, so a burst holds it red (`updateLocksAndReticle`, `RETICLE_FIRING_FLASH_MS` in `main.js`); a sniper's aim at the viewed unit and Kei's sweep hold it red too; the distance has no part in its colour. And no round is steered: both homing rates are 0 in the rules and in the page's own copy (`HOMING_MAX_DEG_PER_FRAME`, `HOMING_SOFTEN_DEG_PER_FRAME`), so a round flies straight from the muzzle at every range. The line reads "Your unit locks on to an enemy by itself. The lock icon turns red when that enemy fires." — nothing of homing left in the Guide, as asked, and "at you" left out, the icon reading the locked enemy's fire whoever it is aimed at (a teammate, in a 2v2). Text only: no rule, number or behaviour moved. Checked headless on both builds: the Guide opened from the main menu shows the line and no word of homing in its 40 lines; and in a duel on Plain Field with the enemy stood 20 from the player, inside the 110 lock range on every frame, the icon was green on every frame while the enemy held its fire (36 and 38 frames) and red on 36 of 87 and 34 of 88 frames while it fired 11 shots, every red frame within 0.2 s of a shot; of the player's rounds at a target moved sideways 100 away, 30 were watched on each build and none turned (0.0000°).

**The ally tactic button goes by whose seat it is now** (b1105iq, 2026-11-16, PT and 9.8, online; user: "Hide the "Forward/Back off" command button in online game when both player in the same team are not BOT. Still shows and functions when one of the player is BOT. Still not functioning in spectating mode.") — Online the button is there exactly while a bot plays the teammate's seat, and a call made with it is taken. Two players on one side had no button already, and have none. Two cases did not follow the rule. **A rematch.** The page read whether its teammate is a bot once, with the match's set-up (`ensureOnlineMatchSetup`), and that set-up is skipped while the map, the seat and the mode stay the same — so a REMATCH kept the first match's answer: with the teammate gone before the rematch began, a bot played the seat from that match's start, the server took calls for it, and the page showed no button to make them with. **A teammate who leaves in mid-match** leaves a bot too; the server refused calls for it (it took the match-start bots alone, the rule the commander's orders have) and the page showed no button. Now the page reads the seat off each snapshot (`botSlots`, the seats the server drives — `onlineAllyIsBot` in `main.js`), and the server takes a call to any seat of the caller's side that a bot plays (`ally:tactic` in `server/src/index.js`; the commander's orders keep their rule, `startBotSlots`). **A call to a seat with no entry.** A seat left in mid-match has no command entry until the first call made to it — a match-start bot has one from the start — and the brain keeps a reload hide on the fighter alone where there is no entry, reading the entry alone once there is one: the entry the first call made would have let the unit out of a reload hide under way (the machine guns', whose 5 s reload a bot spends out of sight). `setAllyTactic` (`shared/src/sim/command.js`) puts a hide under way onto the entry it makes, as the brain would have at the reload's start, to be released when the reload ends. As they were: offline (the teammate is always a bot); 1v1, Sudden Death, command mode and the Shooting Range, which have no button; spectator mode — the Practice toggle, an online spectator — where it shows and does not answer; the call's 20 s, its take-back, and that it goes with the unit it was made to. No message changed. Across versions: the page of before against this server is as it was; this page against a server of before shows the button over a seat left in mid-match and nothing comes of a tap, that server refusing the call, until the server is this one. Checked headless on both builds, two pages in a room on Plain Field against a test copy of the server, the button read by the HUD's class and its own box and a call made by the HUD's function. Before (b1105ip): two players on one side, no button on either page; the teammate gone 6 s into the match, a bot in its seat, still no button, and a call sent past the HUD refused; the rematch begun without the teammate, no button, and the same call taken by the server. Now: each player beside a bot (the two on opposite sides), the button on both pages, FORWARD standing and the button lit 1.2 s after the call; two players on one side, no button on either page and no call sent; the teammate gone in mid-match, the button up on the page that stays, FORWARD standing; the rematch begun without the teammate, the same. Offline on both builds: a 2v2 played by the player, the button shown and its taps answering; the same match under the Practice toggle's Spectator, shown, the two pills not opening and no call taken. No page error and no server error in any. The reload hide in the sim (Streets, Hina's bot 16 ms into its reload when FORWARD is called): made the old way the entry had the unit out of the stance on all 311 ticks of the reload's rest; now on none, the hide released when the reload ends and the call standing on — a test of its own (`shared/test/tactic.test.js`: a seat with its entry from the start and one without, and no hide carried where none is under way), failing on the function as it was. 215 tests pass.

**A commander's page learns at every match's start whom it may order, and in which view it plays** (b1105ir, 2026-11-16, PT and 9.8, online; user: "In online 2v2 command mode, the commander's page must know at every match start whether its teammate's seat can be ordered. Today it reads this once and keeps a stale answer through a REMATCH.") — Online a commander orders their own unit and, in a 2v2, the teammate's seat when a bot played it at the match's start; a seat its player leaves in mid-match is never the commander's (owner 2026-08-22). The server fixes that set anew at every start (`startBotSlots`, `startMatchFor` in `server/src/index.js`) and takes orders by it. The page worked its own answer out once, with the match's set-up (`ensureOnlineMatchSetup` in `main.js`), and that set-up is skipped while the map, the seat and the mode stay the same — the fault b1105iq mended for the FORWARD / BACK OFF button. **A rematch kept the first match's answer**: with the teammate gone before the rematch began — at the end page, or in the middle of the match before — a bot played the seat from the rematch's start, the server took the commander's orders for it, and the page would not select the unit. **Now** the server sends its frozen set with the snapshots (`startBotSlots`, beside `botSlots`: 28 bytes with two bots listed, on a 2v2 snapshot of some 7,100), and the page takes its answer from that list once a match, at the match's first snapshot (THE MATCH'S OWN ANSWERS, at the head of `ensureOnlineMatchSetup`; each `match:start` is its own, the way the intro and the loading screen already tell one match from the next). The rule has not moved: the match-start bots alone and never the live `botSlots`, so a teammate who leaves in mid-match still leaves a bot the commander cannot order. **The view had the same fault.** The page's own Shooter | Command answer (`onl.commandMode`) was taken in the same skipped set-up, while the server reads the picks anew at every start (`commandSlots`). Whether a pick can differ between two matches that the set-up takes for one was checked. In one room the page's own controls cannot change it: the chip is in the queue room, and the end page has Leave and REMATCH alone. But the server takes a changed pick while a room stands ended (`match:configure`), and a page whose connection drops comes back by itself into a new room — within two seconds of the server's return, in the check — whose pick stands at Shooter, the last match's set-up still standing. On b1105iq, a 1v1 on Plain Field against a bot, the server restarted in mid-match and the same unit and map picked again: after a Command match the server ran the seat as a shooter's, and the page stayed in the command view and sent no input frame for it; after a Shooter match, with Command picked in the new room, the server ran the seat as a bot's under orders, and the page stayed in the shooter view, sending 38 input frames in 0.6 s that the server drops for such a seat. Now the view is taken at each match's start with the other answer, and it is part of what the set-up is built for: a pick other than the standing set-up's builds the set-up anew — the diorama's stand and background on or off, the HUD — as another seat does; the same pick builds nothing, as before. **Across versions.** This page against a server of before: no list comes with the snapshots, and the page reads `botSlots` itself as it did, now at each match's start too — the rematch is right there as well. The page of before against this server is as it was: it does not read the list. **As they were**: the orders, their messages and the server's own gate; a Shooter player's page and the FORWARD / BACK OFF button (its own rule, the seat's player now — b1105iq); a 1v1; a rematch with the same pick, which builds nothing — the units and the HUD of the first match are those of the second. **Checked headless** on this build's page and b1105iq's, two pages in a room on Plain Field against test copies of both servers (a match ended by a staged knockout), the teammate's unit tried by the commander's own gestures — a tap on its card, a tap on the shield, a tap on the ground — and by an order sent past the HUD. Before (b1105iq): match 1, the teammate a player — the tap selects nothing and the order past the HUD is not answered; the rematch begun without the teammate — the tap still selects nothing, and the same order is answered `{ kind: 'stance', ok: true, hide: true }`, the teammate echoed hiding. Now: match 1 the same — nothing selected, no order answered, no entry made on the server; the rematch — the tap selects the teammate and the shield shows, the shield's tap is answered `ok` and the unit hides, and a tap on the ground is answered `{ kind: 'move', ok: true }`, the order standing on the unit in the server's echo and on the page. The teammate gone 3 s into a match: `botSlots` holds the seat and `startBotSlots` does not, the tap selects nothing, the order past the HUD is not answered, and the commander's own unit takes its orders; in the rematch after it the teammate is selected and ordered. A commander beside a bot from the first match on, and two Shooter players in a 1v1: both rematches build nothing and play on. The pick changed in the ended window (sent from the page's own connection), both ways, and the two reconnects above: the page in the server's view each time, a commander's orders answered and a shooter's frames acknowledged; after the change in the ended window the picture is that of a first match in that view. This page against the server of before: the same answers in match 1, in the rematch and after the mid-match leave. The page of before against this server: as before. No server error; no page error but one, once — "Cannot decode detached ArrayBuffer", the music loader's (`sfx.js`, untouched here: a track asked for again while still on its way is decoded twice), made to happen the same way on the build of before. 215 tests pass, none new: the suite loads neither the page's code nor the server's. **Seen on the way and left as they are**: after a reconnect the host is not asked the mode and the rules again — the new room is a Duel 1v1 under Health Point — and the Sudden Death HUD is read in the same skipped set-up, so after a Sudden Death match the page shows that HUD over the new room's Health Point match on the same map; and a host who leaves a 2v2 in mid-match leaves a room in which REMATCH waits for ever, nobody sitting in p1 (the last is mended in b1105is, the first two in b1105it, both below). **In PT too** (the same day): the change was made on 9.8 in a session of its own and brought into PT line for line. Checked headless on PT's build against PT's server and again on 9.8's, two pages in a room on Plain Field, the host in the Command view and the second player in the teammate's seat: match 1, the page holds that its teammate cannot be ordered and a Hide order for that seat sent past the HUD is not answered; the rematch begun without the teammate, the page holds that it can and the same order is answered ok, the teammate shown hiding. The FORWARD / BACK OFF button's four cases of b1105iq run again on both builds: as they were. `server/src/index.js`, `client/src/main.js`.

**The end of a match online: REMATCH, RESELECT or Leave, and a player who leaves is named** (b1105is, 2026-11-16, PT and 9.8, online; user: "Revamp the current online rematches flow. Currently there are only rematch and leave options, any player's wouldn't know if opponents have left. I want there be rematch/reselect/leave options, where reselect will let host to reselect the map and both sides to reselect their units. The mode could not be changed though. And player should be notified that other human player has left and be replaced by BOT."; and, of the ways put to them: "等所有人都選完，只要有一人選 RESELECT 就重選。" — everyone chooses first and one RESELECT is enough — everyone picks a unit again and the host the map with the last pick marked, and the waiting room open as a new room's is) — **What it was.** The end page had Leave and REMATCH. A REMATCH turned the page into a bare "Waiting for opponent…" with no button on it, and nothing anywhere said that a player had gone. Measured on b1105ir before the change: the other player gone from the end page, the page showed nothing and a REMATCH began at once against a bot; REMATCH pressed first and the other gone after it, the wait stood for ever; a 1v1 left in mid-match ended on YOU WIN with no reason given; a 2v2's leaver went unremarked while a bot played the unit on; and a 2v2 whose host left in mid-match left a room in which REMATCH waited for ever, nobody sitting in p1 (the leftover b1105ir named). **The end page now** has three buttons, Leave | RESELECT | REMATCH. A player's choice is sent, its button stays pressed with a tick, and it may be changed until the room moves; the page stands the while, Leave with it. Beside every other seat's row a tag says what its player has done: REMATCH, RESELECT, CHOOSING…, **LEFT · BOT NEXT** for a player who has gone — in mid-match or at this page — or BOT for a seat a bot played from the start. The line under the title says why a match ended early ("The other player left the match"), or whom the room still waits for. **The rule** (`shared/src/sim/rematch.js`, `endVerdict`): the room moves when everyone who still sits has chosen; one RESELECT among them is enough for the room to pick again; a player who left is not waited for. **REMATCH** is the match again — the same map, units and seats, a seat nobody sits in a bot's. It needs no host: the room keeps the last match's map itself (`lobby.mapKey`), so the room a host left goes on. **RESELECT** takes the room back to its waiting room (`beginReselect` in `server/src/index.js`): every player's unit — a Trio roster — and the host's map are cleared, and the pages follow the room's state as they do in a new room: the unit picker, then the host's map picker, then the waiting room and the host's Start. The card picked for the last match wears a yellow **LAST** tag — a Trio roster's cards their places, LAST 1·3 — under a line "Last time: …", and the map picker opens on the last map's tab; a mark alone, the card is picked by its two taps as any other. The mode stands: Duel or Trio, 1v1 or 2v2 and the rules are the room's once it has played (`modeLocked`; the server refuses `match:set-mode` there and the host's page does not ask). The bot units the host chose and each player's Shooter | Command pick stay as they were, to be changed in the waiting room as ever — a view changed there is the next match's (b1105ir's answer, taken at every start). And the waiting room is open as a new one is: a player who presses Online is put into a free seat of it, a 2v2's players may change sides, the host starts. **Seats do not change hands while a room stands ended.** The end page names its rows by the match's seats. Until now a host who left the end page was replaced there at once, the player who stayed moved into p1 — and that page, working its own side out from the seat it now held, turned YOU LOSE into YOU WIN. A host is made where one is needed, in a waiting room: at once when the host leaves one, and as the room goes back to waiting on a RESELECT. The page takes its side from the seat it played the match in (`slotMap.cameraId`). **A player who leaves is named, wherever the others are.** In a 2v2 match one line stands for 4.5 s — "ENEMY HOSHINO LEFT · BOT TOOK OVER", the seat by the words of the reader's own HUD — on the left above the stick: the top of the middle column is the kill feed's and the lock bracket's. It is said when the match is on the screen: a leaving under the loading screen or the game-start intro waits for them to be over (`sayLeftNotices`). A 1v1 left in mid-match is a forfeit as before, and its end page says so. At the end page the seat's row reads LEFT · BOT NEXT. In a waiting room or a picker a line at the top middle says "A PLAYER LEFT THE ROOM" — over the page's own title for its 4.5 s, which the user, shown it, took as it is: "I'm fine with that if that's temporary; it is supposed to be visually obvious." **And the room does not move in the same breath as a leaving** (`END_LEAVE_NOTICE_MS`, 3 s, `endHoldLeft`): where the player who leaves the end page was the last the room waited for, the next match would begin at that instant, a bot in the seat and no word of it. The room holds for 3 s from a leaving at the end page; the pages show the seat as left and the line "The other player left · starting with a BOT…" ("back to the room…" where a RESELECT stands); a choice made or changed meanwhile is taken and waits with the rest; a second leaving starts the hold again. A leaving in mid-match holds nothing — the others were told then. **The messages.** `match:end-choice { choice }` is new; a REMATCH is sent in the old `match:rematch-request` as well. The room's `lobby:config` carries `endChoice`, `leftSlots`, `modeLocked` and `reselects` — some 100 characters on a message sent when the room changes, and now at a match's end too. Nothing is added to the snapshots: a match's own traffic is as it was. **Across versions.** A page of before on this server: its REMATCH counts (the old message is a REMATCH, and the old field is still filled); it has no RESELECT and shows no tags; where the other player asks for a RESELECT it is taken to its unit picker with the room, and plays on. This page on a server of before: no RESELECT button — that server knows none — and REMATCH by the old message; that server names no leavers, so a seat left reads BOT, and its old fault stands until it is updated: REMATCH pressed first and the other gone after it, nothing starts (Leave is on the page now). **Checked.** `npm test`: 220 pass, 5 of them new (`shared/test/rematch.test.js`: the verdict, the seats left, the hold). At the wire against a test copy of the server, 21 cases: both REMATCH; one RESELECT with the other still choosing; a changed choice; the old message alone and both messages together; a leaving before and after a choice, with the hold timed (3.0 s); a second leaving in the hold; everyone gone in the hold; a 1v1 forfeit and a 2v2 leaver, neither holding the room; the host gone at the end page and in mid-match; three players of a 2v2; a newcomer taking the free seat of a RESELECT's waiting room and none put into an ended room; the mode refused against the host; Trio rosters. On the pages, two and three in a room headless, 15 cases on each build, read off the DOM and photographed: the three buttons and the tags in Duel, 2v2 and Trio; a choice changed and changed back; the pickers' LAST tags and the map's tab; the next match on the new map with the new units; the leaver's line in a match, and held back under the loading screen until the intro was over; the hold's page; the promoted host's pickers; a newcomer joining; a side changed; the view changed to Command and back. Five more runs on 9.8's with the versions mixed: this server with a page of before as guest and as host, and a server of before under this page. The leaver's line photographed again on screens 568, 667 and 932 px wide and in the Command view (up to 600 px it is set a size smaller, short of the middle column). The FORWARD / BACK OFF button's cases (b1105iq) and the commander's rematch (b1105ir) run again: as they were. No server error, no page error. **Known costs.** A REMATCH after a leaving at the end page starts up to 3 s later than it used to. The waiting room of a RESELECT takes whoever presses Online next, a stranger too, as every waiting room does. A room its host left at the end page has no host until somebody asks for a RESELECT; it needs none for a REMATCH. `shared/src/sim/rematch.js`, `shared/test/rematch.test.js`, `server/src/index.js`, `client/src/main.js`, `client/src/online/connection.js`, `client/src/style.css`.

**A page that comes back from a dropped connection is in another room, and now behaves so** (b1105it, 2026-11-16, PT and 9.8, online, the page alone; user: "重連後不再詢問模式 Explain", and, shown what happens and the way out put to them — the page sends the host's own pick again, with no question: "Fix") — **What it was.** A page whose connection drops and comes back — the server restarted or redeployed, or the player's own line gone for a moment — is a NEW visitor to the server: its seat was let go with the line, and it is put into another room, as the host of a fresh one or as a guest of one that waits (`pickLobbyForJoin`). A fresh room stands at the server's defaults: Duel, 1v1, Health Point. The page went on as if nothing had changed. Measured on b1105is, the server stopped and started again under the pages: (1) the host is asked the mode once a visit to Online and was not asked again — Trio, 2v2 and Sudden Death chosen, the page stood in a Duel 1v1 Health Point room under a picker still headed "Trio … Rules: SUDDEN DEATH", three units asked for and one played; (2) a guest who came back first, and so as the host of a new room, was not asked either (a guest counts as asked), was told "A PLAYER LEFT THE ROOM" of the room it had just come into, and kept "Waking the server up…" over its page; (3) the old host, back second as a guest of that room, was shown its own old mode's picker — three units asked for in another host's Duel room; (4) a page back in a Health Point room on the map, in the seat and under the team size of the Sudden Death match it had just played kept the Sudden Death HUD, its bars hidden, over the new match (b1105ir named this one and the first as leftovers); (5) and at any join, a frame that fell between the seat's message and the room's config drew the unit picker off no config at all, the Duel picker whatever the room: a guest of a Trio room then sent one unit at a time for ever and never left the picker (seen once in some forty joins; made to happen each time by holding the config back 700 ms on a test copy of the server); (6) and, read while the fix was written and then seen on a page of b1105ip: the unit picker is drawn when the phase moves, and stood as drawn when the room's mode changed under it — a guest who came into a room while its host was still choosing the mode stood in the Duel picker of the defaults, the host chose Trio, and the guest's picker went on sending one unit, which a Trio room does not take as a roster: the guest never left it. That one is as old as the mode page, and the host's pick sent again (below) would have opened the same gap for a guest who comes back in the same moment. **What it is now.** The page knows that its room changed: `connection.js` counts the connections made (`getJoinCount` — not the room's id, which a restarted server gives out again: its rooms are numbered from 1 anew) and drops the old room's config as the connection is made again. From then until the new room's config is in, the page is the connecting page: what it holds meanwhile is of two rooms, the old one's match and the new one's seat. Then (`settleNewRoom` in `main.js`): **the new room's host, who chose a mode as a host before** — that pick is sent again (`match:set-mode`, which a room that has not played takes), with no question, and the pickers follow it; **the new room's host, a guest until now** — asked the mode, as any host is; **a guest of the new room** — the room's mode, as any guest, the page's own old pick put by for a room it hosts again; **a room that has played** — its mode stands, nothing sent and nothing asked. The page is drawn anew for the new room whatever its phase, and what it had noted of the old room goes with it: no word of a player's leaving read across two rooms, no overlay left over a page whose phase did not move. The units and the map are picked again — the server holds nothing of the old room — with the last picks tagged LAST, as at a RESELECT. The match's rules are part of the match set-up's signature (`ensureOnlineMatchSetup`), as the view is since b1105ir: other rules build the set-up anew, the same rules build nothing. No picker is drawn before the room's config (`computeOnlineUiPhase`), at a first join either. And from the tap on the mode page until the server's echo the pickers go by the page's own pick as a whole (`modeSent`): the team size and the rules with it, where the main mode alone was held so, and for good (a host who chose 2v2 read "Trio 1v1" over its picker). **The picker follows the room** (`unitPickerSig`, `refreshUnitPickerIfStale`): what the unit picker was drawn for — the seat, Duel or Trio, the team size, the rules — is kept and looked at every frame the picker stands; other values draw it anew, the picks half made in it dropped. **Nothing on the server and no message changed.** The fix is the page's alone and holds against a server of before. **Checked.** `npm test`: 220 pass, none new — the suite loads neither the page's code nor the server's. Headless against a test copy of the server, stopped and started again in mid-visit, the pages' DOM and state read and photographed, on 9.8's build: the host back alone (the room a Trio 2v2 Sudden Death again 0.4 s after the connection, the picker headed so, the match played under those rules); the guest back first and the old host second (the first asked the mode, no line about a leaver, no overlay left; the second shown the room's Duel picker); a Sudden Death match and then a Health Point room on the same map in the same seat (the bars shown); the config held back 700 ms at a first join (the connecting page, then the Trio picker) and at a coming back — from a waiting room, from a match, from an end page, the guest back first into another seat, and the host back first with its pick sent again — with every frame of the pages recorded in the page itself: no page of a room drawn while no room was in hand; a guest standing in the unit picker while the host chooses Trio, 2v2, Sudden Death (the picker drawn anew as the Trio picker with the rules line; on a page of b1105ip it stood as the Duel picker and took one unit after another for good); and both pages back, the host's pick held 9 s on its page so that it reached the room after the guest was in (the guest's Duel picker drawn anew as the Trio picker as it landed). b1105is's fifteen page cases again, four runs with the versions mixed (a page of before as guest and as host on this server; this page on a server of before), the FORWARD / BACK OFF button's cases (b1105iq) and the commander's rematch (b1105ir): as they were. On PT's build seven of the reconnect cases and five of the page cases. An offline match on each build: it runs. No server error and no page error in the online runs; the offline match shows the music loader's page error of before (a track asked for again while on its way is decoded twice — the same on a page of b1105ip; left as it is). **Known costs.** The units and the map are picked again after a coming back. Which of two players who come back becomes the host is the server's answer, as before: the first to connect — an old guest who is first is asked the mode, and the old host follows as its guest, its own pick waiting for a room it hosts. The connecting page may show for an instant between the connection and the room's config. A match in play when the line dropped is not come back into: the seat is gone, and a 1v1 was forfeited by the drop. `client/src/main.js`, `client/src/online/connection.js`.

**Subaru's and Minori's gunshots change hands, and Aris's is twice as loud** (b1105iu, 2026-11-16, PT and 9.8, online and offline; user: "Switch Subaru and Minori gunshot sound. Increase Aris gunshot sound by 200%." — and, asked whether that is twice or three times what it is, a level of 0.99 or of 1.49: "2 倍（0.99）") — Subaru's shot is the last shot of the AK-47 recording's burst, which was Minori's, and Minori's the HK recording's shot, which was Subaru's. Each plays at the level it had as the other's (0.26344 and 0.15288 of the game's volume): the files and their levels changed hands, nothing was cut anew, and each unit fires its new sound at its own rate. Aris's shot plays at 0.9924 where it was at 0.4962, 6 dB up — the loudest gunshot of the table now (Kurumi's is at 0.87). On the listening site (its fold 13) Subaru's sound is the AK-47 group's C and Minori's the HK33 group's shot: each stands first in the other unit's group as its pick and is marked in its own group as the other's, and Aris's slider is back at 100. The table is written by the site's tool as ever; no file is added and none removed. **Checked.** The table differs from the one before in those three rows alone (6 of its 210 values, the fold's number among them). `npm test`: 220 pass. In the built page of each tree, headless, a match as each of the three with the fire key held: the game plays the unit's own shot (the engine's own log), and the table's row of it names the new file at the new level; on b1105it's builds, the same run, the old. No file missed, no page error. The listening page: the three groups' rows and marks read off it, no page error. `client/src/sfx_data.js`, `client/src/main.js` (the tag).

**The weapon in hand, on the fire button** (b1105iv, 2026-11-16, PT and 9.8, online and offline; user: "I'm thinking the possibility to add a weapon i on the player is using in the screen, along with a mini map. But the HUDs are already crowded on mobile ends. What options do i have?"; of what the mark is for: "what i want is to add the call of duty style weapon using feeling. so theoretically the nearer the icon is to the firing button, the better. but not strictly necessary."; of the design canvas's round 16b, three places by the fire button: "W1, but don't use silhouette for weapon, use origin image"; of round 16c, that place with every unit's weapon on it: "描邊：深色描邊只加在這五把 彈數維持 17 px PT and 9.8") — the fire button shows the weapon of the unit the HUD follows, over the ammo count: the unit's own render, the picture the kill feed and the profile card show (`weapons/<spriteKey>.png`), cut to the gun's box (`WEAPON_BOX`). No new piece stands on the screen. The user's phone shows the game on a page of 597 × 320 — measured off their screenshot: a frame of the game at that size has every HUD piece where the screenshot has it — and on that page the one whole free place is the button grid's empty slot. **The measures** are shares of the button's own size (`--btn-size`), read off the canvas's sample on that page's 67.33 px button: the count 17 px (22 px and more where it stands alone), 3 px between the picture and it, the pair 2 px low, and the picture as wide as its top corners allow 6 px inside the button's edge (`fireArtFit`), so that a long thin gun is drawn wider than a short tall one — on that button Yuuka's MPX 41.8 px, Kurumi's UMP45 42.7, Iori's KAR98K 48.5. The button's crosshair logo is out while a picture is on it. **Five weapons with white or pale bodies** lose their shape on the white button — Aris's, Fubuki's, Hoshino's, Kei's and Koyuki's — and carry an ink line round them (`FIRE_ART.lined`); the others carry none (every weapon carries it since b1105ix, and the list is gone). **Drawn ahead**, as the kill feed's pictures are and for the cost measured there: `warmKillArt` draws the button's picture from the render it fetches already (the loading screen waits for it) and keeps an `<img>` to clone, the ink line drawn into it — no canvas and no filter in the HUD. `fireArtShow`, from `updateHud`, puts it on the button when the HUD's unit changes: a Trio roster's next unit, the watched unit in Spectator mode, the ally once the player is out for good. Until a drawn picture is in, and on a page that cannot draw one, the plain render stands in, placed by the gun's box. The kill feed's sticker and this picture share the step that grows a shape for an edge (`killArtGrown`, lifted out of `killArtGun`). **States.** The reload ring is as it was. An empty magazine fades the picture with the count and the button. A unit with no weapon render keeps the button as it was, logo and all. **Checked.** `npm test`: 220 pass, none new — the suite loads no page code. Headless, on each tree's build, a touch page of 597 × 320: every one of the 21 units in a Duel — the button shows that unit's weapon as the drawn picture, the logo gone, the count at 17 px, the gun centred within 0.02 px, its top corners 2.98 to 3.00 px inside the button's white, 2.90 to 3.12 px over the count, 41.8 to 48.5 px wide — and a photograph of each button, the five with their line; a magazine emptied: the picture at 0.4, the ring on; a Trio with the first unit knocked out: the next unit's weapon, drawn already; Spectator mode: the watched unit's weapon, and after TARGET the next one's; the drawn picture taken away (Kurumi's): the plain render with the gun where it was, within 0.1 px. On PT's build, pages of 727 × 390 and 1280 × 720 (Kurumi, Iori, Yuuka): the same shares of a 72 px and an 84 px button, the count at 18.2 and 21.2 px. A frame of the game on the phone's page, Town, the scene of the canvas's samples: the button as the sample has it. Online, each tree's build, two pages in a room on a test copy of 9.8's server, Kurumi and Hoshino: each page its own unit's weapon as the drawn picture, the count beneath at 17 px, and the button as it stood after the knockout that ended the match. The kill feed's stickers (Kurumi's and Saori's, gun and face), read off the page as the pictures' own data on the build of b1105ip and on this one: the same. A picture takes 1 to 2 ms to draw in the headless page. No file missed, no page error, no server error. **Known costs.** The count is smaller where the picture stands over it (17 px for 22 on that phone). The crosshair logo is gone from the button. The thumb that fires covers the picture, as it covers the count. One more small picture per unit is drawn under the loading screen. The mini map of the same design rounds is not part of this build. `client/src/main.js`, `client/src/style.css`.

**The mini map** (b1105iw, 2026-11-16, PT and 9.8, online and offline; user, who had asked for a weapon icon "along with a mini map" on a phone HUD "already crowded", of the design canvas's rounds 16 and 16b: "Use the W1 version of mini map. When player taps on the map, the option C style map pops up. tapping anywhere again closes the pop up map (pause button still functions on its own).") — **a round map** stands in the one empty slot of the thumb buttons' grid, the one above TARGET's: the 35 m about the unit the HUD follows (`MINI_MAP.reach`), turned with the camera so that what the screen looks at is up — where the stick pushes forward — with an N tag riding the rim at north. That unit is the arrow at its middle under a fan of its view; every other unit standing is a dot in its own colour, its chip's and its edge triangle's, and one farther off than the map reaches is a triangle on the rim pointing out to it. It is 4 px smaller than a button and sits in the buttons' grid, so it is sized with them on every screen and hides where they hide: command mode, the game-start intro, the finale. **A tap on it** lays the whole map over the middle of the view, between the chips and BOOST: north up, the map's name on it, each unit's mark with the chips' side word beside it (none on a spectator's page, as the chips have none). Play goes on under it. **The next press anywhere** takes it away and is no press of anything else — the stick, a thumb button, the round map itself, the empty screen — except PAUSE's, which closes the map and opens its menu as ever (one listener on the window, ahead of every other, stops the press). (Since b1105iy the round map alone closes the large map and every other press, PAUSE's too, does its own work under it: that entry.) A touch already down when the map opens, the stick's or the fire button's, keeps working. A knockout that ends the match under the large map takes the HUD away, the map with it, and the next press is the ending cameras' own: the listener lets a press through while the map is not on the screen. **The picture** is the map itself, photographed from straight above once per arena (`miniMapShoot`) under the loading screen, with the match's warm frame: the play area and a little about it through an orthographic camera on the game's own renderer, 1024 px on its longer side (the renderer's buffer at the picture's size for that frame, set in one step and back in one: never the picture's size at the screen's pixel ratio), nothing drawn but the map — and the map dressed as command mode shows it from above, which every map already has an answer for: the built maps' airborne dressing out, a modelled map's roof out (Compound, the Backrooms), its ceiling cut (the Hospital — by the camera's own height here), the things over and beside it out (Neon's towers, Dust's skyline). No shader is made for it that play does not use: the fog is pushed out of range, not taken away; a unit's light stays lit while the unit is not drawn; the picture is read off the canvas in the same task, no render target. A little colour out and a wash of ink, then one JPEG data address for both maps' `<img>` — no canvas and no filter in the HUD — and a frame moves a handful of transforms. The picture is kept while the map stays the same: a second match on it takes none. **Who is on it**: the edge triangles' rule — under the anomaly rules and on the Shooting Range none but the viewer's own mark; a unit down has none until its roster's next stands. **Checked.** `npm test`: 220 pass, none new — the suite loads no page code. Headless, on each tree's build. A touch page of 597 × 320, a 2v2 Duel on Town: the round map 63.3 px at (434.3, 92), in the slot above TARGET's, its picture 1024 × 778 and 122 kB, the viewer's arrow at its middle and the three others as dots and rim triangles in their chips' colours; a tap opens the large map, 213 × 162 at (192, 100), between the chips and BOOST, with TOWN, N and YOU / ALLY / ENEMY / ENEMY 2 beside the marks; a touch on the stick closes it and the stick does not move; PAUSE closes it and opens its menu. The rules, on that page: a 1v1 (no TARGET — the round map alone in its column); two fingers — the stick held, a second finger opens the map, a touch on FIRE closes it and fires nothing, the next one fires, the stick held throughout; Spectator mode (the watched unit's arrow, TARGET moves it, no side words on the large map); a Trio (a unit down has no mark, the next has one); command mode (the round map away with the buttons, the large map closed with them); free pov (the round map turns with the crosshair); the game-start intro (both maps away, PAUSE there) and the ending cameras begun by a knockout under the large map (the HUD away, and one tap ends the shot). Every one of the 20 maps on 9.8's build and eight on PT's: a picture of each, 40 to 296 kB, the marks where the units stand; the Backrooms (Anomaly) and the Shooting Range with the viewer's own mark alone. PT's build at 727 × 390, 844 × 390 and 1280 × 720: the round map 68, 68 and 80 px, the large one 215.8 × 164 and, on the last, 578.5 × 439.7 with its marks at 2.0 for the phone's 1.2; and a mouse page — a click opens, a click on the view closes and fires nothing. Online, each tree's build, two touch pages in a 2v2 on Town on a test copy of 9.8's server: each page its own arrow in its seat's colour, the large map on the page that touched it alone, a touch on the stick closes it. The shader programs a match holds once it plays: 24, 30 and 31 on Town, the Hospital and Factory, as on b1105iv (the picture's frame makes 5, 6 and 1 of them ahead of the warm frame, which made them before). The frame: the maps' update 0.05 ms a call, with the large map up too; style 0.1 ms a frame more than without them, layout the same; the page's frame clock the same with and without, within its scatter. No file missed but the Backrooms' grid file, which the tree does not have; no page error, no server error. **Known costs.** The slot above TARGET is no longer empty. A press that meets the large map closes it and does nothing else: a round map touched by mistake costs the next press (until b1105iy). The large map covers the middle of the view while it is up. The first match on a map holds its loading screen for one more frame, the one that draws the whole map (in the headless software renderer that frame, its reading back and the JPEG take 0.6 to 0.9 s on Town, District and the Hospital, as much as one to two frames of play drawn to their end there, and the screen stayed 2.7 and 3.8 s longer over three starts each on Town and District — most of that the wait for the frames that renderer still had queued, which a picture read back must wait for); it has not been timed on a phone. On a desktop in free pov the mouse is held by the view and cannot reach the round map: the large map opens there in the angled view only. A dark map (Factory, Station, the Backrooms) gives a dark round map. `client/src/main.js`, `client/src/style.css`.

**The ink line round every weapon on the fire button** (b1105ix, 2026-11-16, PT and 9.8, online and offline; user, of b1105iv's line on five weapons alone, "描邊：深色描邊只加在這五把": "apply to all other weapons as well.") — every weapon's picture on the fire button carries the ink line now: the sixteen others as the five pale ones have had it since b1105iv, 2 px of the 208 px picture in the ink (`FIRE_ART.line`; 0 draws none), drawn into the picture ahead, under the loading screen, with the kill feed's pictures. The list of the five (`FIRE_ART.lined`) is gone. Nothing else on the button moves: the picture's size and the gun's place in it, the count at 17 px, the empty magazine's fade. The kill feed's stickers are another picture and stay as they were. **Checked.** `npm test`: 220 pass, none new — the suite loads no page code. Headless, on each tree's build, a touch page of 597 × 320, every one of the 21 units in a Duel: the drawn picture read as its own pixels — of its outermost solid pixels the ink's share is 0.99 to 1.00 on all 21 (on the build before: 0.99 to 1.00 on the five, 0.00 to 0.08 on the sixteen), the sixteen have 1,315 to 2,240 more solid pixels each and the five the same as before; the button's layout and the picture's size as before on every unit (the gun 41.8 to 48.5 px wide, centred within 0.02 px, its top corners 2.98 to 3.00 px inside the button's white, the count at 17 px) — and a photograph of each. A magazine emptied, a Trio's next unit, Spectator mode: as before. On PT's build, pages of 727 × 390 and 1280 × 720 (Kurumi, Iori, Yuuka): the line on each. The kill feed's stickers (Kurumi's and Saori's, gun and face), read off the page as the pictures' own data on the build before and on this one: the same. The mini map's page on PT's build: as b1105iw. A frame of the game on the phone's page, Town: the button with the line. Online, each tree's build, two pages in a room on a test copy of 9.8's server, Kurumi and Hoshino: each page its own unit's weapon as the drawn picture, the count at 17 px. No file missed, no page error, no server error. **Known costs.** A dark gun reads a little heavier with the line about it. **The round map is the large map's switch** (b1105iy, 2026-11-16, PT and 9.8, online and offline; user, of b1105iw's "tapping anywhere again closes the pop up map": "Change it to tapping the map button to close it instead. Other buttons still function while the big button is shown.") — a tap on the round map opens the large map and the next tap on the round map takes it away; no other press does.
