Every other press does under the large map what it does without it: the stick, FIRE, DODGE, JUMP, SPRINT, TARGET, the tactic button, PAUSE, the empty screen's taps and swipes; a press on the large map itself goes through it to the view (it takes no press: `.mm-pop`). The listener on the window that closed the map and stopped the press (b1105iw) is gone, and with it the press a map opened by mistake used to cost. PAUSE no longer closes the map: its menu's page covers the screen, and the map is still up when the pause ends. The large map goes of itself only where its switch cannot be reached: where the buttons hide — command mode, the game-start intro, the ending cameras — and on a desktop page as the free pov takes the mouse (`pointerlockchange`). **Checked.** `npm test`: 220 pass, none new — the suite loads no page code. Headless, on each tree's build, a touch page of 597 × 320, a 2v2 Duel on Town: a tap on the round map opens the large map; under it the stick moves (its knob at 38.4 px), FIRE fires (25 rounds to 16), DODGE, JUMP and SPRINT take their presses, TARGET moves the lock from ENEMY to ENEMY 2, a tap on the empty screen and a tap on the large map itself (the view takes that press) change nothing — the large map still up after each; a tap on the round map closes it, and it opens and closes again. PAUSE under the large map: the menu opens, the map open behind its page and still open after Resume, then closed by the round map. Two fingers: the stick held, a second finger opens the map and closes it on the round map, the stick held throughout. A knockout that ends the match under the large map: the HUD away, both maps with it, and one tap ends the shot. Command mode puts the large map away with the buttons; back in the shooter the round map is there and the large one closed. A page without touch, 1280 × 720, each build: a click opens, a click on the view leaves it open, a click on the round map closes; the free pov taking the mouse puts it away. PT's build besides: 727 × 390 the same (the knob at 44.8 px); the mini map's other rules as they were (a 1v1, Spectator mode, a Trio, free pov; Town, the Hospital, the Backrooms (Anomaly) and the Shooting Range); the fire button's picture as it was. Online, two pages on a test copy of 9.8's server, PT's build and 9.8's: page A opens its large map (B's stays closed), holds its stick for 2.5 s under it and its unit moves — 30.9 units on A and 31 as B sees it (9.8's build: 34.9 and 35) — the map still open; A's next tap on the round map closes it; no page error, no server error. **Known costs.** The large map stays over the middle of the view until the round map is tapped again: a tap elsewhere no longer puts it away, and a player who leaves it open fights under it. `client/src/main.js`, `client/src/style.css` (a comment).

**The round map goes down under a finger** (b1105iz, 2026-11-16, PT and 9.8, online and offline; user: "Give mini-map button the same pushed down effect when tapped as other maneuvers buttons.") — pressed, the round map moves 2 px down and right onto its shadow and the shadow halves, 4 px to 2 px: the thumb buttons' own pressed look (`.buttons button.is-down`); its ink ring stays. By the buttons' own means (b1105cd): each touch marks the round map while it is down (`.is-down`, `setupMiniMap`), so it shows pressed under a second finger too, the stick held; lifted, or cancelled by the system, the mark goes. A mouse keeps `:active`. The press itself is as it was: the large map opens or closes as the finger goes down (b1105iy). **Checked.** `npm test`: 220 pass, none new — the suite loads no page code. Headless, on each tree's build, a touch page of 597 × 320, a 2v2 Duel on Town: at rest the round map is 63.3 px at (434.3, 92), not moved, its shadow 4 px; a finger down on it: marked, 2 px down and right (at (436.3, 94)), its shadow 2 px, the large map open; lifted: back where it was, the large map still open; pressed again it goes down the same and the large map closes. JUMP under a finger, read the same way: 2 px down and right, its shadow 4 px to 2 px — the same move. The stick held by one finger and the round map under a second: pressed, the stick still held; the fingers lifted, each by its own event (the stick's, the round map's), and the mark gone. A touch the system cancels: the mark gone. A page without touch, 1280 × 720: the mouse held down on the round map shows it pressed (`:active`, no mark), released it is back; a click opens the large map and the next closes it. PT's build besides: 727 × 390 the same (68 px at (559, 140), pressed at (561, 142)); the switch's rules as they were (b1105iy's probe: the stick and the buttons under the large map, PAUSE, the ending cameras, command mode). Online, two pages on a test copy of 9.8's server, PT's build and 9.8's: the large map opened, the stick held under it — the unit moved 37.8 units on A and as B sees it (9.8's build: 41.2) — and closed by the round map; no page error, no server error. One thing was not driven: one finger lifted while the stick stays held — this page's touches lift only all at once; each finger's lift is its own event, as the two seen. `client/src/main.js`, `client/src/style.css`.

**A waiting bot keeps its search while it paces** (b1105ja, 2026-11-16, PT and 9.8, online and offline; user, of the 2v2 Sudden Death bots that stood with no plan, the second of three ways laid before them: "Do 乙") — a Sudden Death bot's route search that does not end inside its slice of time is parked and taken up at the bot's next tick, as long as its key stays the same; and the key carried the square the body stood in. A bot that waits in cover paces (THE WAIT): a step on and its square is another, so its parked search was thrown away and begun again, at every step and without end. On the page each of those calls took the frame's one search (`searchSlot`), and the bot after it in the order stood with no plan — the standing bots of the user's 2v2. A waiting bot's key is now the square its wait began in (`plan.cx`, `plan.cz`), which the pacing does not move, so the search goes on to its end and the frame's search comes free for the others. The route it finds begins where the search began, a few steps of the pacing from the body: the body walks to its first square as to any waypoint, the route priced from where the body stands; a wall met on that leg is the route follower's own case (the slide, or the route dropped and searched again). A search that ends inside its slice never meets its key, so nothing changes where there is time. Checked: 221 tests in both trees, one of them new (`wait_search.test.js`: on Town, every search cut after the same few checks on any machine, a waiting bot's parked search is never thrown away for another square and one that has lived across a step of the pacing goes on to its end; the code before threw 27 away and ended none), the frozen references unchanged. The trial, 12 matches of 120 s on Town and Compound, the player's unit standing and its teammate down, a 1 ms slice and the page's fixed order: parked searches thrown away 4,201 before and 28 after, searches ended 246 and 1,490, a bot with no plan for 20 s or more in 5 matches before and in none after (for 5 s or more: in none). The same with the playtest tree's tank that never empties: 4,276 and 39, 204 and 1,520, 4 matches and none (the longest stretch left is 5.8 s, the bot's own search running). The 45 routes taken from a wait began 1.9 units from the body in the middle and 5.4 at the most, the straight leg to their first square fitting the body in every one, none dropped at a wall. With no slice, 12 matches on six maps played the same to the last knockout. `shared/src/sim/sdbrain.js`, `shared/test/wait_search.test.js`.

**The light count held** (b1105jb, 2026-11-16, PT and 9.8, online and offline; user, told that a knocked-out unit takes its light out of the picture and that every shader is then made again: "keep the light count constant") — each unit carries a small light of its own (the boost's glow on the floor, dark but while the unit boosts), and the renderer writes the number of lights in the picture into every shader: one light more or fewer and every lit material is compiled anew in the middle of a frame, the map's own at once and each new round's, trail's and mark's after them. A knocked-out unit is hidden whole, its light with it. Now a dark light stands in for every unit's light the picture has lost (`holdUnitLights`, before every drawing of the scene): a point light of no colour and no strength, which lights nothing and is counted like any other. The count is the number of the units on the field from the loading screen's warm frame, where the match's shaders are made, to the match's last frame, whoever falls. The cost: after a knockout the map is still lit by the starting number of lights, the frame's work what it was before the knockout, where it used to get a little lighter. A match of another size has another count (1v1: 2, 2v2: 4), its shaders made under its own loading screen. Checked in the built page (a software renderer, 480 x 270), offline and online, 221 tests in both trees. One enemy's HP set to 0 in a 2v2 on Town, then the teammate's: before, 4 lights to 3 to 2, seven shaders made at each (the map's four lit materials and three unlit ones) with a long task of 0.45 s to 0.59 s; now 4 lights throughout, one dark light shown and then two, no shader made and no long task. A 2v2 Sudden Death of four bots to its end: before, five to eight shaders at each of the first two knockouts with a long task of 0.8 s to 0.9 s, and 33 programs at the end in 9.8, 41 in the playtest tree; now 26 from the fifth second of play to the end in both, the ending cameras included. Online, two pages in one room and the knockouts staged by a test copy of the server as real hits leave them: before, six to eight shaders on each page at each knockout and long tasks of 1.1 s to 3.7 s; now none of either. A Trio seat knocked out three times: 4 lights while its roster lasts and after it is spent (before: 3 then, and seven shaders). A 1v1's end, the command view, the Shooting Range (the player's light alone, no stand-in for the sign dummies) and a 1v1 started after a 2v2 with a unit down (no stand-in carried over) read as they should, with no page errors. What a match still compiles in play is its see-through materials in the first seconds (five on Town), none of it from the light count. `client/src/main.js`.

**The plain bots' band: 15 either side of the lock range** (b1105jc, 2026-11-17, PT and 9.8, online and offline; user: "change the lockrange band in normal brain from +-7 to +-15. SD brain should be uneffected if i'm not misunderstood. PT and 9.8") — a plain bot keeps its target inside a band round its lock range: inside it the bot fights where it stands (Engage, the orbit with its pull to the band's middle), nearer than it the bot backs off and farther it closes in (Pursue), and a route ends at a firing spot inside it (Maze). The band reached 7 either side of its middle and reaches 15 (`BOT_BAND_HALF_WIDTH`, nbrain.js): the assault rifles' is 95–125 where it was 103–117, the shotguns' 65–95, Aru's and Hiyori's 185–215 (the list under Preferred engage distance). Its middle is as it was — the lock range; 160 at the least against an enemy shotgun, 145–175 now; 30 nearer under the FORWARD call — and so is everything else that reads a lock range. The firing-position search's reach, 2.5 times the band's upper edge, is 20 longer with it. Measured before the build on 80 matches of four plain bots (2v2 Health Point, eight maps, ten seeds a map, the same seeds either way), at 7 and then at 15: a bot's time in Engage 6.3% and 11.3%, in the band with sight of its target 7.8% and 12.5%; backing off 4.9% of its time and 3.5%; out of Engage for the range's sake 4.7 times a bot-minute and 3.6; pressed to a wall while in Pursue 12.2 ticks a bot-minute and 9.7. The fights are no shorter, a little longer: damage dealt a bot-minute 232 and 225, the bots' time alive in all 4.5% more. The searches' nodes a bot-minute 67,000 and 60,100; under the server's budget (2 ms a tick for the searches; 24 matches, each run twice) the brains' time a tick is as it was — median 0.1 ms, the 99th percentile 2.3 to 2.4 ms either way, the ticks over 2 ms 1.9 to 2.0% and 2.3%. **The Sudden Death brain reads no band** and plays as before: its own reach is all it has, and under it the plain brain's states stand parked. Its seven reference matches part from the old references in one value alone, the plain brain's clock for its Maze trigger, which is written on the fighter off the band's upper edge and never read under the Sudden Death brain; with that value left out they play equal to the last tick, and 64 Sudden Death matches of four bots on eight maps came out the same to the last record at 7 and at 15. The bots' frozen references are re-frozen at b1105jc. `shared/src/sim/nbrain.js`, `shared/test/band_width.test.js`, `shared/test/nb_equiv.test.js`, `shared/test/sdbrain_equiv.test.js`.

**The band's width is one number** (b1105jd, 2026-11-17, PT and 9.8; user: "i might want to change the normal brain lock range band to +-25 in the future. make sure it won't take long when i ask to implement that to PT and 9.8") — `BOT_BAND_HALF_WIDTH` moved from the brain to `shared/src/sim/constants.js`, where the bots' frozen references read it as the live brain does: a new width parts no reference match and asks for no re-freeze. The band's test lays its distances off the number and pins it once; the comments name it instead of repeating it; the travel sprint latch's first test runs clear of the band as its second does (at a band of 25 its 3 s pursuit reached the band's edge, and the leg that the arrival cut short read as a flicker). **To change the width:** that number, its pin in `shared/test/band_width.test.js`, the build tag, and this README's list of bands under Preferred engage distance. Play is as it was: the bots' reference matches played equal to the b1105jc references after the change, and they are re-frozen at b1105jd so that the frozen brain reads the number where it now lives. **The width 25 was tried ahead**, on scratch copies of both trees with that number and its pin changed and nothing else: the 224 tests pass, and 64 Sudden Death matches of four bots came out the same to the last record. On the 80 matches of b1105jc's trial, at 15 and then at 25: a bot's time in Engage 11.3% and 14.2%, in the band with sight of its target 12.5% and 17.3%; backing off 3.5% and 3.0%; out of Engage for the range's sake 3.6 times a bot-minute and 2.8; damage dealt a bot-minute 225 and 228, the bots' time alive in all 1.6% less. Pressed to a wall as much in all (54.6 ticks a bot-minute and 57.7): less of it in Pursue (9.7 and 9.2), more of it in Maze (9.8 and 17.8, on five of the eight maps). The searches' nodes a bot-minute 60,100 and 56,800; under the server's budget the brains' time a tick is the same within the runs' spread at 7, 15 and 25 (median 0.1 ms, the 99th percentile 2.2 to 2.4 ms, the ticks over 4 ms 0.3 to 0.45%). `shared/src/sim/constants.js`, `shared/src/sim/nbrain.js`, `shared/test/band_width.test.js`, `shared/test/travel_sprint.test.js`.

**Marina's bot gate on the 20%-hit line, Hina's own** (b1105je, 2026-11-17, PT and 9.8, online and offline; user: "change marina's firing gating from 33% to 20%. That should make her gating same with Hina if i'm not misunderstood.") — unit13 gets `botGateWidth: 11.42` in both unit tables (the sim's, online; the client's, offline): her bot fires while her current cone still lands one round in five on a standing target, the line Hina has had since b1105ga, where the autos' 8.84 line she rode until now asks one in three. The line is the same one; what it does to her is not what it does to Hina, because her cone stops at 0.11 where Hina's goes to 0.16: at that cap her line stands at 103.8, past her 100 lock, so inside it she never holds for the cone — the 71-round drum goes in one pull of 3.4 s (7.3 s on the 33% line, in bursts of thirty with 2.0 s holds: that line passed her at 100 once the cone reached 0.088) — and she holds only beyond 103.8, the outer part of her band (it runs to 115). Hina at her 120 lock still fires bursts of thirty-two and holds 1.8 s. Measured on the hit-rate field at her 100 lock (200 runs a series): her hit rate a round falls on a standing and a walking target, 54 / 47 (83 / 67), the later rounds flying from the capped cone, and rises on a sprinter, 30 (24). Over 120 s with the trigger held and the 2 s reloads her long-run damage is where it was on a standing target (16.1 a second; 15.9), a tenth higher on a walking one (14.0; 12.8) and more than double on a sprinter (10.5; 4.7 — a gated burst's tight cones pass behind a sprinter); a standing 100 HP target goes down later (5.5 s where it took 4.3: the gate's first thirty rounds all but landed), a walking one sooner (6.0 s; 6.9) and a sprinting one much sooner (8.1 s; 19.7). Tests: bloom.test.js — her line (the width, Hina's; the capped cone's line past her lock; no hold at 100 or 103, the hold at 105), and the recovery-hold test moved onto her 20% line (its numbers were the autos' 33% line's); 225 pass. The other rows of the hit-rate table re-ran within 2 points of the table; her row and her panel are new.

**The stick takes the thumb that is there** (b1105jf, 2026-11-17, PT then 9.8 the same day, online and offline; user, of the stick on a phone: "i feel i was holding it but there was no input so i have to retap to hold the joystick button", then, of the four remedies offered: "甲乙丙丁一起做", and "port the joystick changes to 9.8") — the stick took one touch alone, the one that went down on its ring, and was dead to a thumb that was down by any other way. Measured before, with real touches on a phone's screen (844 × 390): a thumb put down where the stick will be before it is on the screen — the loading screen, GET READY, START: the HUD is away for the intro's 3.8 s — gave nothing after START, held or moved, until it was lifted and put down again; a thumb that had slid out of the ring (the stick follows a held thumb anywhere, and full push is 46 px out, the ring's edge 70) and lost the glass for 40 ms came back as a touch on the empty screen; a second touch inside the ring, the heel of the thumb, took the stick over and let it go as it lifted, the first thumb still down; and two quick taps beside the ring, a re-tap that missed, switched to Free pov. Now (`client/src/main.js`, THE STICK'S TOUCHES) every touch that is down is kept by its pointer, and the stick reads that list. **Its zone** is the ring and a collar round it, twice the ring's radius about its centre (`JOY_ZONE_SCALE`: a 140 px ring's zone reaches 140 px out, 70 past its edge; a 120 px ring's 120 and 60): a touch that goes down there on the empty screen or the ring is the stick's — a control inside the zone keeps its own (the tactic button on a small screen) — and one that goes down outside it never becomes the stick's, slide where it may. **One touch drives it**, the first of its touches down: a second waits and takes nothing; when the driver lifts, the oldest still down takes the stick on, the sprint as it was held. **A thumb down before the stick is there** takes it as the intro's gate comes off the HUD, where it stands, with no move asked of it. **A tap in the zone is no tap on the screen**: Free pov's swipe and its two taps leave the stick's touches alone. A mouse or a pen is as before, on the ring alone. A release the browser never sent would now hold the stick for good, so the list is checked against the browser's own count of touches at every touch going down, lifting or cancelled, and cut back oldest first. Event work only, nothing per frame, the same inputs sent online. A touch the system cancels (an edge swipe, a call) still lets the stick go: the page has no say there. The landing plant — a held stick ignored for 0.6 s after a landing on a lid at another level — is as it was. **Checked**, headless, real touch events, on PT's build and again on 9.8's. 844 × 390 and 597 × 320, thirteen scenes each: the ring as ever; the thumb 110 px out, the glass lost 40 ms and back: taken; the same 20 px past the zone: not; the heel of the thumb in the ring and off: the knob and the walk stay with the first thumb, which still steers; the first of two fingers lifted: the second takes the stick on, and both lifted lets it go; a system cancel lets go; a touch 5 px outside the ring starts the stick and one 6 px outside the zone does not; ten pairs of quick taps beside the ring never switch the pov, and of ten pairs far from the stick eight to ten switch it, as ever (a pair that lands more than 260 ms apart in the slow headless page is no pair: one pair alone can miss by time, so ten were sent); each right-hand button shows pressed under a second finger, the stick held; a Free pov swipe across the zone turns the view and starts nothing; a touch pointer made by hand with no release drives the stick until the next real touch, which drops it. A thumb down in the intro, and one down on the loading screen, held perfectly still: the knob out and the unit walking as START leaves; one down far from the stick: nothing. A 2v2 on 597 × 320 and 568 × 280, the tactic button 7 and 47 px inside the zone: its touch opens its pop-up and starts no stick. A desktop page: the mouse on the ring walks the unit, 30 px outside it nothing, the D key as ever. Online, two pages on a test copy of 9.8's server, a 1v1: the thumb down in the intro on A and still through START — A's unit walks on its own screen and as B sees it (PT's build: 25.7 and 25.1 units in 2.5 s; 9.8's: 25.3 and 24.6), and stands once the thumb lifts; no page error, no server error. What a phone does past these events — its own gestures, its palm rejection — is not driven here.

**Hina's round takes 3.5, Maki's 4** (b1105jg, 2026-11-17, PT and 9.8, online and offline; user: "Change Hina damage to 3.5. Change Maki damage to 4. PT and 9.8.") — `damage` 3.5 for unit5 (4 until now) and 4 for unit21 (4.5 from b1105dt, 5 before), in both unit tables: the sim's (`shared/src/sim/constants.js`, the server's, online) and the client's (`client/src/main.js`, offline). Nothing else of either gun is touched, and the picker's card reads its Dmg off the table. With every round landing: Hina needs 29 rounds for a 100 HP unit where she needed 25 (1.34 s of her 48 ms beat; 1.15 s), 35 for Kurumi's 120 (30); her burst is 72.9 a second (83.3), 43.2 with the reload counted (49.4), and a drum carries 525 (600). Maki needs 25 rounds (23; 3.07 s of her 128 ms beat, 2.82 s), 30 for Kurumi (27); her burst is 31.3 a second (35.2), level with Kurumi's, 22.6 with the reload (25.5), and a magazine carries 400 (450) — she no longer fires Koyuki's 4.5 bullet. Measured on the hit-rate field at their 120 lock (the shooter strafing, the trigger held 30 s with the 5 s reloads, 100 runs a series; before, then after): the damage dealt in the 30 s falls by the bullet's own share, an eighth for Hina and a ninth for Maki — Hina's bot, firing by her 20% gate, 769 and 676 on a standing target, 721 and 632 on a walking one, 362 and 321 on a sprinter; Maki 539 and 480, 505 and 450, 339 and 302. The time to the first 100 damage grows by more than that for a held trigger of Hina's: her bot 3.4 s and 3.6 on a standing target, 3.5 and 3.7 on a walking one, 7.1 and 7.6 on a sprinter; a trigger held with no gate, a player's, 1.9 s and 2.7, 2.2 and 3.2, 4.8 and 5.9 — the four more rounds she needs leave a cone already open (at 120 her first nine rounds are the sure ones on a standing target). Maki's: 3.8 s and 4.1, 4.3 and 4.6, 8.0 and 8.6. The tables below carry the new numbers (Weapons; Theoretical DPS, where Maki's row now stands beside Kurumi's). A new test (`shared/test/sim.test.js`) holds the trigger on a standing target in the sim and reads the bullets off its HP hit by hit: 3.5 a hit and 29 hits to the kill, 4 a hit and 25.

**Aris's bolt takes 9** (b1105jo, 2026-11-17, PT and 9.8, online and offline; user: "Change Aris damage from 12 to 9. PT and 9.8(online and offline). Also update the readme weapon stats.") — `damage` 9 for unit7 (12 from the user's tune of 2026-08-06, 15 before it), in both unit tables: the sim's (`shared/src/sim/constants.js`, the server's, online) and the client's (`client/src/main.js`, offline). Nothing else of her gun is touched, and the picker's card reads its Dmg off the table. With every bolt landing she needs 12 bolts for a 100 HP unit where she needed 9 — eleven leave it at 1 — and 14 for Kurumi's 120 (10); a full magazine of 8 carries 72 (96). The kill takes longer than three more bolts of her 336 ms beat would cost, because of how her rounds come back: a held trigger sends ten bolts 336 ms apart, the magazine's 8 and the 2 rounds that come back while it empties (one every 1.2 s from the first shot), and after those ten each bolt waits for its round. At 12 the killing bolt was the 9th, inside that run, 2.69 s after the first; at 9 the ten make 90, the 11th leaves at 3.62 s and the 12th, the killing one, at 4.82 s. Kurumi: the 10th bolt at 3.02 s until now, the 14th at 7.22 s. Her burst is 26.8 a second (35.7) and her long run, one bolt every 1.2 s, 7.5 (10.0), still the lowest sustained figure in the game. Measured on the hit-rate field at her 135 lock (the shooter strafing, the trigger held 30 s, 100 runs a series; before, then after): the damage dealt in the 30 s falls by the bolt's own share, a quarter — her bot, which fires by her sure-hit gate, 384 and 288 on a standing target, 358 and 269 on a walking one, 216 and 157 on a sprinter; a trigger held with no gate, a player's, 295 and 225, 287 and 216, 190 and 140. The time to the first 100 damage, the middle run of each series: her bot 4.9 s and 6.8 on a standing target, 5.6 and 7.4 on a walking one, 9.7 and 15.7 on a sprinter; the held trigger 9.7 s and 13.3, 10.9 and 13.3, 14.5 and 20.5 — at 135 a held trigger's cone is open after the first bolts, which is why the gated bot is the faster of the two. The tables below carry the new numbers (Weapons; Theoretical DPS, where her row now stands between Fubuki's and Kei's, and the note under it). A new test (`shared/test/sim.test.js`) holds the trigger on a standing target in the sim and reads the bolts off its HP hit by hit — 9 a hit, 12 hits to the kill, the last one taking 1 — and reads when the first twelve bolts leave: ten 336 ms apart, the 11th 3616 ms after the first, the 12th 4816.

**Town: the strip at the east deck's edge filled** (b1105jp, 2026-11-18, PT and 9.8, online and offline; user, with the readout "town  x 66.79  z 31.99  feet 0.00 m (model)  cam E 89°   [arena x 269.1 y 2.45 z 168.0]   build b1105jo 11-17": "Fix this pit. PT and 9.8. Don't take too long.") — between the raised deck's east edge (model x 66.5, its floor 3.88 m up) and the map's east edge (model x 67) a strip half a metre wide had no floor from model z 22 to 36: south of z 22 the strip is filled (a box from under the street to the deck's height and a floor on it), north of it it was not. A body that walked off the deck's edge there fell 3.88 m to the street into a slot narrower than itself, and neither a walk nor a jump took it out. The strip is now filled there as it is south of it: one box, arena x 268..270, z 128..184, from 2 under the street to the deck's 15.51, and one floor on it (`client/public/maps/town_data.json`: 7,710 boxes, 8,336 floors; nothing moved or removed). The route grid file is rebuilt. Checked on the sim: a body walked east off the deck at 29 places along the edge (arena z 128 to 184, every 2) fell into the slot at 21 before, and none got out; now none falls, and every one walks back west; checked in the built page as well, on the reported spot.

**Aris's plain body is no longer drawn** (b1105jq, 2026-11-18, PT and 9.8, the page's drawing alone, so online and offline alike; user, with a picture of the winner camera on her and two rings round thin pale slivers at her shoulders: "Aris winner motion seems to have a bit flickering/glitching issue on her back."; then: "For the time being, just focus on Aris winner motion first. No need investigate other units.") — her file holds two bodies, the plain one (`CH0334_Body`) and the armoured one (`CH0334_Arms_Body`), and each carries one morph that gathers every vertex of it at a single point, the file's way of putting a body away. All eight of her clips hold the plain body's morph at 1 and the armoured one's at 0: the plain body is never meant to be seen in this game. It was still drawn. Gathered at one point and then skinned, its vertices hang on 73 bones, so each bone carried its share of that point somewhere else, and the triangles between them became long thin slivers out of her chest and shoulders — in every motion of hers, plainest in the winner camera's close view. Measured in the built page at one frozen frame of her ending loop (`Normal_Idle`, 0.77 s): 166 of the plain body's 3,900 triangles had an edge over 8 % of the model's size, the longest 21 %; with the fix, none of the triangles drawn has. The fix is a list on her model's entry (`away`, `UNIT_MODELS.aris`, `client/src/main.js`) naming the plain body's two meshes: `makeUnitModel` hides them, gives them no see-through outline, and the loading screen's warm frame leaves them alone, so they never go to the graphics card. They stay in the model, hidden, because her clips' morph tracks name them. She is drawn with 21 meshes where she was drawn with 23, and her own outline with 9 where it had 11. No other unit's model is touched, and nothing of the sim or the server.

**Minori walks and sprints with Subaru's motion** (b1105jr, 2026-11-18, PT and 9.8, the page's drawing alone, so online and offline alike; user: "Apply Subaru's walk/sprint motion to Minori's walk/sprint.") — Subaru walks and sprints on one clip, her `Move_Ing`, at half speed for the walk and at its own speed for the sprint. Minori now does the same on the same clip at the same two speeds. The clip is written out of Subaru's file by `shared/tools/anim/clip_extract.mjs` as `client/public/models/subaru_move_clip.json` (174 KB; one cycle is 0.667 s and two steps) and borrowed by Minori's `walk`, `sprint` and `sprintShoot` entries (`UNIT_MODELS.minori`, `client/src/main.js`; the sprint with the trigger held shows the sprint clip for every unit, so it goes with the sprint). Until now those three played her own `Move_Ing`, which is Saori's (b1105dn): an upright run, 0.6 s a cycle, the gun held across the body. Two things are new in `borrowClip` for this. First, an entry may ask for the clip *whole* (`whole: true`). The borrowed dodges and jumps take the Biped body's rotations alone and leave the gun to the unit's own clip. A run taken that way stood wrong on her: the body ran at its standing height, and the gun hung at her hip while her hands held Subaru's grip, because the weapon bone hangs on the root bone and not on a hand. Minori's Biped has Subaru's proportions (the rest offsets of the spine, the arms and the legs are the same in the two files), so with `whole` the Biped's positions are taken with its rotations (the root's rise, lean and sway) and the weapon bone's with them. Her own `Move_Ing` still moves everything else: her eyes, the helper bones of her arms, and the scale of her gun's bone, which rests at a thousandth and is scaled up by every clip of hers. Second, entries of one rig that borrow the same file on the same base share one clip and so one action: a walk into a sprint keeps its step and is only retimed, as a unit's own `Move_Ing` does under those three keys. Checked in the built page: pose by pose her run now stands as Subaru's does, crouched, the gun low in both hands; played by her bot she shows the sprint at the clip's own speed and the walk at half of it, both on the borrowed clip; the footsteps are read off it as two steps a cycle; her gun stays at full size. Her stand, shot, reload, jump, dodge, death and climb are untouched, and so is every other unit.

**The teammate's messages: four quick messages between two players on one side; BACK OFF is now PULL BACK** (b1105js, 2026-11-18, 9.8 alone, online; user: "I want to add a online 2V2 feature. 1. When both player are human player, the BOT command button still shows. 2. When tapping it, instead of "Forward/Back off" options for BOT, it pops up four options. "I'll go forward", "I'll back off", "Please go forward", "Please back off". 3. After the player chooses the message, their teammate will see the pop up message on their screen. The message will shows up for 2sec. 4. The cooldown for sending the message is 3sec. 5. No other effect is needed; it's just a simple communication tool. 6. No more lag should be caused to online games by this feature."; then, on the open points: the kill feed's place, and "The sender is notified as well. But do make sure player won't be confused identifying the message of being sent by theirselves or receive from others."; "mouse click for now"; of Sudden Death, "yes, 2v2 online SD only"; of a teammate whose unit is down, "No. BTW, if the teammate is still alive and the human player left, the button changes its function to normal BOT command version."; 'Yes, change existing "Back Off" to "Pull Back".'; and "no need this on PT. I'll verify it on 9.8") — Until now the ally tactic's button was hidden beside a player (b1105iq). Now, online, in a 2v2 with a player in the seat beside one's own, it brings up four messages: I'LL GO FORWARD, I'LL PULL BACK, PLEASE GO FORWARD, PLEASE PULL BACK. **What a message is.** One small packet from the sender to the server (`ally:msg`, the message's kind alone) and one from the server to each of two pages: the teammate's, and the sender's own as its receipt. It is not in the sim, not in a snapshot and not in a tick, and no unit does anything for it, so the match's own traffic and the server's work per tick are what they were. **The rule** is one pure function shared by the server and tested by itself (`shared/src/sim/allymsg.js`, `allyMsgTo`; ten tests in `shared/test/allymsg.test.js`): a 2v2; the sender a seated player not in command mode; the seat beside it a player's now, its unit standing; one of the four kinds; and 3 s since the sender's last message. The page holds its sender to the 3 s; the server holds it to 250 ms less, because two messages a page sent 3 s apart can reach the server a little nearer each other, and a message its page took as sent is not dropped for that. A message that does not stand is answered by nothing. **The button** (`allyTacticVersion`, `client/src/main.js`) is read every frame off the last snapshot: the two calls beside a bot, as before; the four messages beside a player; nothing while the teammate's unit is down. In Sudden Death it shows for the messages alone. A teammate who leaves in mid-match leaves a bot, and the button is the two calls again from the next snapshot. A spectator is shown no messages. The four are the calls' pills: a column where the calls stand in a column, and two by two under PAUSE on a small wide screen, where the lower two lie over the inner ends of the chips' second row for the three seconds they are up. **What the two pages show** (`updateAllyMsgHud`; `style.css` `.amsg`): a line at the kill feed's place for 2 s from its arrival. The teammate's is a paper sticker at the feed's size with the sender's face in its seat's ring, the word ALLY and the UI's sound. One's own receipt is smaller, dark and see-through, with one's own face, the word YOU and no sound, and it shows only once the server has passed the message on. One line at a time; with knockouts in the feed the line stands under them where they stand one under the other, and left of them where they stand side by side. **The mouse and the keys.** A message is picked with a tap or a click ("mouse click for now"). `E` brings the four up as it brings up the calls; in free pov the canvas holds the mouse, so that press gives the mouse back, and the next click on the field takes it again. `W` and `S` pick a call, never a message. **BACK OFF is PULL BACK**: on the bot's pill, in the Guide and in this README's current chapters; the dated entries keep the name they were written with. The call itself, the Hide stance, is unchanged. The Guide's Tips gain one line on the messages. **Checked** with the repo's own server and two pages in one room on one side of a 2v2, each page keeping its own log on its own clock: the four come up; a pick sends, puts them away and dims the button; inside the 3 s the button does not open and a second message sent straight at the server is dropped; after it the button opens and the next message goes; each message reaches each of the two pages once; the teammate's page shows it as ALLY's and the sender's as YOU's, each for 2 s to the frame, both ways round; a bot's call to a player's seat is still refused; the teammate leaves and the button is FORWARD and PULL BACK, PULL BACK is taken by the bot and no message goes to it; in Sudden Death a message goes through, and with the teammate gone there is no button. 235 tests pass (225 and the ten new ones). The PT site does not have this, nor the new name.

**Minori's left fingers are put away while she walks and sprints** (b1105jt, 2026-11-19, PT and 9.8, the page's drawing alone, so online and offline alike; user: "When Minori is in walk/sprint motion (where she just got from Subaru motion), her left hand finger is overlapping/merging with her gun. Can you address this by temporary removing her left hand (not arms) or smth? smth that won't cause any more lag to online games."; then, of the two ways shown, the fingers alone or the whole hand: "做法 A：只收起手指") — Since b1105jr Minori walks and sprints on Subaru's clip. Subaru's hand has five fingers' bones and Minori's has three, and Subaru holds an HK33 where Minori holds an AKM, so Subaru's grip on Minori's hand put her left fingers through the handguard: pale fragments on the gun's far side through the whole cycle. Her own clip's fingers go through it as well, and fingers held open stand out under the gun; both were tried. Now, for as long as that clip plays, the three finger bones of her left hand are held at a thousandth of their size, so the fingers shrink to a point at their joints and the palm stays under the handguard, which is what shows from her left. It is an entry's `tuck` (`UNIT_MODELS.minori`, the list `MINORI_RUN_TUCK`, `client/src/main.js`): `borrowClip` adds one track of one key for each named bone the rig has. The clip has 58 tracks where it had 55, and nothing else is added to a frame: no mesh, no draw, nothing on the line or the server. The mixer gives the bones their size back when the clip is no longer played, so her stand, shot, reload, jump and dodge show her hand whole, and across a crossfade the fingers go between the two sizes in its 0.12 s. Checked in the built page: close up at four moments of the cycle the handguard is clear from its far side and the palm shows from her left; played by her bot, the left fingers stand at a thousandth through walk, sprint and sprint-and-fire and at their own size in her reload, and a right finger at its own size throughout. Her left hand in that clip has no fingers when seen from very near. No other unit is touched.

**The LAST tag no longer stretches on a tapped card** (b1105ju, 2026-11-19, PT and 9.8, online, the page's style sheet alone; user, with a picture of a LAST 3 tag as tall as Kurumi's card: "In online mode reselect screen, the "last X" tag will get incorrectly extended while the unit card is tapped. Fix this without making any more lag to online games."; then: "The "Last" tag in online mode map selection also got incorrectly extended as well.") — In the pickers of a RESELECT the card picked for the last match wears a LAST tag (b1105is), and a tapped card wears a PICK! tag under it. Both tags were the card's `::after`: one box. On a tapped LAST card the two rules met in it, and the box took LAST's words, colour and top with PICK!'s bottom, so it stood from over the card's top to under its foot, and PICK! was gone. A map's card is a unit's card by class, so the map picker's LAST tag did the same. The LAST tag is now the card's `::before` (`client/src/style.css`, `.menu .unit-card.last-pick::before`); nothing else is changed, and no script runs for it. A tapped LAST card wears both tags. Checked on the repo's own server with one page as the host, the page told what the last match was as a RESELECT leaves it, in the unit picker of a Trio and in the map picker; the two boxes of the card measured before and after a tap. Before: the tapped card's one box read LAST 3 and was 104 px tall on a 107 px card, and on the map's card LAST, 102 px on 106. Now: LAST is 11 px tall tapped or not, and PICK! is there on the tapped card, in both pickers.

**A fourth battle track** (b1105jv, 2026-11-22, 9.9, the page alone, so online and offline alike; user, with an MP3 named "Blue Archive OST 24. Endless Carnival": "Add this BGM to one of those randomly picked for battle. There should be four of them now including this new one.") — The battle drew one of three tracks; it draws one of four, each as likely as the others. The file is `client/public/bgm/combat_4.mp3`, the upload as it came (3.4 MB, 142 s, an MP3 of 192 kb/s at 44.1 kHz, as the other ten are), and one line of the table in `client/src/sfx_data.js` gives it the battle's place. The player reads its places off that table, so the player itself is not changed. **Its level.** The other ten lines were written by the listening site's tool. That site has been put away (its page is kept in `Testing_0.9.0_Develop-Tools`, `shared/tools/sound-board/`, and does not know this track), so this one line is written by hand: by the site's own rule, and not by ear. The site brought every track to one loudness, a level of 0.1875 for a track of -11.5 LUFS, and the game plays the music at 0.8 of that (b1105il). ffmpeg's `ebur128` reads, for each of the ten, the loudness the site's page has for it, and the rule then gives each of the ten levels to within 0.0001. The new track reads -7.8 LUFS, the second loudest of the eleven: 0.1225 on the site's scale, 0.098 in the game, the second lowest level of the table. It plays in full sound to its last moment and starts again, as the third battle track does: there is no fade at its end. Checked on the built page in a headless browser. The battle entered 2,000 times drew the four tracks 493, 512, 487 and 508 times. With the draw steered to the new track, an offline match started with the main menu's GO!! fetched it (200), held it decoded beside the menu's track under the loading screen, and cut to it when the battle began, 3.2 s after the press: playing at 0.098, its round from 0.064 s to 142.245 s, the ending's track drawn ahead, no page error. `npm test`: 235 of 235. Not tried: a phone. Nobody has listened to it here, so whether it sits right beside the other three is for the ear to say. The build carries 33.6 MB of music where it carried 30.2. `client/public/bgm/combat_4.mp3`, `client/src/sfx_data.js`, `client/src/main.js` (the build tag).

**The disclaimer at the Guide's foot** (b1105jw, 2026-11-22, 9.9, the page alone, so online and offline alike; user, with the six points of their disclaimer: "Add the disclaimer at the bottom of in game Guide. Use small gray text; I don't want it to be too lengthy.") — The Guide ended with the command mode's list. Under it now stands the user's disclaimer: what the game is, whose the characters, models, pictures and music are, the nine modelled maps with their makers, where the sounds come from, how it was made, and removal on a rights holder's word. It is small print: 11 px in gray (`#6F6F7F`, 4.5 to 1 on the card's cream) where the Guide's own text is 14 px in ink, under a dashed line. **Short on the page, whole in what it says.** The words are the user's, compared word for word on the built page (268 words), but for two: the title reads Disclaimer where the text's read Full Disclaimer, and the tracks are counted off the music table, so the line says 11 where the text said ten (the eleventh came at b1105jv) and stays true when a track is added. A point's heading runs into its text. None of the text's twelve addresses is printed: the name each belongs to is the link, opened in a new tab so that the game's page stays — the wiki, the soundtrack's playlist, the licence, and each map's model, one map to a line. It adds 470 px to a Guide of 2,651 px on a card of full width (a desktop, a phone held wide), and 582 px to one of 3,519 px on a phone held tall. `client/src/main.js` (`showGuidePopup`, the build tag), `client/src/style.css` (`.guide-legal`). Checked on the built page in a headless browser: the words and the twelve addresses against the user's text; the nine maps' titles, makers and addresses against Sketchfab's own records, read on 2026-11-21; a touch on a link on a phone held wide and on one held tall, and a click on a desktop, each opening that address in a new tab with the game's page and its Guide still up; nothing of it past the card's edge, no scrolling sideways, no page error. `npm test`: 235 of 235. Not tried: a phone in the hand. **Seen in passing and left as it was:** on a phone held wide, a real touch on the Guide's button opens the Guide and its release closes it again. The release's click lands on the dark ground beside the card, and a click there closes the Guide; held tall it lands on the card, and a mouse's click lands on neither, so those two open it. The build before this one does the same. This was seen in Chromium's own touch handling, which is Chrome's on Android, and not on a phone; for the check above, the Guide of the phone held wide was opened by giving its button the press alone.

**The player's own steps, dodge, jump and landing sound online** (b1105jx, 2026-11-22, 9.9, online, the page alone; user: "footsteps/dodge sounds are missing in online games. investigate, check the jump/landing sounds as well. Fix without making online games any more lag.") — In an online match the player's own unit made none of its four movement sounds: no step, no dodge, no jump, no landing. Its shots, reloads and hits sounded as they should. **The cause.** The four are played by one function (`updateMoveSfx`), which is silent unless a match is being played, and it read that off the page's phase: `'match'`. Offline that is a match's phase. Online the phase is `'online'` from the room to the end page and never `'match'`, so the function was silent in every online match since the sounds came in (b1105hk), for the player of a unit and for a room's spectator alike. The checks of b1105hk, b1105id and b1105if ran these four offline only. **The fix.** Online the function reads the match's own word (`onlineMatchLive`: the server's match under way, past the game-start intro and before the ending camera) and stays silent under the loading screen; offline it reads the phase, as before. The rule that decides each sound is not touched, and is the same offline and online. **No more lag.** One comparison on a frame that ran this function already; nothing is sent, nothing is asked of the server, and the server is not changed. Measured in a 1v1 against the room's bot on the repo's own server, the same keys pressed each time (a walk, a sprint, two dodges, two jumps), the build before and this one in turn, twice over: before, none of the four sounded; now the steps, both dodges, both jumps and their landings. The page's socket carried the same before and after: 62.5 frames a second sent (19.9 to 20.0 kB/s), 62 received (181 to 183 kB/s). The frames were as long: a middle of 167 ms before and 133 after in one pair, 167 and 167 in the other, on a software renderer. With the way to the server made 120 ms longer each way: one dodge's sound a dodge, one jump's and one landing's a jump. Offline the same keys sound as they did, the build before and this one alike. `npm test`: 235 of 235. Not run: a room's spectator; a phone. **Seen on the test's slow browser** (7 frames a second), in 3 of 14 online runs and in one more with the longer way: a jump's sound with no jump pressed, where a dodge or a sprint took the unit up onto something low (Town's raised strip, a low object beside it) and off its far side between two frames. The rules do lift the unit there: one dodge run on the shared rules alone climbs 3.8 u onto the object and falls off it. The sound's rule — a leap is a jump when the body rises as it leaves the ground — reads those two frames as one rise. It is the rule the sounds have had offline since b1105hk, unchanged here; between frames close enough to show the unit standing on the thing before it falls (a dozen a second and more, for these two), it hears the landing alone. Offline a slow page slows the match itself, so its frames stay that close; online the match keeps the clock's time. `client/src/main.js` (`updateMoveSfx`, the build tag).

**Dust's kerb before the first home's yard is walked up** (b1105jz, 2026-11-22, PT and 9.9, online and offline; user, standing at model x 61.88, z −42.11 with the collision view's boxes #1876 and #1877: "Add a little slope or smth at this gap so the units can walk up here instead of needing to jump. PT first."; then, having tried it there: "Apply to 9.9, online and offline.") — On Dust the street before the first home's raised yard ends at a kerb: two slabs, model x 57.15..64.38, 3.70 and 3.80 m high over a street at 3.03 m. That is 0.67 and 0.77 m where a body steps 0.65 m, so a unit walking at the kerb was held, 2 and 12 cm short, and got up by a jump alone. **The slope.** An unseen slope lies on the street along the kerb's whole length, half a metre deep, from the street's height at its outer edge to each slab's top at the kerb's face: two sloped floors in `client/public/maps/dust_data.json`, written by `shared/tools/maps/dust_kerb_slope.mjs`. The slabs, the street's floor and every box are as they were. Half a metre is the least that took everything up: at 0.37 m a dodge was held at half the places, and a low flat step let a body up but left the route grid's links here jumps. **The kerb's top reaches the yard.** The floors on the two slabs' tops ended 2 cm short of the yard's floors. A body crossing that slit was in the air for a few ticks and nothing more, but the route grid's walk test stands in it, so the grid had joined the kerb's top to the yard by a jump link and never by a walk, and a link inside one walked area needs a jump's rise. With the slope alone the kerb's top became part of the street's walked area, that link was no longer built, and the bots' route from the street into the yard went round by the east end, 48 to 80 units where it had been 8 to 16. That was the PT site's b1105jy, the same day, and it was found by reading the bots' routes while the change was brought here. The two floors now reach the yard's (their north edge from z −42.51 to −42.6 in the data) and the walk is unbroken: street, slope, kerb, yard. **Measured.** On the sim at 31 places along the kerb, a walk, a sprint, a dodge and a diagonal walk at each: before, none got up but 8 of the diagonal walks, which slid along the kerb to the slope past its east end; now 31 of 31 each, with no tick in the air on the way up. In the route grid the eighteen links at the kerb's three rows of nodes are eighteen walk edges and nothing else differs (2,876 nodes; 354 jump links, now 336), and the bots' route from the street to the yard runs straight over the kerb as before with no jump in it. A unit under a move order still hops there, as it did: the order's driver vaults at any waypoint more than 1.7 units over its floor (`command.js`, ROUTE JUMP), whatever lies between, and it is over the kerb within the same second. For the shots and the bots' sight, 9,450 pairs of a spot on the street and a spot in the yard were read by the sim's own rules: the locked shot's line, the bots' eye line, and the lines to a hit capsule's low shoulder and lowest point, both ways, are closed nowhere they were open; a unit on the slope stands higher, so 231 lines from the yard to its lowest point that the yard's edge had closed are open; lines lower than a body can be hit, at the feet, differ. In real matches on the built page the same walk was made in both modes (the unit starts in the yard at the map's first home, goes down to the street, and walks back up at three places): offline, and online on the repo's own server with a second connection sitting in the other seat. It came up at 0 of 3 before in both and at 3 of 3 now in both, online with the server's own place for the unit the page's at the end of each walk and no step back, and the server took the new grid file as the map's (`[warmup] grid dust: file`). The client and the server read the same two files, the data and its grid, so the two modes have the one kerb. The 235 tests pass. **What it costs.** The slope is not drawn, the model having none, so a unit standing within half a metre of the kerb stands over the paving it is seen on: 0.33 m where a body used to be held, 29 cm from the kerb, and up to the kerb's own height at its face. Online, at the test browser's ten frames a second, the page drew the unit up to 6 cm over the floor for about a second after a quick climb (the page's easing of its own prediction on a steep slope; the server's height was the floor's). A regeneration of Dust's data must run the tool again, and `navgrid_build.mjs` after it.

**Dust: the piles, crates, baskets and barrels are one box a thing, the columns that stood up out of them are gone, and the plaster wall's end is a wall** (b1105kf, 2026-11-22, the PT site's b1105ka to b1105kf and 9.9, online and offline; user, with the collision view's marks on two piles of stone blocks: "Replace the cboxes of these two cube clutches to one simple cube cbox for each. Shouldn't take too long. PT first."; with the readouts of five columns, #1171, #1168, #1181, #589 and #578: "These are some more un-needed high pillar cboxes. Remove or reshape them in PT."; "By the way, since you already at it, make those crates, boxes objects' cboxes significantly simplified, like what we just did previously; simple cubes cboxes will do, no need to be pixel accurate."; asked whether the map's other crates should go the same way: "okay"; of a trap the work had found: "Fix in PT."; with the readouts of a basket's and a barrel's boxes: "Simplify these two clusters as well"; then: "Apply Dust to 9.9, online and offline.") — The reading of Dust's model had made of each pile, crate, basket and barrel a heap of quarter-metre cells, many with a floor of their own on top, and had stood columns in among them from the ground to whatever hangs over the place — a deck, an awning's frame, a balcony, the passage's vault: walls in the air over the things, which a body and a shot met and the eye did not. **One box a thing.** At fifteen places 281 of those boxes are 34: the two piles of stone blocks on their pallets (`shared/tools/maps/dust_cube_piles.mjs`) and thirteen places of crates, baskets and barrels (`dust_crates.mjs`, which names each with its boxes), each thing one upright box of its own measure in the model with a floor on its top. A thing turned on the map, or round, has the upright rectangle that overlaps its outline best; a thing lying on another stands from the lower one's top; things that touch share a face; a slit narrower than a body between a thing and a wall, which the reading had filled, stays closed, and what was filled by hand on 2026-10-24 stays filled. The 39 columns, 4.6 to 14.5 m high, are gone with the cells (two of them stood in the slit between the crates and the plaster wall, which the crates' boxes close): what hangs over a place keeps no box where it has none elsewhere along its length, and the five under the passage's vault, over the barrels, are cut from below to the vault's underside (3.90 m, the model's mean over them) and stand above it as before. Where the crates' old boxes had hidden bare street the ground has a floor (three places), and one of b1105fb's flat plugs, which reached in under the barrels' old boxes and would have shown 12 and 20 cm past the new one, ends at the wall's face. **The plaster wall's end.** East of the two crates by the plaster wall (model x 72.1..74.5, z −32.8..−27.4) the wall stands 5.00 m and its north end, three cells, 4.50 to 4.70 m, each with a floor on its top and the buffer of a thing a body steps onto. A jump from the big crate beside it came onto the end, went along it and off its east side into the corner behind the wall, outside the map, which no jump leaves: on the sim's own tick 83 of 1,952 double jumps from the crates' tops ended there. The three cells are walls now, with neither floor nor buffer (`dust_wall_end.mjs`): 0 of 2,160, the feet no higher than 4.21 m. The wall's long top was reached over its end alone and is reached no more. **Measured**, the old data against the new, which is the PT site's byte for byte, its grid file too. 2,012 boxes are 1,765 and 1,590 floors 1,463. A body walked at every face of a new box that has free ground before it, 163 walks: 145 are held a body's radius from the face, 0.29 m; nine are at the wall's end, which stands behind the crates; four meet the next crate first; five are held 5 and 7 cm early by two more of b1105fb's plugs, whose edges are the old boxes' and which were left. A body put over each new top stands at its height, 164 of 164. Where a body can get to, read over the whole map in cells of 0.25 m from the first home: 39,459 cells before and 39,685 now, and every cell lost (177) or gained (403) lies at a changed place, on the plaster wall's top or in the corner behind it; that corner, 40 cells with no way back, is reached no more. The route grid has 2,911 nodes (2,876), 327 jump links (336) and 211 drop links (183), and no node, edge or link differs farther than 2.6 m from a changed place. For shots, 52,000 straight lines through the thirteen places of crates, baskets and barrels were asked of the model's faces and of the boxes: the boxes answer as the model for 96.0% of them (91.3%) and stop 3.8% in the air (8.4%); of 3,977 lines passing over the things' tops 594 were stopped, and now 65, all by the plaster wall's end, which the model has. In real matches on the built page one walk was made in both modes — from the first home down to the street and west to the four baskets, at the small basket's east face, up by a jump, along the baskets' tops and down — offline, and online on the repo's own server with a second connection sitting in the other seat: the unit was held at x 38.61, the box's face and a body's radius (the old data holds that walk at 38.94, by a cell beside the basket), came up by one jump, stood at 4.38 m on the small basket and at 4.67 m on the middle and the west one, came down from a jump straight up where it had stood, and stood on the street again at 3.03 m, seven checks of seven in both; online the server's own place for the unit was the page's at every stop, and the server took the grid file as the map's (`[warmup] grid dust: file`). The client and the server read the same two files, the data and its grid, so the two modes have the one map. The 235 tests pass. **What it costs.** A round thing in a box has corners: at the baskets the boxes answer as the model for 92.8% of the lines, where the cells did for 93.4%. Tops that columns had stood in can be stood on: more of the two piles' (0.90 and 0.77 m), and the barrels' (1.29 m) and the south-east corner crate's (2.60 m), which no body stood on before. The barrels stand under the vault's springing with the passage's arch south of them, and those have boxes of their own from 3.50 m: a jump straight up from the barrels' top, read at 248 places, is moved in one tick by nothing at 76 and by up to 0.29 m at the rest, and comes down off the top at two; a jump held south rises into the arch's boxes and is put out at their faces, up to 0.74 m in a tick, onto the passage's floor. A walk leaves the top every way but into the wall (the 0.25 m flood, whose step cannot, counts the top as a place with no way back; at 0.125 m it finds the way off). A regeneration of Dust's data must run the three tools again, in this order — `dust_cube_piles.mjs`, `dust_crates.mjs`, `dust_wall_end.mjs` — and `navgrid_build.mjs` after them.

**Neon: the scaffolds carried up to the skybridge, and the rail stub at the catwalk's joint taken out** (b1105kg, 2026-11-22, the PT site's b1105kg and 9.9, online and offline; user, with the collision view's mark on box #7208, model x 5.56..6.06, z 33.37..34.10, 8.75 to 11.25 m, and the panel outlined in a picture of the game: "Moving on to map \"Neon\". 1. remove this object physically and visually. 2. Use existing objects as materials to extend the current scaffolding structure. So the unit can walk seamlessly to the top catwalk. 3. From neon x -7.92 z 24.15 feet 12.59 m (model) ... to neon x -5.87 z 23.57 feet 21.24 m (model) ... 4. From neon x 16.01 z 31.64 feet 12.59 m (model) ... to neon x 8.53 z 57.10 feet 21.80 m (model) ... Apply in PT."; "Of course, with simple cboxes."; then: "Apply Neon to 9.9, online and offline.") — Each of Neon's two scaffolds ended at a bare platform 12.59 m over the street, and what stands over them — the tower top with pad B and the strip at the skybridge's north end (21.24 m), the bridge's floor (20.24 m) — was reached by the transporters alone. **Two ways up**, each built of the scaffold's own pieces, copied (`shared/tools/maps/glb_place.mjs`, new: an object copied to a place with a size, a turn and a move of its own; 12 copies, 24 objects, their meshes shared, so the model file grows by its node list alone, 4 kB): the ramp to the top platform (4 m square, a rise of 3.96 m), that platform, and a walkway piece. *The north way*, from the first scaffold's top platform to the strip, which stands 8.65 m over the platform's east edge: a stair of two flights side by side, south of the platform — a ramp up to the south (model x −10.31..−6.31, z 25.84..29.85, to 17.01 m), a landing of two platforms (x −10.31..−2.31, z 29.72..33.72), a ramp back up to the north (x −6.31..−2.31, z 25.71..29.72, to 21.44 m) and a platform lying on the strip (x −6.31..−2.31, z 22.84..25.84: three quarters of a piece deep, so that it ends south of the slanted panel that leans over the strip's north side). The two ramps are the piece made 12% taller: 47.9° for its 45°. *The south way*, from the second scaffold's top platform to the bridge's last east window, 26 m south and 9.3 m up: three ramps in a row to the south (x 13.43..17.43, z 33.85..45.87, each 79% of the piece's height: 37.7°), a walkway of three pieces at the height of the window's sill (21.88 m; z 45.75..57.75, passing 2 m over the ramen sign and ending 7 cm short of the south tower's corner), and one piece west to the bridge's side under the window (x 8.78..13.43, the piece made 16% longer). The design is one file, `shared/tools/maps/neon_ways_up.mjs`: it writes the copies' list for `glb_place.mjs` and makes the data's change. **Simple boxes**, the user's word: a ramp or a row of ramps is one sloped floor from the floor it leaves to the floor it reaches (three of them), a landing or a walkway one box 0.2 m thick with a floor on its top (four); no box stands under a ramp. One floor more, inside the bridge: under the last east window the bridge's floor ended a quarter metre short of the wall the sill stands on — a slit no body's centre comes into, but the route grid's walk test stood in it, found the street 20 m below, and joined the sill to the street and not to the bridge. The floor is carried to the wall there (model x 7.81..8.06, z 55.49..57.87), and the grid has the drop from the sill into the bridge. **The stub** was b1105bk's: a 0.735 m piece of the scaffold's own fence panel (Node_216_216_copy_stub / Node_217_217_copy_stub) set on the copied walkway's west edge at the catwalk's joint, to close the band south of the catwalk. It is out of the model (`glb_drop_nodes.mjs`) and of the data (its three boxes and the two floors on its top). **Measured**, the old data against the new, which is the PT site's byte for byte, its grid file too. 8,188 boxes are 8,189 and 3,719 floors 3,725. On the sim's own tick, with the stick alone: the north way from the user's start to the user's end in 6.9 s, 413 ticks, none in the air, the feet at 21.44 m — the way's platform lies on the strip there, 0.2 m over the user's 21.24 m, and a step north is the strip itself; the south way from the user's start onto the sill at the user's end in 10.8 s, 648 ticks, none in the air, the feet at 21.80 m, and through the window a body drops onto the bridge's floor (20.23 m). On the old data both walks end on the street. Going down, a body walks the south way without leaving its floor and comes off the north way's steeper ramps in short hops (46 of 413 ticks in the air), as off the scaffold's own ramp (29 of 143). Where a body gets to from the first scaffold's top with no transporter, read in cells of 0.25 m over model x −22..32, z 14..62: 41,145 places (a cell and the floor stood on) before and 51,969 now, no cell lost; no place higher than 19 m before, and 6,224 cells with one now — the two ways, the tower top and the strip, the bridge's floor, and with them what a body drops to from up there, the south building's roof among it, all of which the transporters reached before. That flood calls a ledge on the south building, half a metre deep at 11.50 m, a place with no way back; on the sim a body walks or jumps off it 12 to 14 ways of 16. The route grid has 20,927 nodes (20,744), 498 jump links (498) and 3,104 drop links (3,032), and no node, edge or link differs farther than 2.0 m from a changed place. Without the transporters' links there was no route to pad B or to pad D from any of the five spawns; now there is one on foot from each — to B up the north way (81 to 115 m, not a link on it), to D up the south way (90 to 111 m, the one link the drop from the sill into the bridge). With the links nine of those ten routes still take a pad (to B three by A and one by C, to D four by C and one by A) and one goes on foot, the west home's to B, 82 m. At the corner the stub stood on, 880 straight walks from the catwalk to the walkway south of it and 880 back: 651 and 616 fell off the deck with the stub there, 522 and 522 without it (most such lines cross the open side south of the catwalk whatever stands at the corner; the stub turned some that would have crossed it into the gap); a body walking due west along the band it closed was held at x 6.35 and now walks off the walkway's west edge onto the 5.78 m deck, 2.86 m down. On the PT site's page both ways were walked with the keyboard, none of it in the air. In real matches on the built page one walk was made in both modes — from the west home on foot by the route grid's own route to the first scaffold's deck, along the deck and up the scaffold's own ramp, up the north way to the user's end point, off the platform onto the strip and the tower top, east along the strip and down into the bridge, south through it, a jump from its floor up through the last east window, out along the walkway and down the three ramps to the user's start point, and back up them to the user's end point on the sill and in through the window — offline against a bot, and online on the repo's own server with a second connection sitting in the other seat: 14 stops of 14 at the wanted height in both, no reading in the air on the climbs, online the server's own place for the unit the page's at every stop, the server's grid the file (`[warmup] grid neon: file`). The client and the server read the same two files, the data and its grid, so the two modes have the one map. 238 tests pass: `shared/test/neon_ways.test.js` is new (the two walks on the sim, and a body standing where the stub stood), and two of `pads.test.js`'s asked what is no longer so — that every spawn reaches B and D "up through A or C, and no other way", and three seconds with no pad goal for a bot just moved onto B — and ask now for a route by a pad or up a way and one on foot without the links, and for no goal while the bot stands up there. **What it costs.** The ways have no railing, as the scaffold's own top platforms have none: a body steered off a side falls, up to 21.7 m (the sim has no fall damage). The tower top is no island now, and a bot there shows it. A bot backs off a target that is too near; the strip's south edge used to hold it (a step past the edge it stood over the street, the "vertical stack" rule stopped the retreat, and it turned back), and the way's platform and ramp now go on from that edge. So it backs on down the ramp, where it stands lower than its target and is "too near" no more, and the pursuit's descent hop (`findDescentDirection`, the rule that hops a pursuing bot off the deck it stands on by an open edge within reach — the ramp's east side is a metre from the line it backs along) takes it off, 12 m down onto the 8.63 m deck; it walks back up the way and does it again. Against a body standing still on the tower top 1.5 m north of pad B, the bot starting by pad A, 60 s, eight runs: before, the bot stayed up twelve to sixteen seconds a visit, went off the strip's south edge by a drop on its route and came back by the pad — it had sight of the body for 34.1 s, did 959 damage and left the top level twice; now it stays some three seconds a visit — sight for 14.9 s, 591 damage, and it leaves seven times. (Tried on a copy of the data and not shipped: thin side rails on the upper ramp and on the platform on the strip keep the bot up there the whole minute — sight for 51.6 s, 1,480 damage, no coming down. The model has no rail there, and none was asked for.) Against a body standing in the bridge nothing changes (damage 1,319 before and 1,310 now, sight 53.4 and 53.3 s). The bots' brain is as it was. Out of the bridge by the last east window is a jump: the sill is 1.57 m over the bridge's floor, a player's jump clears it, and the route grid's jump link does not reach that high, so the bots come in by the window and do not go out by it. In the command view the ways stay drawn, as the scaffold does (the bridge is one of the things that view clears). The map's card picture is as it was. A regeneration of Neon runs `neon_ways_up.mjs` again — on the model `spec` and `glb_place.mjs`, after the stub's two nodes are dropped; on the reading `data` — and `navgrid_build.mjs` after it (`shared/tools/maps/README.md`, the record of b1105kg).

**Neon: rails on the two ways, a little ramp out of the bridge, and an unseen slope at the bridge's north step** (b1105kj, 2026-11-22, the PT site's b1105kh, b1105ki and b1105kj and 9.9, online and offline; user, told what b1105kg cost — a bot backing off a target on the tower top went down the north way's upper ramp and hopped off its side, and rails tried on a copy kept it up: "加欄杆可以解決 Implement in PT and 9.9", and before it was done: "On a second thought. PT only first."; shown the last east window from inside, its sill 1.57 m over the bridge's floor: "Add a little slope connecting the catwalk floor and the platform so the BOT knows to walk onto it. PT first."; standing on the south way's slope at model x 15.62, z 39.82, feet 17.20 m: "Also, can you add guarding gates on this slope as well?"; then: "Apply Neon to 9.9, online and offline."; and, standing in the bridge against the step at its north end, at model x 4.83, z 24.91, feet 20.29 m, looking north: "Oh, and add an invisible small slope at this gap edge so units can walk right onto the higher ground here. PT and 9.9") — Three things on the ways b1105kg built, all of them `shared/tools/maps/neon_ways_up.mjs`'s. **The rails** (b1105kh on the north way, b1105ki on the south). The scaffold's own fence panel (Node_214_214 and its mesh: 4.00 m long, 0.59 m through its feet, 2.69 m tall, its feet 8 cm in the deck) stands along both sides of the north way's upper ramp, leant with the ramp and its posts upright, and goes on level along the platform's two sides as far as the platform hangs over the air south of the strip (1.32 m: the panel's first piece, cut off); and along both sides of the south way's slope, a leant panel a ramp piece, three a side. Where the platform lies on the strip its sides stay open — a body steps off it onto the strip — and so do the second scaffold's top platform under the slope's foot and the walkway over its top. `glb_place.mjs` has two new words for this: `rise: [gx, gz]`, a lean (the copy's heights go up that much a metre along x and along z, so what stood upright stays upright), and `clip: { axis, from, to }`, a cut (the copy keeps what lies between two planes of the original). A copy with either is baked — its own corners written out, 3,052 triangles here — where a plain copy shares the original's mesh, so the model grows by 308 kB (9,602,792 bytes to 9,911,232; 636 nodes to 680). In the data a level rail is one box, the panel's own depth and its height over the floor (2.61 m), and a leant one boxes in steps, each the panel's height over the ramp at the step's middle (four a side on the north ramp, nine a side on the south slope's 12 m): 28 boxes. The floors end at the rails' inner faces — the north way's upper ramp and the platform's overhang lie between x −5.72 and −2.90, the south way's slope between x 14.02 and 16.84, 2.82 m of the pieces' 4 m — and that is what keeps a bot on them: the rule that hops a pursuing bot off the deck it stands on asks for free air 0.75 m beyond the deck's edge, and that place now lies inside the rail's box, so the hop is not tried, where a rail standing on the floor's own edge would only be jumped at. In the page a rail is one of the things that fade when they stand between the camera and a unit. **The little ramp in the bridge** (b1105ki). The scaffold's ramp piece again, made small — 2.10 m long, 2.26 m wide (the passage through the window onto the piece outside) and 1.57 m high: 36.7°, under the 37.7° of the south way's ramps, which a body walks down without leaving the floor — stands on the bridge's floor against the wall under the last east window and rises east to the sill (model x 5.96..8.06, z 55.49..57.75; pad D's ring ends 0.79 m west of its foot). In the data one sloped floor, and under it three boxes in steps, each as high as the slope at its low end, so that a body on the bridge's floor is held at the ramp's side where the ramp stands over a step's height and steps onto it where it does not. That window was a way in alone — its sill is over the route grid's jump — and is a way out now, a bot's too. **The unseen slope at the bridge's north step** (b1105kj). At the bridge's north end the strip — the tower top's level, 21.24 m — stands 0.95 m over the bridge's floor (20.29 m) from wall to wall: over a step's height (0.65 m). A body walking north was held at the step's face, where the user stood, and had to jump it; one walking south walked off it and was 19 ticks in the air. A sloped floor lies against the step now, from wall to wall (model x 0.81..8.06, 7.25 m) and 1.25 m long (z 24.62..25.87: 37.3°), in the data alone. The model has nothing there — the user's word, invisible — so a unit on it stands over the floor the picture shows by as much as the slope has risen, 0.47 m half way up. No box stands under it: its two sides are the bridge's walls. Lengths from 0.75 m to 2 m were tried on the sim and every one was walked both ways with no tick in the air; 1.25 m gives the steepness of the ways' other slopes. **Measured**, b1105kg's files against these, which are the PT site's byte for byte, the grid file too. 8,189 boxes are 8,220 and 3,725 floors 3,728. On the sim's own tick, with the stick alone: a body walked at a rail for three seconds is held at its inner face (its centre at x −3.19 and −5.44 on the upper ramp and on the platform's overhang, at x 16.56 and 14.31 on the south slope), its feet where they were, and walked off the platform's east side where it lies on the strip it steps down onto the strip; from pad D up the little ramp, over the sill and out onto the walkway is 3.9 s with no tick in the air, and none back down; up the unseen slope onto the strip and back down — by the west wall, where the user stood, and by the east wall — none in the air; and one walk goes from the first scaffold's top to the second's and back with no jump: the north way, the strip, the slope, the bridge, the little ramp, the south way (with no jump, not with no tick in the air: the platform lies 0.2 m over the strip, three sills 0.27 m high cross the bridge's floor, and down the north way's two ramps a body still leaves each ramp's top for 0.4 s). Where a body gets to from the first scaffold's top with no transporter, in cells of 0.25 m over model x −22..32, z 14..62: 32,764 cells before and 32,774 now, none lost, the ten new ones on the slope (51,969 places — a cell and the floor stood on — are 51,494: the ramps' floors under the rails are places no more). The route grid has 20,892 nodes (20,927), 492 jump links (498) and 3,058 drop links (3,104), and no node, edge or link differs farther than 1.15 m from a changed place. The rails took 38 nodes with the floors under them; the unseen slope turned the row of nodes before the step into a row on it and the six jump links over the step into walk edges, and took with them thirteen drop links that led nowhere — the bridge's floor ends a quarter metre short of the step's face, the grid's walk test stood in that slit and found the street 20 m below (as under the last east window, b1105kg), and so the grid had drops "to the street" from the bridge's end and from the strip that no body could fall. One drop link is new, from the slope's west end over the west window's sill to the scaffold's deck, as the bridge's floor has them along that wall. The bots' routes: from pad D to the second scaffold's top on foot is five points, out by the window (thirteen before, with a jump out of a window at z 33.87); from pad D to pad B, and from the first scaffold's top to pad D, are walked all the way (a jump at the step before). **The bots**, sixty seconds, eight runs, the bot starting by pad A and its target standing still 1.5 m north of pad B: sight of the target for 53.2 s, 1,460 damage, and the top level left not once — it was 14.9 s, 591 and seven times in b1105kg (34.1 s, 959 and twice before the ways). What takes it off the floor now, some twenty times a minute, is the 0.8 m off the platform's edge onto the steep ramp, on the way. Its target standing by pad D in the bridge: 1,270 damage (1,307 with the rails and without the slope, 1,310 in b1105kg) — with the slope the bot backs up it onto the strip, a metre higher and two farther off, where it stood against the step's face before (sight 54.3 s, 53.4 before). A bot by pad D whose target stands on the second scaffold's top walks up the little ramp and is out of the window in 2.5 to 4 s; one sent from the bridge to the tower top walks the slope where it jumped the step. The bots' brain is as it was (`nbrain.js` untouched). On the PT site's page the four routes were walked with the keyboard — the two ways, out of the bridge by the little ramp and back, the unseen slope up and down at its middle and by both walls — with no reading in the air on any climb and none on the slope either way (one, stepping off the platform onto the strip, as before); the page's grid is the file.
