In real matches on the built page one walk was made in both modes — from the west home on foot by the route grid's own route to the first scaffold's deck and up the scaffold's own ramp, up the north way with the body walked at each rail of the upper ramp on the way, off the platform onto the strip and the tower top, east along the strip and down the unseen slope into the bridge, back up it where the user stood and down again, south through the bridge, up the little ramp onto the sill, out along the walkway, down the three ramps with the body walked at each of their rails, and back up them to the user's end point on the sill and down the little ramp — offline against a bot, and online on the repo's own server with a second connection sitting in the other seat: 20 stops of 20 as wanted in both, the body held at all four rails' inner faces on its ramp, no reading in the air on any climb nor on the unseen slope or the little ramp either way, online the server's own place for the unit the page's at every stop, the server's grid the file (`[warmup] grid neon: file`). (A first online walk at the rails went wrong by the walk's own keys and not by the map: they give eight directions, a push held "east" ran a little south of it, and the body slid down along the rail to the ramp's foot and off the landing, which has no rail; the push was made to keep its place along the ramp.) 242 tests pass: `shared/test/neon_ways.test.js` has seven (three before; new are the rails holding, out of the bridge by the little ramp, the unseen slope, and the walk from one scaffold's top to the other's and back), and `pads.test.js`'s bot test asks again what b1105kg had to give up — the three seconds whole after the pad's move with no pad goal — and that the bot's feet stay over 17 m meanwhile. **What it costs.** The rails narrow the two slopes to 2.82 m. A unit on the unseen slope stands in the air over the drawn floor, up to 0.95 m at the step; a shot's line that passes under the slope's plane stops at it, as at any sloped floor (the wedge under it holds no body, and such a line would meet the step's face next). Two things about the bots were seen while this was measured and are as they were before it: a bot that comes onto the strip from the bridge's side and backs off a target on the tower top walks off the strip's own south edge between the bridge's wall and the way's platform — 2.1 m of edge with no rail — in eight runs of eight, with the slope and without, falls 10.7 m onto the first scaffold's own ramp and walks back up the north way; and a bot in the bridge's north end whose target stands on the scaffold's deck below, just west of the bridge, stands at the west window's wall and does not get over its sill (the grid has a drop there; twenty seconds a run, every one of eight runs, on b1105kg's files, b1105ki's and these alike). A regeneration of Neon runs `neon_ways_up.mjs` as before — `spec` and `glb_place.mjs` on the model, `data` on the reading, then `navgrid_build.mjs` (`shared/tools/maps/README.md`).

**Neon: a bot in the bridge is no longer sent out by a window — the bridge's floor is carried to its side walls** (b1105kl, 2026-11-22, PT and 9.9, online and offline; user, of one of the two things b1105kj's entry said of the bots — a bot in the bridge's north end, its target on the scaffold's deck below, stands at the west window's wall — first "screenshot visual location", and with the picture: "investigate and fix without touching universal rule. pt and 9.9") — **What was found.** It is not one window. With its target on the decks under and beside the skybridge, a bot in the bridge is routed out by a drop "over a sill" at the nearest window of either side, and does not get down it, in one of two ways. *It stands at the wall.* With no firing place in its band the bot takes the walk route toward its target, cut at the first place that has sight of the target; that place is the drop's landing, the cut writes a new point there, and the new point has not the mark that tells the follower to hop (`nbrain.js` `truncateAtFiringPoint`) — so the bot walks at the wall under the sill, stands, plans again after two seconds, and gets the same route. On the PT site's page a bot left to itself stood at the west wall under the northmost window for the minute it was watched. *It hops and turns back.* Where the mark is kept the bot does hop onto the sill; the landing is the deck's node right under the wall, a cell away in the plan, so from on top of the sill it counts as reached, and the route's next point — under the bridge — turns the bot round, off the sill and into the bridge again, where it plans the same route. Both are the bots' own rules, the same on every map, and are not touched (the user's word). Counted on the sim, the bot starting at three places in the bridge and its target standing still: on the 8.63 m decks (150 places a metre apart) the bot never came to sight of its target, nor within 3 m of it on its floor, in 156 runs of 450 of 25 s; on the street (91 places, 4 m apart) in 40 of 273 of 30 s; on the other floors about the bridge (168 places, 2 m apart, 5.78 to 21.88 m) in 122 of 504. **Why the grid had those drops.** The bridge's floor in the data ended a quarter metre short of the inner face of each side wall (model x 1.06 for the west wall's 0.81, 7.81 for the east wall's 8.06), though the slab runs on under the walls. The grid's walk test looks at floors alone: from a node by a wall to the node a cell beyond it, it walked off the floor's edge, fell, and landed on what lies under the wall — a deck, a roof, the street — and the sill being a lip that a jump hops, the builder made a drop: 42 of them, from the bridge's floor (one from the unseen slope's west end) over its sills, at the windows of both sides, and three more from the east sill down the same slit to the street under the bridge. **The fix, in the map alone.** The floor is carried on as far as the walk test goes, and only where no body's centre comes at a height to stand on it: three floors in Neon's data at the bridge's floor's height, written by `shared/tools/maps/neon_ways_up.mjs`. *East*, where the grid's next node lies on the wall's inner face: across the gap alone (model x 7.81..8.06, z 25.87..57.87) — the strip b1105kg laid under the last window, grown north along the wall; a wall holds a body 0.29 m off its face. *West*, where the next node lies under the wall's outer face: across the gap and under the wall (x 0.06..1.06), as far along as the sill's own floor lies over the wall (z 25.12..57.87) — a body over that stretch at the sill's height stands on the sill's floor, and a lower one is outside the wall. And *a hair* of it, 2.5 cm wide, on the grid's one line across the west wall's north end (z 24.87), where the wall's top has no sill floor: the corner pillar's foot and the tower's top keep a body's centre 2.5 cm south of the hair at every height under the pillar foot's reach, and over that height the hair is 1.3 m under the body's feet. (3,728 floors are 3,730; the boxes and the model are as they were.) The walk test stays on the bridge's floor, and a fresh build of the grid has none of those drops: 20,892 nodes, 73,010 walk edges and 492 jump links as before, 3,015 drop links for 3,058 — 45 links gone and two new, the east sill's proper drops onto the bridge's floor beside the south way's piece. **A first form, taken back.** For about an hour and twenty minutes the PT site carried a first form (b1105kk): the floor under the whole of both walls, end to end. It gives the same grid, and it was wrong for a body. At a wall's end, where the wall's top has no sill floor — a sill's floor ends half a metre or more short of the wall's end — it was a floor inside the wall: a body walking north off the west sill's end stood in the wall at the bridge's floor's height, where before it fell outside onto the scaffold's deck, and one walking up the little ramp to the south-east stayed in the bridge, where before it went out over the sill's end. It had been called the same for a body on 45 readings at two windows and a flood of the cells a body reaches; both were the same and neither went to the walls' ends. A walk in a real match that took 67 s over a stop that had taken 7 was what sent the check back, and the check was made tick by tick: 144,448 set walks about the two walls (every place of three lattices let down from two heights, eight directions, with and without a jump, 8 and 60 ticks) and 34,000 random ones (425 starts, two units, one of them the flier). On the first form 1,947 of the set walks went otherwise than on the data before — 1,920 of them from a start let down into a wall or onto a wall's end, where the body now stayed in the wall, and 27 from a place a body stands on — and 1,014 of the random ones. On the floors as they are now every one of both goes as before, tick for tick. **Measured.** The same counts on the new files: on the decks 18 runs of 450 (156), on the street none of 273 (40), on the other floors 25 of 504 (122). The user's case — the bot at model x 4.5, z 28 in the bridge, its target at x −2.0, z 24.5 on the deck — has the bot up the unseen slope, west along the strip, off its south edge and down on the deck with sight of its target after 4.9 s, six runs of six (never in thirty seconds before, six of six); a target under the bridge (x 6.06, z 33.87), after 7.9 s (never). For a body nothing changes — the walks above; and the flood from the first scaffold's top reaches the same 32,774 cells and 51,451 places, and the page's fade has the same 284 pieces. The two standing measures of the bots: a target by pad B, sight for 53.2 s of 60 and 1,461 damage, the top level not left (the same); a target by pad D in the bridge, 1,261 damage (1,270). On the PT site's page and on the built 9.9 page the route grid is read from its file, and a bot let go in the bridge, its target on the deck where the user's stood, goes up the unseen slope, west along the strip, off its south edge and is down on the deck after 12 and 13 s (it stood at the wall for the minute it was watched before). In real matches on the built page the walk of b1105kj's entry was made again in both modes: 20 stops of 20 as wanted offline and online, no reading in the air on any climb, online the server's own place the page's at every stop and the server's grid the file. (The walk's own script was made sturdier first, at three steps that went wrong by its own keys and not by the map, which is the same there as in b1105kj: a stop half a metre short of the deck's node, on the ramp's foot 8 cm lower; a diagonal of taps before the sill that ran along the walkway's open edge and fell off it; and a walk along the strip that was not asked whether it had arrived.) 245 tests pass, three of them new in `shared/test/neon_ways.test.js`: the route grid has no link from the bridge's floor down past a sill; a bot in the bridge comes to sight of a target on the decks below, beside the bridge (the user's case) and under it, within 15 s; and no body comes to stand in a wall on the floors carried to the walls — one that walks north off the west sill's end is down on the deck (the first form fails this one). **What it costs.** A bot no longer leaves the bridge by a window: it goes by the unseen slope, the little ramp or the transporter, and a window is a player's way out alone. Where a window hop did work the bot is slower now — in the runs with sight both before and now, after 7.1 s on average for 5.5 s on the decks (later by over 2 s in 96 of 291, sooner in 22), 6.9 for 4.9 on the other floors (157 of 369, 41), 7.6 for 5.5 on the street (116 of 233, 24); a target on the walkway west of the bridge was seen after 1.1 s and is after 4.6 s. Sixteen runs had sight before and have none now, 3 on the decks and 13 on the other floors, and nine of the thirteen are one place: the roof under the bridge's east side (12.73 and 12.86 m, model x 4.56..10.56, z 41.37..47.37), along the wall's line. A window drop onto it did work, the target being in sight from the sill itself — after 4.5 to 10.6 s from the three starts; now the bot stands in the bridge over its target, 9 runs of 9, and the grid has no way onto that roof for a bot (its 36 nodes, and 4 on a beam at 11.32 m, are reached from the bridge's own roof alone). A target on the roof's open part is seen and shot from elsewhere as it was, sooner from one start and later from another — from the bridge, the walkway, the second scaffold's top, the street under it and the west home, first sight after 5.6, 0.2, 9.2, 6.3 and 11.6 s (7.0, 0.2, 2.1, 6.3 and 11.8 before), damage in 40 s 444 to 792 (336 to 704); one on the part under the bridge was seen from none of the five before and is from two now (the walkway, 1.4 s; the scaffold's top, 8.3 s). Leaving the drops over that roof in the grid was tried, on the first form, and is worse than doing nothing: with the east wall's floor open along the roof and the beam, never in 270, 169 and 83 runs (decks, other floors, street); along the roof alone, 245, 166 and 55; with the west wall's floor alone, 281, 251 and 136 — every route down from the bridge then runs to the windows that are left. What is left of "never" is the bots' own again, in other places: a target that stands right under the strip's south edge keeps the bot on that edge over it (8 of the 18), and a target right under or over the bot's own place in the plan keeps it there (17 of the 25, nine of them the roof's); the rest were on their way round when the run ended (9 and 6) or still in the bridge (1 and 2). The two rules found — the hop mark lost at a cut, the landing counted from above — stand as they are on every map.

**The Guide and this README checked against the game; two sentences on the homing put right** (2026-11-17, after b1105jn, no build of its own; user: "update the readme and in-game Guide if needed. PT and 9.8.") — The in-game Guide was read line by line against the game and needed nothing: its text is the same in PT and 9.8, its keys are the handlers' (dodge `L`, `Q` or the right click; switch target `U`; point of view `T`; map `R`; ally command `E`), the ally command's line has no length since b1105jn, and its numbers are the game's (100 HP and Kurumi's 120, a Trio's next unit safe for 3 s, a command unit's 40 s on its spot, the transporter's 1 s). This README: the unit tables agree with the sim's unit table cell for cell and the client's with the sim's (566 cells, no difference); the chapters that state what b1105jl, b1105jm and b1105jn changed — *Free pov*, *Controls*, *The ally tactic* under HUD — say it as it is; no old name is used as a current one (Blockade, the `I` key, `lockRange2v2` and the landing plant stand as history alone). Two dated entries spoke of the homing as if it steered, and no round is steered (`HOMING_MAX_DEG_PER_FRAME` and `HOMING_SOFTEN_DEG_PER_FRAME` are 0): b1105gq's "the red lock's and the homing's" lock range, and b1105ct's "the homing still steers at the standing centre" — each now says the homing is off. Not re-read in full: the *Maps* chapter's dated history (its list of the nineteen arenas, the ten maps that name more spawn places and the two with a boost scale are the game's).

**The ally tactic has no time limit** (b1105jn, 2026-11-17, PT and 9.8, online and offline; user: "Change the in-game BOT command effective duration (Forward/Back Off) to no time limit. PT and 9.8 (offline and online).") — FORWARD (the bot teammate's range band 30 nearer its target) and BACK OFF (the Hide stance) each stood 40 s from the call (b1105jm; 20 s and 10 s before that); a call now stands until it is taken back. What ends one: the same one picked again (the take-back), the other one (which replaces it at once), and the unit's fall — its orders are wiped with it, as they were, so a Trio's next unit comes in with no call. **The clock is out of the rule** (`shared/src/sim/tactic.js`): the call's record is its kind alone (`{ kind }`, no `until`); `tacticOf`, `tacticSettle`, `setTactic` and `tacticEcho` take no time; `ALLY_TACTIC_MS` is gone from `constants.js`; and their callers pass none (`command.js` `setAllyTactic`, the brain, the server's `ally:tactic`, the page). The page lights a call for as long as it is there (`allyTacticLive`: offline the rule's own record, online the server's word with each snapshot) and reads no clock for it. The Guide's line reads "The teammate keeps doing it until it falls. Pick the same one again to cancel." **Online the length is the server's, as it was**: a match has no time limit once its server runs this commit; until then the server ends a call after 40 s and stops sending it, and this page follows. For a page built before this commit, which lights a call only while its clock is short of the call's end, the echo carries an end no clock reaches (`ALLY_TACTIC_NO_END`), so such a page on this server lights the call and can take it back. **Tests** (`shared/test/tactic.test.js`, 7 of the 224 that pass; the four that held the length written anew): the record with no time, the echo, set, replaced, taken back, wiped; FORWARD on Plain Field keeps the bot within 70 to 90 of its target for a minute under the call — from 4 s after it, and the same past the 40 s it stood before —, inside the unit's lock, and back within 100 to 120 once taken back; BACK OFF on Streets breaks both enemies' lines within 8 s and is seen on no tick for the rest of a minute, the call still standing, then the take-back drops the stance and the bot comes out; a call taken back leaves the commander's own Hide order standing. **Checked** headless on both builds. Offline, a 2v2 on Plain Field: FORWARD by the HUD's taps stands, the button lit, 20.5 s, 40.5 s and 45 s after the call; the two opened again show it lit, and a tap takes it back at once; `E` then `S` calls BACK OFF, `E` then `W` replaces it with FORWARD; the teammate falls, and the call is gone with it. Online, two pages in a room against a test copy of the server: the host's FORWARD echoed 0.10 s after the call, standing and lit 45 s in, the other side's page told nothing of it; taken back, gone from the page's reading 0.06 s later and the button unlit 0.27 s after the take-back (9.8's run; PT's, timed as one, 0.52 s); the guest's BACK OFF gone 0.16 to 0.23 s after its bot was knocked out — on the PT build and on 9.8's. And across versions, on PT's builds: this page against the server of before (b1105jm) — the echo with an end 39.95 s ahead, the call gone by 40.5 s, the server's word; the page of before against this server — the call lit 45 s in, and taken back by that page. No page error and no server error in any.

**The ally tactic stands 40 s** (b1105jm, 2026-11-17, PT and 9.8, online and offline; user: "Change the in-game BOT command effective duration (Forward/Back Off) to 40sec. PT and 9.8 (offline and online).") — FORWARD (the bot teammate's range band 30 nearer its target) and BACK OFF (the Hide stance) each stood 20 s from the call (b1105io; 10 s before that); they stand 40 s (no time limit since b1105jn, above, which took the number out). The one number is `ALLY_TACTIC_MS` in `shared/src/sim/constants.js`, 20000 to 40000: the brain, the server and the page read it from there, so the call's end, the lit pill and the lit button move with it and no other code changed. Everything else about a call is as it was: the standing one called again is taken back at once (the pill's tap, or `E` then its key), the other one replaces it at once and its own 40 s run from then, a call goes with the unit it was made to, and two calls to one unit stand 0.5 s apart at least. The Guide's line reads "The teammate does it for 40 seconds." **Online the length is the server's**: the server sets the call's end and the pages light what it echoes, so a match is on 40 s once its server runs this commit — whatever the page's build — and stays on 20 s until then. The test's pinned value is 40000 (`shared/test/tactic.test.js`, its times written off the constant since b1105io), with one line more: the call still stands at the moment the 20 s used to end. FORWARD on Plain Field keeps the bot within 70 to 90 of its target (the pushed band is 65 to 95) from 4 s after the call to the call's end at 40 s, inside the unit's lock, and back within 100 to 120 (its own band, 95 to 125) from 5 s after; BACK OFF on Streets breaks both enemies' lines within 8 s of the call and is seen on no tick from then to the call's end. 224 tests pass. **Checked** headless on both builds. Offline, a 2v2 on Plain Field, the first call by the HUD's own taps: FORWARD and BACK OFF were gone after 19.9 s before (b1105io's own check); now after 39.93 to 39.96 s, the call standing and the button lit 20.5 s in, the button unlit the frame the call ends, a call taken back 2 s in gone at once. Online, two pages in a room against a test copy of the server, the host calling FORWARD to its bot and the guest BACK OFF to its own: the echo within 0.15 s, 39.85 to 39.95 s left on it, the call and the lit button gone after 39.85 to 39.95 s, the other side's page told nothing of it — on the PT build and on 9.8's. And across versions, on 9.8's build: this page against the server as it was before (the commit of b1105jl), 19.89 and 19.96 s — the server's word. No page error and no server error in any.

**The right mouse button dodges** (b1105jl, 2026-11-17, PT and 9.8, online and offline; user: "Change the mouse right click binding from target switch to dodge. PT and 9.8") — a press of the right button on the empty screen is DODGE's press, `L`'s and `Q`'s (`input.stepTap`; the step's own rules decide the rest — its cooldown, its boost, a body free to move), in either point of view and in the shooter view alone. It was the target switch for one build (b1105jk), and that is `U`'s and TARGET's alone again. As in b1105jk, free pov on a page whose frame refuses the pointer lock, where a held button's drag is the aim, takes a clean click — under 12 px and 300 ms, the dodge going off at the button's release — so a drag to look about is no dodge; everywhere else the dodge goes off at the press. Command mode turns its camera by that button, as before, and a spectator's right click does nothing (it was TARGET's press there for that one build). The Guide's PC line reads "dodge L, Q or right-click" and "switch target U". The client alone (`main.js`). **Checked** headless on each tree's build: offline in a 2v2, a right click dodges — the unit carried 9 units — with the lock left where it is, a second one inside the dodge's cooldown does nothing, and `U` switches the lock as before; in free pov a clean right click dodges and a right-button drag turns the aim and dodges nothing; a right click while the left button fires dodges; on a HUD button, under the pause menu and in command mode it dodges nothing; the page's menu stays away; online, two pages with a real server, the server carries the unit through the dodge and a drag in free pov dodges nothing.

**The target switch on the right mouse button; the Guide and this README brought up to the game** (b1105jk, 2026-11-17, PT and 9.8, online and offline; user: "Bind target switch to mouse right click for desktop as well." and "Also, Update 9.8 readme(including weapon stats) and in-game Guide(keep it easy to read and concise).") — **The right button**: a press of it on the empty screen is TARGET's press and `U`'s (`cyclePlayerTarget`: the lock in a 2v2, the lanes on the Shooting Range, the watched unit for a spectator), in either point of view and in the shooter view alone — command mode turns its camera by that button, as before. E had been the hand's own key for it beside W A S D until b1105jj gave E to the ally tactic. One case waits for the button's release: free pov on a page whose frame refuses the pointer lock, where a held button's drag is the aim — there the switch is a clean click's, under 12 px and 300 ms (the two taps' own measure), so a drag to look about switches nothing. It reads the mouse's own events (`mousedown` / `mouseup` in `setupFreePovPointer`), since a button pressed while another is held, the left one firing, sends no pointerdown; and the page's menu is kept off the canvas while a match is played. **The Guide**: the PC line names the right-click and is shorter (the ally command's W and S moved to the ally's own line under Fighting), and Fighting has a line each for the round map (b1105iw) and for the fire button's gun and rounds (b1105iv), which the Guide had not said. Every other line was read against the code and stands. **This README**: the weapon tables — Weapons, the theoretical DPS, Handling, the fire caps and the gate table — were checked cell for cell against the sim's unit table by script and agree (the client's table agrees with the sim's on all 566 numbers both name); the hit rates stand as measured, nothing since b1105je touching them. What was stale lay in the text around them and is corrected: the game's name in the title; the opening's "no manual targeting" (the free pov aims by hand); Dummy's place (the Practice row); the corner HUD, which has been HP chips since b1105w, with the online chip's side word in place of the white rim, and the bars over the units, in each unit's own colour since b1105dz; the lock bracket's scale at the caps (×2.11, ×3.03 and ×3.21 for Saori, Marina and Koyuki since the base cones went to 0); the profile card's `4 ×8`, its walk tiers' newer units and the shotguns' picture at 80; the bot gate's lines read against today's bands (they were set beside the 43–63 bands of before b1105eg), Iori among the marksman rifles and Maki among the 5 s reloaders; the firing search's reach (237, 287 and 537 u); Neon among the maps whose data is a file; the route files' sizes; what `docs/img` holds; and, in the implementation notes, the band's ± 15, the 2v2 lock ranges that went at b1105eg and the rounds' speed. The client alone changes in code (`main.js`: the right button, the Guide's text, and one comment's numbers, the lock bracket's). **Checked** headless on each tree's build: offline in a 2v2, a right click switches the lock in the angled pov and the next one switches it back; in free pov a clean right click switches it and a right-button drag turns the aim and switches nothing; a right click while the left button fires switches it; the page's menu stays away; a right click on a HUD button, under the pause menu and in command mode switches nothing; online, two pages with a real server, the switch comes back as the server's word. The Guide's page was photographed on a phone's screen and a desktop's.

**The keyboard: E the ally command, T the point of view, R the map** (b1105jj, 2026-11-17, PT and 9.8, online and offline; user: "For desktop keyboard, apply these changes. 1. bind "E" to shooter mode in-game BOT command. After clicking E, pressing "W" would command "Forward", pressing "S" would command "Back Off". 2. Change pov switch from "I" to "T". 3. PT to 9.8 (online and offline)." and then "Oh, and "R" for calling up map.") — three keys under the hand that rests on W A S D. **E** is the ally tactic button's own press in the shooter view (`allyTacticKeyPress`): the two calls come up, a second E puts them away, and they close of themselves 3 s on, as after a tap. While they are up a press of **W** is a tap on FORWARD and a press of **S** a tap on BACK OFF (`allyTacticKeyPick`; the tap and the key share `allyTacticPick` — the lit call taken back, the other called, the two closed). That press is the call's alone: the window's key handler returns on it, so it moves nothing however long it is held; a W or an S already held when E was pressed goes on moving the unit; the arrow keys stay the stick's. Where the button answers no tap (a 1v1, a human teammate, Sudden Death, the Shooting Range, a spectator) or is not on the screen (command mode, where E turns the camera as it did; the game-start intro; the ending camera; the pause menu) E does nothing. **E no longer switches the lock** in the shooter view, as it had from 2026-10-30; **U** switches it, as before. **T** switches the angled and the free pov; **I**, its key from 2026-10-30, does nothing now. **R** is the round map's own press (`miniMapKeyPress`): the large map comes up, and the next R, or the next press on the round map, takes it away — the one way to the large map on a desktop page in free pov whose pointer is held. A button is on the screen to be tapped or it is not, and a key is always under the hand, so the new keys ask `matchKeysLive()`: offline the loop's flag (not the menus, the pause menu, the intro or the ending camera), online `onlineMatchLive()` (b1105ji), never under the loading screen. The keys sound as the taps do (the tactic's small UI sound; the map silent). The Guide's PC line and its "press T" name the keys. The client alone: no rule, no shared code, nothing of the server's. **Checked** headless on each tree's build, by the page's own keyboard. Offline, a 2v2 beside a bot: E up and away; E then W gives FORWARD with the unit not moved through a 0.6 s held press (the same hold without the two up moves it some 5 units); E then S gives BACK OFF; E then S on the standing call takes it back; a W held from before goes on moving the unit while E and S make the call; the arrow keys move it with the two up; E leaves the lock, U switches it; T switches the pov and back, I does nothing; R up and away, and R and a press on the round map undo each other; under the pause menu and in command mode R and E bring nothing up; the taps work as before; in a 1v1 E does nothing, R and T work. Online, two pages in a 2v2 with a bot beside each, a real server: E then W comes back from the server as FORWARD, the other side's page told nothing; BACK OFF and the take-back the same; U switches the lock, E not; T gives Free pov and the other page sees the free aim; R the large map.

**Free pov in an online match** (b1105ji, 2026-11-17, PT and 9.8; user: "Free pov is unavailable in online matches. Fix in PT and 9.8") — online the pov could not be used: two taps on the empty screen switched nothing, and on a desktop `i` turned the camera with no crosshair on the screen, no aim by the mouse and no shot by its button. The cause is as old as Free pov (PT b1027c, 2026-10-30): its gates read `state.running`, and that is the OFFLINE loop's flag — `startOnlineMatch` puts it down and nothing online raises it, an online match runs off the server's word. So the taps and the swipe on the empty screen, the mouse's aim and its click (`setupFreePovPointer`) and the crosshair (`updateCrosshair`) never came on online, though the frame carried `freeAim` / `aimYaw` / `aimPitch` and the server fired along them all the while. Online they read `onlineMatchLive()` now (main.js): the server's match `'active'`, this page's units built, the game-start intro over and no ending camera up. Offline they read the flag, as before. Two more things read that flag and are right with it: **the crosshair of a free-aiming unit that a fallen teammate or a spectator watches** (the same `updateCrosshair`), and **the transporters' ring** (`updatePadRing` — online the hold counted and the unit was carried, with no TELEPORTING ring on the screen). The client alone: no rule, no shared code and nothing of the server's changes. **Checked** headless on each tree's build against a real server. Before (b1105jh), two pages in a 1v1: the phone page's two taps switch nothing, no crosshair, a swipe turns nothing, the other page never sees `freeAim`; the desktop page's `i` gives no crosshair, its mouse turns nothing and its left button fires nothing; on Neon's transporter C the hold counts through 8 samples and the ring shows in none. After: the phone's two taps switch, the crosshair shows, a swipe turns the aim, the fire button fires along it, two taps switch back; the desktop's `i`, mouse and left button the same; the other page sees `freeAim`; in a 2v2 a fallen player watching its free-aiming teammate sees that unit's crosshair and `i` does nothing for the watcher; the ring shows in every sample of the hold and the unit arrives at D. Offline the same checks — a desktop page, a phone page, the ring on Neon — pass as they did.

**A landing holds nothing: the landing plant is gone** (b1105jh, 2026-11-17, PT and 9.8, online and offline; user, of the rule as it was explained to them — a body landing on a crate's, a container's or a roof's top at another level stayed where it landed, the held stick ignored until it was let go or 0.6 s passed: "Remove this feature. PT and 9.8") — the rule of 2026-10-22 ("make the containers standable") is out of the sim and out of the client's mirror, for a player's unit and a bot's alike: a landing drops no momentum and holds no stick, whatever the body lands on. Removed with it: `LAND_PLANT_MS` / `LAND_PLANT_LEVEL` (constants.js), the fighter's `landedOnLid` and `plantUntil` (state.js; their resets at a ladder's mount and a transporter's arrival), the check at the landing (`integrateVertical`) and the hold in the input (`applyInput`), the same two in the client (`updateTransforms`, `updatePlayer`), and the surface lookup the rule alone asked (`groundSurfaceAt`, physics.js and the client's copy). Kept: `floorY` (the void's edge, the ladders, the transporters and the bots read it) and the `lid` mark on the maps' surfaces (the client's fade reads it), so no map data changes. **What it is like now**, on the rule's own ground — Warehouse's 3.19 m crate and the 2.5 m container south of it, its roof 2.5 m deep — in the sim, before and after: a sprint-jump with the stick held throughout stood 0.6 s where it landed and then walked off the roof (0.80 s on it in all); now it runs on at once, 8.9 u in the first 0.3 s, and is off the far side 0.19 s after its feet came down, as before the rule. The stick let go as the feet come down, or within 0.1 s of it, keeps the body on the roof (it slides 1.4 u and 4.2 u); let go 0.2 s after or later it goes off (with the rule any release within the 0.6 s stayed). A walk off the crate with the stick held is on the roof 0.83 s (1.44 s), a walking jump 0.45 s (1.06 s). The built client, offline, the player's own unit by its keys (W, Shift, Space), gave the same: with W held throughout 0 u in the first 0.3 s and 0.82 s on the roof before, 8.8 u and 0.23 s after; W let go as the feet come down, or 0.1 s after, stays on the roof; 0.3 s after, off. **The bots**: the rule dropped a bot's momentum too; 72 matches of four plain bots on six maps (Warehouse, Compound, Town, District, Dust, Neon), the same seeds on either sim: five maps came out the same to the last number read and Town differs a little; in all 55 and 56 landings on a lid at another level, 12 and 11 of them off the thing within a second, damage a bot-minute 186 and 187, the matches 30.8 s either way. Tests: `shared/test/landing.test.js` in place of `plant.test.js` (the run-on with the stick held and the momentum kept, the stay with the stick let go, the walk-off, the flags gone); 224 pass.

**Hiyori joins** (b1105cy to b1105da, 2026-11-08, PT and 9.5; user: "Add unit \"Hiyori\" into PT. Give her Aru stats with below differences", then "Change Hiyori damage scale from \"60/40/25\" to \"60/40/20\"" and "Update unit Hiyori to 9.5") — unit19 in both unit tables: Aru's stats with `weapon: 'NTW-20'`, `damage: 60`, `rangeDamage` near 20 / mid 40, `magCapacity: 3`, no `crouchHit`. Her model, client/public/models/hiyori_v2.glb (the user's Hiyori without her backpack case, her Formation_Idle, the swimsuit Hiyori's seven battle motions bound by exact bone names, her hair following her Formation_Idle, Saori's jump, Fubuki's dodge); `UNIT_MODELS.hiyori`: Normal_Idle to stand, Kneel_Attack_Start to dodge, Move_Jump to jump. A new clip key, `cooldown` (fit 'cooldown'): updateUnitModelState plays Normal_Attack_Delay from the shot to the end of her 1 s fire cooldown, one cycle, over walking and standing; the reload, a dodge, a jump or a sprint take its place, and an emptied magazine goes straight to the reload. Her pictures: units/hiyori_profile.png, units/hiyori_profile_thumbnail.png (UNIT_FACE 0.56 / 0.47), weapons/hiyori.png (WEAPON_BOX 158, 20, 712 x 206). Online the server reads her from the shared table; nothing new rides a snapshot.
**Dodging while firing faces the dodge, offline and online** (b1105cr, b1105cx, 2026-11-08, 9.5; user: "When the character is doing dodge+firing+motion input. Make the character facing the dodging direction instead of facing target. Once the dodge animation is over and the character is still firing, face at the target again. Also, if \"dodge+firing+motion\" is airborne, the character still faces the target.", then "implement the dodge facing direction update in 9.5. Online and offline.") — a player's dodge on the ground whose line the stick chose turns the model to that line while the unit fires, over its target and the free-pov crosshair; when the dodge ends the firing rule turns it back, at the usual MODEL_TURN_RATE. Offline the line is stamped where the dodge starts (updatePlayer: `stepFaceAt`/`stepFaceX`/`stepFaceZ`). Online the server marks the dodge: tick.js applyInput sets the fighter's `stepStick` when the stick chose the line, tryStartStep and tickStep clear it to undefined, so the snapshot carries the key only while such a dodge runs; mirrorFighterToMech reads it with the dodge's line (stepTo minus stepFrom), so every client turns that player's model. Untouched: a dodge with no stick, a dodge in the air, a dodge without firing, the bots. Only the model turns: the rounds, the hit capsule and the bots' reading are as before. Tests: 185 of 185.
**Hina dodges with Fubuki's motion** (b1105cw, 2026-11-08, PT and 9.5; user: "Apply Fubuki's dodge motion to Hina. Implement in PT and 9.5.") — her `dodge` borrows fubuki_dodge_clip.json (Fubuki's Kneel_Attack_Start) with `base: 'Move_End_Normal'`, her own clip, which still poses her MG42, jacket and wings; 32 of her 44 body bones take Fubuki's rotations, the rest (helper bones, toes) keep her own. Her jump stays Saori's (b1105ck). Her clips crossfade (MODEL_FADE_S).
**The lobby's map die takes the taps around it** (b1105cv, 2026-11-08, PT and 9.5; user, of the phone lobby with the die circled: "make the random map dice's detection wider in main menu. user sometimes trigger the map selection menu instead of the dice itself. Keep current visual layout.") — an invisible pad on the die (style.css `.memo-dice::before`) reaches 16 px out on the left, the top and the bottom and 12 px on the right, inside the paper's edges: about 64 x 68 px where the die is 36 x 36. A tap on the pad is a tap on the die and presses it the same way; nothing drawn changes. Measured on a 1000 x 462 phone view: taps 10 px left, 12 px above, 12 px below and 9 px right of the die roll the map; 40 px left still opens the picker.
**Warehouse: the two small crates stepped over** (b1105cu, 2026-11-08, PT and 9.5; user, of the debug panel's warehouse #195 and #506: "Make these two object walk-over-able instead of blocking the way.") — each crate's box and the thin lip box beside it (data obstacles 195 and 214, 506 and 515, 1 m tall) now top out at 0.6 m (2.4 u), under the 0.65 m a body steps (SURFACE_STEP_HEIGHT 2.6 u), and their floors (surfaces 19 and 168, 62 and 325) lie at the same height: a unit walks onto the crate and over it instead of being stopped, as Neon's ramp frames were trimmed (b1105bm). The model is unchanged, so a unit on a crate stands 0.4 m inside its top. The nav grid is rebuilt (2291 walkable nodes); the nav data test passes in both trees.
**Asuna's and Aru's crouched hit capsule online** (b1105ct, 2026-11-08, 9.5; user: "update Asuna/Aru HB to online as well") — the shared unit table lists `crouchHit` for unit9 and unit3 as the client's does, and the server's hit tests (shared/src/sim/projectiles.js: the round, the pellets, the beam, the charged beam, free aim's re-pick) read `hitCapAt`: the server plays no clips, so `hitCapOf` (history.js) reads the client's clip-picker moments off the fighter: alive, off a ladder, not dodging, not airborne, not sprinting, and firing (within 200 ms of a shot, charging a sniper shot, sweeping a charged beam) or reloading. Lag compensation records each tick's capsule with the body (`hitCap` on the history ring), so a lagged shot meets the capsule the shooter saw. The homing's aim point is still the standing centre, as the client's is — and the homing itself is off, both of its rates 0, so no round is steered. The bots never read it. Nothing new rides a snapshot.
**Borrowed motions for twelve more units, and Asuna's and Aru's crouched hit capsule** (b1105cj to b1105cs, 2026-11-08, PT and 9.5; user: "Update unit motions and Asuna&Aru hitbox to 9.5.") — the unit model table is PT's: Subaru's dodge (subaru_dodge_clip.json) for Koyuki, Asuna, Atsuko, Saori, Marina, Haruka, Yuuka and Rabu; Saori's jump (saori_jump_clip.json) for Koyuki and Fubuki, and for Hina's jump and her dodge; Fubuki's dodge, her Kneel_Attack_Start written out by shared/tools/anim/clip_extract.mjs --no-root-motion to client/public/models/fubuki_dodge_clip.json (0.6 s, 124 tracks, 166 KB), for Aris and Kei. Each borrowed entry's `base` is the unit's own clip it replaced, which still poses its gear, hair and clothes (borrowClip). Asuna and Aru list `crouchHit: ['Normal_Attack_Ing', 'Normal_Reload']`: while their model plays one of them, `hitCapsule` gives them a capsule two thirds as tall (HIT_CAP_CROUCH [-16/15, 8/15], the bottom kept), read by every hit test of the client (rounds, pellets, Aris's beams, Kei's charged beam, the free pov's round and crosshair, the online mirror's own-shot ring); the bots never read it. Online, see b1105ct.
**Hoshino jumps with Saori's motion** (b1105ci, 2026-11-08, PT and 9.5; user: "Apply Saori's jump motion to Hoshino. implement in PT.") — Saori's jump, Move_Jump in her file, written out by shared/tools/anim/clip_extract.mjs (the b1105cf sampler) to client/public/models/saori_jump_clip.json (1 s, 119 tracks, every rotation of unit length, its root pinned in plan); Hoshino's `jump` entry borrows it as her dodge borrows Subaru's: her body takes Saori's rotations, her gear, gun, hair, skirt and face her own 01_Move_End_Normal (`base`), and the way back to her stance eases over BORROWED_EXIT_S. Its entry has `easeIn`: the way in eases the same way (measured at 60 fps under the fake clock, the cut into Saori's first frame turned 139 degrees in one frame); her dodge keeps its snap in.
**Hoshino eases out of the borrowed dodge** (b1105ch, 2026-11-08, PT and 9.5; user: "there is a sudden flick between the last moment of dodge motion and her normal stance when she finishes the dodge move") — Hoshino's model cuts between its clips (`fade: 0`, the owner's 2026-10-10 pick: a crossfade slid her backpack across her back), and her own dodge ended near her stance, so the cut never showed; Subaru's ends in Subaru's pose. Leaving a borrowed clip on a cutting model, every part the clip moved but the gear (`borrowClip`'s `body`: the borrowed body, the Biped's helper bones, the hair and the skirt of her own clip) is caught as it stands and eased into the new clip's pose after the mixer each frame over BORROWED_EXIT_S = 0.15 s, the rotations by slerp and the positions by lerp; the gear (shield, plates, pistol, shells, holster, pockets, grenades) still cuts, so the backpack never slides. Measured under Playwright's fake clock at a steady 60 fps, the dodge's end: before, 83° of turn in one frame (three dodges alike); after, at most 9.4° a frame, the turn spread over the nine frames of the 0.15 s.
**Hoshino's gear stays on through the borrowed dodge** (b1105cg, 2026-11-08, PT and 9.5; user, with her bind pose: "it's better, but her accessories from \"bind pose\" are showing up during the dodge motion") — her plates, shield, pistol and shells are posed only by her own clips (her 01_Move_End_Normal scales the plates to 0.2 and the shield to 0.63 x 0.38 and moves them into her vest and onto her back), and a part no playing clip moves falls back to its bind pose, where her file lays them out beside her. `borrowClip` now takes from the borrowed file the Biped's body bones alone (`Bip001…`, the face, the eyes and `Bip001_Weapon` left out) and adds, for every other part, the tracks of the rig's own clip named by the entry's `base`, fitted to the borrowed clip's length: Hoshino's `dodge` has `base: '01_Move_End_Normal'` (0.9 s, fitted to 1 s), so her gear, gun, hair, skirt, pockets and mouth follow her own dodge while her body does Subaru's.
**Hoshino's borrowed dodge no longer flickers** (b1105cf, 2026-11-08, PT and 9.5; user, with a screen recording: "it's all flickering and distorting") — Subaru's Move_Jump keeps most of its rotations as glTF CUBICSPLINE samplers, each key an in-tangent, its value and an out-tangent; shared/tools/anim/clip_extract.mjs wrote those triplets as plain values marked smooth, which AnimationClip.parse read three keys to one, so a tangent was taken for a rotation: 88 of the 117 rotation tracks, quaternions up to 1.6 off unit length (Bip001_L_Hand the worst) in 3 of the 14 frames sampled through a dodge, the mesh sheared and stretched. The tool now samples a CUBICSPLINE sampler at 60 Hz by the glTF Hermite form, normalizes a rotation and writes plain linear keys; subaru_dodge_clip.json re-extracted (258 KB, every rotation of unit length). models/climb_clip.json carries the same fault but binds to no one: all sixteen unit files have their own Vital_Retreat.
**Hoshino dodges with Subaru's motion** (b1105ce, 2026-11-08, PT and 9.5; user: "let's try to apply subaru's dodge motion to Hoshino first. Implement in PT only.") — Subaru's dodge, Move_Jump in her file, written out by shared/tools/anim/clip_extract.mjs to client/public/models/subaru_dodge_clip.json (1 s, 117 rotation tracks, its root pinned in plan, 242 KB); Hoshino's `dodge` entry names it with `from`, and `borrowClip` fetches the file once and binds it by bone name as the climb clip is bound: the rotations alone, the bones her rig lacks left out. Her jump keeps her own 01_Move_End_Normal.
**The phone's buttons press down under a held stick; a cancelled touch lets go** (b1105cd, 2026-11-07, PT and 9.5; user: "when the joycon is being held, other buttons on the right side (Target/Fire/Dodge/Sprint/Jump) do not show the pushed down effect", then "No" to a held look for a locked sprint and "If the fix won't bring any more lag to online/offline games, then yes" to the cancelled touch) — the pressed look was CSS `:active`, which a page gives one element at a time, so the finger on the stick held it and a second finger's button stayed up, its input going through all the same. setupHUD now marks each touch's own button `is-down` on its pointerdown and clears it on its pointerup or pointercancel, and style.css gives `.buttons button.is-down` the `:active` look; a mouse keeps `:active` alone. A touch the system cancels (a call, a swipe in from the screen's edge) sent no pointerup, so the stick stayed deflected and a held button stayed held, the unit walking and the gun firing on until the next touch: the stick and the buttons now take pointercancel as a release. Measured on a touch emulation with Hina, before: the fire button stayed up under the held stick, and after a cancel the unit walked 4.2 u and fired 13 rounds in 1.2 s. Event work only, on a press or a release: nothing per frame, the same inputs sent online.
**Streets' thumb between the middle and the top** (b1105cc, 2026-11-07, PT and 9.5; user: "not very good either. can you drop at the height between middle and upper part for "Street" thumbnail?") — MAP_DATA arena2 `thumb: '50% 25%'`: the value is the picker tile's CSS object-position, so the wide thumb now sits halfway between the card's middle (50%) and its top (0%), the buildings' lower floors, the bridge and the street under it.
**Streets' thumb at the top** (b1105cb, 2026-11-07, PT and 9.5; user: "crop the upper part for \"Street\" thumbnail as well.") — MAP_DATA arena2 `thumb: 'top'`: the picker's wide thumb shows the upper part of its card, the buildings and the bridge over the crossing.
**Shooting Range's card again, its thumb at the top** (b1105ca, 2026-11-07, PT and 9.5; user: "Shooting range again. Crop the upper part for thumbnail this time.") — client/public/maps/range.png replaced by the user's new 1024 x 1024 picture, the two DMG 0 targets and the walls from farther back; MAP_DATA range `thumb: 'top'`, so the picker's wide thumb shows the targets and their DMG 0 boards.
**Five more map cards, new pictures** (b1105bz, 2026-11-07, PT and 9.5; user: the pictures in the order "Lobby/Scrapyard/Factory/Street/Plain Field", thumbs in the middle, and "No" to moving Warehouse's, Spacecraft's and the Backrooms' thumbs) — client/public/maps lobby.png, factory2.png (Scrapyard), factory.png, arena2.png (Streets) and arena1.png (Plain Field) replaced by the user's 1024 x 1024 pictures: the Lobby's hall with its pillars and showcases, the Scrapyard's yard from above, the Factory's floor under its beams and pipes, the Streets' crossing from above, the Plain Field's grid at night (sent as WebP, saved as PNG). None of the five sets `thumb`, so the picker's thumbs show the middle; Warehouse, Spacecraft and both Backrooms keep theirs.
