MAP_DATA hospital loses `thumb: 'top'`, so the picker's wide thumb shows the middle of all five, as it already did for the other four.
**The lobby's red line is dashed** (b1105bx, 2026-11-07, PT and 9.5; user: "change the red line style from current \"G\" to \"E\". Apply to PT and 9.5.") — `lobbyCrayonLine` draws the design canvas's round 10b style E in place of G: an even dashed line, 24 px dashes 9 px apart on the 388 px box along the same slow wobble, each dash a 3.9 px crayon stroke (within 5% thicker or thinner and 0.2 px up or down, from a seeded draw) whose ends thin over its first and last 28%, the canvas's generator (seps2.py `dashes_e`) with a seeded draw of JS's own; the same red, the same #crayon-line wax, the same two places, seeds and tilts. The SVG stretches to the column as before, so the dashes and the gaps stretch with it (22 and 8.3 px in the 358 px column of a 390 px portrait phone; the landscape phone's lobby is zoomed whole, the lines with it).
**Compound's roofs go in Command mode** (b1105bw, 2026-11-07, PT and 9.5; user: "When in command mode, hide the building rooftops in map \"Compound\". Apply to PT and 9.5.") — GLB_MAPS compound `roof`: Object_4 (the sheds' sawtooth and corrugated roofs and the front shelters'), Object_27 (the long hall's and the middle block's), Object_15 (the hut's by the north lot) and Object_35 (the middle block's lid under its roof), 8 meshes with their fade copies, hidden in Command mode by the roof rule every map has (`updateMapRoof`; on PT photo mode follows its panel). Compound's objects are the meshes themselves and `fadeNodes` adds each one's fade copy beside it, not under it, so the name lists missed the copies: the copy now carries `userData.fadeSrc`, its source, and the `roof` and `cmdClear` lists match a copy by its source (Neon's copies sit under their nodes and are found as before). The middle block's upper floor (Object_16, 27.6 u) is walked and stays: the nav grid's reachable floors on Compound top out at 27.64 u, and of its 356 reachable nodes at 8 u or higher none stands on one of the four (a ray down from each).
**Town keeps its whole model in Command mode** (b1105bv, 2026-11-07, PT and 9.5; user: "Town does not need this treatment") — GLB_MAPS town `cmdClear: false`: a map that says so is left out of `updateCmdClear`, rule and list alike, so Town's 2 meshes stay. The costs measured (headless Chromium, software GL, this build): the per-frame check under 0.1 µs; Neon's list looked up at load, 0.3 ms; the list built on a map's first entry into Command mode, once, 5.5 to 12.8 ms with every bounding box still to compute (Dust 5.5, Neon 6.4, Compound 9.1, Hospital 12.8 with its million vertices) and 0.9 to 5.7 ms with them computed; the hidden parts are not drawn, 112 meshes and 47,270 triangles fewer on Neon, 100 and 8,495 on Dust, 8 and 9,327 on Compound. A match started in Command mode never drew them, so the first switch to Shooter uploads their buffers then (Neon 97 geometries and 3 textures more in that frame than when they had been drawn once, Dust 75 and none), with no new shader program and no longer frame in the test; a Shooter start uploads them at load as before. Nothing is sent over the network.
**Command mode clears the view on the modelled maps** (b1105bu, 2026-11-07, PT and 9.5; user: "Currently there are some maps where items outside the map blocking player's site in command mode (Blockade, Dust etc.). I want those objects be temporarily hidden in command mode", then "自動規則加手動清單" and "implement in PT first") — main.js `updateCmdClear` (called from `updateMapRoof`, every frame, acting only when the mode changes): in Command mode a modelled map hides, by rule, every mesh lying wholly outside the collision area and rising more than 2 m over the street, and by hand the meshes under the nodes its GLB_MAPS `cmdClear` lists; Shooter shows them again, each as visible as it was (on PT photo mode too). Neon lists 56 tower nodes, every part starting 12 m or more over the street (112 meshes; the highest floor a unit can reach there, from both spawns over the nav grid's walks and links, is 51.3 u, the lowest hidden part starts at 52.6 u, so no floor anyone stands on goes). The rule takes Dust's floating skyline (100 meshes), 2 meshes on Town and 8 on Compound; District, Spacecraft, Warehouse, Hospital and the Backrooms have none outside. Visibility only: the collision, the bots, the shots and the online sync are unchanged. Its own userData key, `cmdClear`: `userData.cmdHide` is the older flag on the built maps' airborne dressing, which `applyDioramaDressing` hides wherever it sits in arenaDecor.
**A Command unit under a move order defends for 0.3 s** (b1105bt, 2026-11-07, PT and 9.5 together; user: "In command mode, when a unit is given an assigned location, change these 4 durations from 1sec to 0.3sec for that assigned unit", then "Both" (the way there and the 40 s guard) and "No. Keep SD brain untouched") — nbrain.js `BOT_DEFENSE_CMD_MOVE_MS` = 300: while a unit holds a move order, its travel and its anchor alike, the four Defense commits (a hit, a hit under the locked sniper's charge, the anti-glint dodge's follow-through, the vestigial stuck one) run 300 ms instead of 1,000, so the escape across the line hands the frame back to the order sooner; a hit's Defense is then about 0.65 s with the 0.35 s evade window before it, where it was about 1.35 s. A lock-only order, a unit without orders and Sudden Death keep 1,000 ms. Assessed before the build (a Command unit ordered to the enemy's spawn under an unkillable machine gun, 8 runs a map): the time in Defense 20 to 40% shorter, the units that arrived there sooner (21 to 13 s, 25 to 17 s), more damage taken on the way. The equivalence matches carry no orders and played equal to the b1105br references after the change; re-frozen at b1105bt.

## Sudden Death

Picked as the **rules mode** beside the view mode (**Health Point** is the normal game; the two were named Normal / Classic until 2026-10-10). Everyone fights at **1 HP** — one landed round decides — and Trio respawns arrive at 1 HP too. The three sniper rifles (Aru, Kei, Hiyori) are left out of the Random and All Random cards and of the default units of the online bot slots (Aru's seat fields Saori); one picked by hand — a player's own unit or roster, the host's unit for a bot — plays (owner 2026-10-10: "if the human chooses it manually, it should appear"). The match opens on the game-start intro and then a full-width **SUDDEN DEATH** banner, the simulation frozen behind both; every bot slot's 650 ms first-shot delay counts from the banner's end (the server holds the same way online). Since b1105gz the banner follows the intro's START and no unit is immune at the match's start (the 3 s spawn immunity used to count from the banner's end).

**The Sudden Death bot brain** — one module for both hosts, `shared/src/sim/sdbrain.js`: the server's `tickBot` and the offline client's `updateEnemy` each build a small host and call `sdBrainTick` once a tick, for every bot slot offline and online, 1v1, 2v2, Trio and the spectated player slot; there is no second copy and no switch. Rewritten on 2026-10-16 (b1017a–h) as **one rule in four parts, each written once**, after the earlier brain had grown to 3,000 lines and 144 knobs of special cases: the unit stands only where it is hidden; it is exposed only to buy something — a firing line, a better cell, the way back; every exposure is priced the same way, and the cheapest one that buys the most is taken. 1,000 lines, 44 knobs (`BOT_SD`), nothing per weapon or per map; the enemy's magazine is never read, its shots are.

1. **Hidden** — one test of the body's silhouette (the front of the capsule, both shoulders, the four shoulder corners, the top) against every **eye**: each enemy where it stands, where its run takes it in the next 0.5 s, and where it fired **every round still in the air** (a shot log per enemy, each shot kept for a round's life — a sprinting sprayer has three or four rounds in the air at once, each from a different place). Hidden means no eye reaches any point of the silhouette.
2. **The price** — the chance of dying in an exposure of a given length at a given distance, moving across the line, under the enemy's reaction: none while its stream is on, 250 ms while its gun is aimed (it fired within 2 s), 600 ms when it is not, or the median of its last five measured reactions, whichever is faster; with its live bloom and cadence, the shotguns by their real cloud. Against it, the chance the unit's own rounds land in the same window. An attack is taken when the deaths it costs to land one hit stay under the one cap, **0.15**; a route, when its death chance does.
3. **The plan** — hidden with nothing under way, the unit prices **the Engage Run** (a straight run across the line through a firing point to a hidden end), **the Stand Peek** (a leg to a firing point, the window, the way back: inside **30 u** the leg is the dodge itself, **the Close Peek**, beyond it a sprint with the dodge kept for the way out) and a **relocation** (a hidden cell by a priced route: closer while beyond the unit's reach, a line-capable cell inside it, else any cell nearer), and takes the cheapest under the cap. Every option refused, it asks what it is waiting for, priced once more with what time brings back — the gun gone quiet, the dodge in hand, the tank full: something under the cap then makes the plan **the Wait** (pacing its cell inside a 6 u leash, priced again every 250–500 ms); nothing makes it **the Approach**, the pathfinder's route to the enemy, priced like any route. The cell it waits in is read at the horizon as well: seen by then, **the Escape** — the nearest cell hidden through the horizon, by a priced route, before the line opens. Caught exposed with no plan, **the Reaction**: the dodge across the line on the tick the line opens, then the sprint to the nearest hidden cell.
4. **The legs** — hidden, along the route; exposed, the crossing legs, always across the line, the side flipped only by a wall (physics' note of the face it pushed the body out of). A peek's window runs across the line away from its cover and ends the tick its way out would cost the cap. The brain and the gun keep running through the dodge; only the body's velocity stands down for the step.

**The unit's own reaction** (b1017f–g): the trigger opens **250 ms** after the unit *knew* — an attack's take (the Stand Peek and the Engage Run walk it into sight: a leg longer than the reaction fires the tick its line opens, a shorter one waits out the rest), else the first sighting of its silhouette. The hosts take this trigger as the Sudden Death bot's only trigger — the plain brain's bloom gate does not apply — and the own-hit price counts rounds from the same moment, so a window too short for its first round is refused rather than walked.

**The tank** (b1017d, b1017h): a reserve of **86** (a dodge, 48, and half a second of lateral sprint) times the map's boost scale — **430** on District and Compound, some nine dodges, the dodge and the sprint being costs, which the scale leaves alone — is kept through every planned exposure, which starts only with its own cost on top of it; the body exposed spends to the floor, 8 times the scale (40 on District and Compound). Hidden legs sprint through **the valve** — the tank is spent in one draught and refilled in one: the valve shuts when the gauge is down to the reserve plus the longest peek's sprint and opens again only at a full gauge. (A hidden leg used to sprint a tick every few hundred ms as the gauge refilled, each tick resetting the 500 ms refill pause, so the gauge never rose while the unit walked and attacks went out on a third of a tank.) Defensive spending — the dodge, the crossing legs, the retreat, the escape — does not pass the valve.

**Reach** — the one per-unit number the brain still reads: how far the unit's own gun lands every round on a standing target (the base cone's footprint fits the hit radius, and a 0 cone, every unit's but Aris's since b1105fw, reads 200; a shotgun's lock range stands in; `sdReach` in the unit data overrides the formula). Attacks are searched inside the reach plus 10 u of slack; beyond it the relocation closes in. The engage floor and the band of the earlier brain are gone: a route's or an attack's safety is its price, not a distance.

| Unit | Reach |
|---|---|
| Saori, Asuna, Subaru, Minori, Koyuki, Maki | 80 (set) |
| Hoshino, Haruka, Rabu | 80 (their lock range) |
| Aris | 160 (her 0.02 base cone) |
| Atsuko, Marina, Yuuka, Kurumi, Hina, Fubuki, Iori, Aru, Kei, Hiyori | 200 (a 0 base cone; from 53 to 160 by their cones until b1105fw) |

**Measured** (headless, the shared sim driven tick by tick): against the brain it replaced, 63% of decided duels at b1017e and about 50% once the unit's own reaction was in (the old brain reacts in 0 ms); the wall-hugger scan on flashpoint (the human 1.2 u off every obstacle face) 29–30 of 31 kills in 40 s; 10 ms of CPU per bot per second (was 14). The equivalence test (`shared/test/sdbrain_equiv.test.js`) plays the live brain against a frozen copy tick for tick on seeded matches, so a port between lines changes nothing. **F9** in the client prints the Sudden Death report to the console — the plans taken, the pricings refused, the rounds fired, and the last five seconds as a tape — and a bot's death prints the same with the killing round's geometry.

**Open observations** (2026-10-17, from playtest death reports; none changed yet — a single report never justifies a change, and every change must be a rule, not a patch):

- **The Reaction priced, not ordered.** Caught exposed, the unit always dodges and then runs to the nearest hidden cell; it never chooses to stand and fire. Three deaths in narrow corridors came at the turn-around right after the dodge, the body passing back through the point the gun was already on. The run and the stand should be priced by the same function (the route's exposure against the trade, deaths per hit) and the cheaper taken; a fixed order would fight where three steps reach cover.
- **The dodge's side and the run's side.** The dodge takes the side with the longer open run; the escape search then finds the nearest hidden cell, usually back where the unit came from, so the body reverses. Candidate: among the sides with room, the one toward the cell it last stood hidden in.
- **A gun is aimed when it has seen the unit, not only when it has fired.** A hunter who sprints without firing reads as a cold gun (600 ms), so escape routes with exposed stretches price low; he fires the moment the unit lands. The brain already knows when it was last seen.
- **A route under way keeps its first price.** Only the Approach is re-priced at the cadence; a relocation's exposed stretches are priced once, and a sprinting enemy changes them within a second. Candidate: every route re-priced from where the body is, at the cadence, like the Approach.
- **The Approach's route and the price of waiting.** When every option is refused, the Approach prices the shortest path, which crosses the open; the safest path (the exposure-weighted search the relocations already use) would pass more often. If stalemates remain, waiting needs a price of its own — the longer the duel stays undecided, the dearer the wait — compared against the safest route by the same function. Needs a stuck report (F9) first.
- **The crossing legs lean 45° toward their goal** (`reactDriftDeg`), against the rule that every sprint runs across the line. Held on the owner's word.
- **The jump walks the whole route ahead of it** regardless of the tank; a route with a jump link is walked while the gauge is full.
- **The dead-end pocket** on flashpoint, N(112, 81.35): the grid has no cell there and the Approach's straight last leg clips a corner. A map change, held.
- Two guards that cannot fire (`taken ?? now`, `Array.isArray` on the shot log) to be deleted with the next change; one report with a geometry the tape cannot explain (a perpendicular dodge whose ends both sit 1.1 u from the killing round's line).

**The one-brain experiment** (2026-10-17): the Sudden Death brain priced in life — a hit costs damage / HP of a life, so one brain and one price serve both modes — replaced the plain brain, was measured (against the plain brain: 69% wins, a 30.8 s median kill against 16.3 s, five times the CPU) and was shelved the same day: the plain brain is cheap, predictable and the easier game. It is archived on branch `Testing_0.9.0_New-BOT-Brain` (`docs/NEW_BOT_BRAIN.md` there: the principle, the structure, the reports, the pending designs). The plain brain here is that work's first two steps: its dead code deleted and one module for both hosts (`shared/src/sim/nbrain.js`), behaviour unchanged.


### Free pov (Shooter mode, b1027c, user 2026-10-30)

Shooter mode has two points of view, switched any time without the pause menu: `T` on the desktop (`I` until b1105jj), two quick taps on the empty screen on a phone (ignored while Command mode stands; a tap in the stick's zone is the stick's, b1105jf). **Angled pov** is the classic one: the chase camera frames the unit and its lock, every shot is aimed at the lock. **Free pov** keeps the angled pov's own camera rig (13 behind, 6.8 up, a shoulder to the right, looking at the mid-point to the lock, the field of view by the lock's distance — with the crosshair level the two cameras stand and look alike at every range, b1027i), turned to the aim and swung by its pitch about the unit itself (b1027j: swung about the far look point, a glance down lifted the camera into a bird's view), snapped with no lag, and a **crosshair** at the screen centre says where the rounds go: the shot is the line from the muzzle to the point under the crosshair (the first wall, or the enemy body the camera ray crosses before it), so it holds even up close. The player aims the crosshair. Desktop: the mouse (the pointer lock when the page's frame allows it, otherwise a drag with a button held), left click fires, and a right click on the empty screen dodges (b1105jl; the lock's switch for one build, b1105jk; where a held button's drag aims, a clean click alone — under 12 px and 300 ms); `Shift` sprints (a double-tap locks it), `Q` dodges, beside the older K / L (b1027f; `E` switched the lock too until b1105jj made it the ally tactic's key — `U` switches it). Phone: a swipe on the empty screen, or a drag on the fire button, the sniper's charge included. Sensitivity (`window.__freePov.cfg`, live): 0.25°/px for a swipe or a fire-button drag (a landscape phone's width ≈ 200°), 0.10°/px for the mouse, pitch −50°…+60°. The lock is kept but not fired at: the unit turns to the crosshair while it fires or charges (the crosshair's own direction, not the shot's line, which leaves the muzzle a few degrees off it when the aimed point is near — b1027k) and otherwise faces the way it moves, holding its heading when still (b1027f), there is no red lock and no homing, the lock bracket hides, every live enemy off the frame has its screen-edge triangle — the one locked before the switch too, and a 1v1's one enemy (b1105fv, 2026-11-11; until then only the enemy not locked had one, as in angled pov, so the locked one was lost once it left the screen) — and the crosshair is Counter-Strike's classic — four lines, 8 px long and 2 px thick with a dark edge (user 2026-11-02, "the traditional counter-strike style crosshair instead of circle crosshair, and keep the bloom expand effect", the first of four samples; a ring until then) — their inner ends on the rounds' cone drawn on the screen (the base spread plus the bloom, as the rounds sample it, so a burst pushes the lines out; a shotgun's cone is fixed at the small base size every gun opens with — b1027h, user 2026-10-30: its rounds are one rigid pattern, not a cone; never under 12 px, the lines 6 px from the centre; b1027e, user 2026-10-30: the camera rig, the facing, the ring and the keys), and one green whatever it sits on (user 2026-11-02, "remove the 'cross hair becomes red when pointing at enemy' effect"; red over an enemy's body from b1027c, the first free pov build, until then). A round hits whichever enemy it meets (each tick its target is the living enemy nearest its flight: the sim's `retargetFreeAim`, the client's mirror); it still carries an *intent target*, the enemy nearest the aim line, for the bookkeeping, and a sniper charge is marked with it. Its glint warns **every** enemy: bots react as if aimed at, online every human enemy sees it. Back in angled pov the lock goes to the enemy nearest the crosshair — the smallest angle off the camera's centre ray; two within half a degree, the nearer one (b1027l, user 2026-10-30; before, the nearest enemy). Online the frame carries `freeAim`, `aimYaw`, `aimPitch` and `lockTarget` (the picked slot; the server takes it only for a living enemy), and the server fires along the aim it is sent. The TARGET button hides while the player's own free pov stands — no lock to switch for the shots; `U` still moves the kept lock, which the camera frames by — and a spectator keeps it to change the watched unit; a spectator's own `T` does nothing — the spectator-mode watcher, a dead player watching a teammate and an online spectator socket alike (b1027o) — and the lock bracket follows the watched unit's pov — shown while it is in angled pov, gone while it free-aims (b1027n, user 2026-10-30); watching a unit in free pov, the spectator sees that unit's crosshair drawn where its aim line lands on the spectator's own screen (the snapshot carries a free-aiming unit's `aimYaw`/`aimPitch`, three decimals, only while it free-aims; b1027m, user 2026-10-30). Universal: every map, every unit, offline and online alike.

## Command mode

The alternative to the classic chase camera (2026-08-21): a tilt-shift **diorama overview** of the whole map where the units fight on the standard bot brain and you play the commander — observe, select, order. The picker is a Shooter / Command chip in the menus; like Duel / 1v1 the choice is session-only — every site open starts Shooter. Offline, `V` or the pause menu also toggles it mid-match, and solo 2v2 play commands **both** of team one's units; online the pick happens in the queue room, is final once the match starts, and commands your own unit **plus a bot-filled teammate slot** (2026-08-22 — human teammates are never commandable; details in Command mode online below).

**Orders** — three kinds, layered on top of the bot rather than replacing it:

- **Move order** — tap your unit (selection glow), then tap anywhere on the map; or drag from the unit and release. Where floors stack (a bridge deck over ground), **hold** the tap to cycle the vertical layer — the stack lists only standable floors, and the preview auto-picks the lowest reachable one. The unit pathfinds to the spot, **fighting along the way but never abandoning the destination** — travel is **pathfinder-guided the whole way** (2026-08-22): the route re-plans from the unit's current position after every combat reflex and on a 1.5 s cadence, and the unit **vaults the route's jump-links** (raised platforms, fence gaps) instead of grinding at the ledge. On arrival it holds the area in an Engage-style orbit anchored on a **radius-12 ring** for **40 s**, then returns to full autonomy; the orbit **turns around at walls** (a ring straddling a fence — Airport's rim glass, say — patrols the reachable arc instead of grinding the pane), and a unit knocked out of the area by a Defense escape **returns as a fresh pathfinder-guided travel leg**, resuming whatever remains of the 40 s window. An unreachable spot draws a red **"Area is not available"** note at the tap and keeps your selection for an immediate retry. The standing ring is also a live handle (2026-08-27, nothing selected): **grab it anywhere on its disc and drag** to move the order (the whole circle plus a finger-sized margin counts at any zoom — a tap inside the area still orders a *selected* unit there, since ring grabs only engage with nothing selected) — the ring brightens in its unit's color the moment you pick it up (overlapping rings resolve to the nearer center; a dead tie hands it to your own unit), the same preview / validation / layer rules apply, and releasing re-issues the order with a **fresh 40 s window** (any drag counts, even released back where it started — there is no in-place cancel; an unreachable release just draws the red note and leaves the old order untouched) — or **double-tap the ring** to withdraw the move order alone (a standing force lock survives).
- **Force lock** — with your unit selected, tap an enemy: the unit locks that target until **either party dies**, then falls back to its own target-finding. The pinned enemy wears corner **triangles in the commanding unit's color** (the X layout; when two allied commanders pin the same enemy, the later lock wears the **+** edge-midpoint layout instead of splitting corners). Tapping the same enemy again cancels the lock; with **nothing selected**, tapping the pinned enemy's marker or card releases **every lock your units hold on it** at once (2026-08-27 — covers the bot teammate without selecting it; a human teammate's lock is never yours to drop).
- **Hide** (2026-09-26) — **select** your unit (tap its marker or its card — the selection glow) and a shield appears centred just above its **info card** (below it at the top edge) for as long as the selection stands; **tap the shield** to hide. It reads lit while the unit already hides. No hold anywhere. **Hide** wipes the unit's move and lock orders; **any new move or lock order releases it** (the order then stands as usual), as does a double-tap on the unit. A manual reload of 3 s or more enters Hide on its own for the reload (see *Reload hide* under the bot brain) — unless a move order stands, in which case it runs a Cover Hide and keeps the order. A hiding unit's first priority is line of sight: every tick it checks whether **both live enemies** can see it; if they can, it walks the shortest route (a walk-only Dijkstra over the nav grid, no distance cap, capped at 600 cells per search, at most one search per 0.5 s per unit and one per tick per match) to the nearest cell hidden from both. Once hidden it never stands still (owner: a statue looks awful): it **paces** its cover at walk speed on short legs that are verified hidden *before* they are taken (a 2.5 u look-ahead eye test against every enemy eye plus the walk rule), on a 6 u leash around the spot, biased away from the nearest enemy, re-picking its heading every 0.35 s. It also **watches the enemies move**: each is projected 0.5 s ahead on its live velocity, and an eye that would see the unit from there — or a nearest enemy inside 26 u closing at more than 2 u/s — triggers a **slip** to a cell hidden from the live *and* predicted eyes (when closing, one at least 6 u farther from that enemy), before the unit is ever seen. Searches run one attempt per tick (the fallback tiers follow on the next ticks) on the same 0.5 s cadence, so the per-tick cost stays at a handful of sight tests. It **does fire back** whenever a shot exists from where it stands, and never moves for a shot. Being hit does **not** trigger Defense while hiding (hit-stun still applies; the anti-glint dodge and the sniper charge rules are untouched) — **unless no spot hidden from both enemies exists within the search budget** (2026-09-27: the nearest-enemy-only fallback is gone — a walking target hidden from one enemy measured worse than Defense): then it fights on its pursue legs, a fresh hit runs the plain Defense escape, and it keeps searching every 0.5 s until such a spot appears. Sprints on the way are latched: a dash arms only above **50 boost** and may spend down to the **8-boost** hard floor (the escape sprint latch — since b1104b the one rule of every escape sprint: Defense's and the cover move's too) (times the map's boost scale — 250 and 40 on District and Compound). Released, the unit is plain autonomy again — no badge. Online the order travels as `order:stance` (same per-unit 0.5 s rate limit), lives in the team-scoped command side-table like the other two, and never reaches the enemy; a landed `order:move` / `order:lock` releases it server-side.
- One command per selection — the glow drops as soon as an order lands. **Double-tap** your unit to clear both orders at once; a slow re-tap just deselects.
- **Cover Hide** (2026-09-26, reload-only since 2026-09-27) — a unit under a move order spends a **long manual reload (3 s+)** in a hide **bound to the order**: it looks for cover from **both** enemies **inside the order's area first** (14 u around the ordered point; a unit still travelling has no area yet and skips that tier), then the nearest such cover anywhere, sprints there like Defense (down to the 8-boost floor — 40 at the scale of 5, District's and Compound's — then a walk until the gauge is back over 50, 250 at that scale: the escape sprint latch, b1104b; until then no arming), paces once hidden, and ends with the reload — after which the order resumes as after any reflex (travel replans, an anchored unit walks back to its ring; the 40 s hold clock keeps running). No cover anywhere: the reload is spent on the order's legs and Cover Hide is not retried for 0.5 s. No badge, the move order stands throughout, and a new move order landing mid-reload takes over at once. **A fresh hit under a move order runs plain Defense** — the hit-triggered Cover Hide measured about half of Defense's survival time in bot duels on Factory, Streets and Airport (the hidden unit paces at walk speed, stops firing back, and two routing enemies re-acquire it within seconds), so it was retired on 2026-09-27.
- **Reflexes always preempt orders**: Defense escapes, the anti-sniper dodge, the reload hide, hit-stun and the rest interrupt exactly as in autonomous play, and the route replans from wherever the unit ends up.

**Commanded travel dashes on the normal stamina economy** — no speed cheat. Dash segments are *latched*: a segment only starts once boost reaches **125** (or the unit's cap if lower), spends down to a **50-boost reserve floor**, then the unit walks until the gauge re-arms. Route jumps fund at the flat **mandated tier — 60 boost** (2026-08-22, shared with the Defense survival hop; the bot's discretionary jumps fund from its 200 travel floor since 2026-09-29 — 250 before — which the 50↔125 dash cycle could never reach), and while a jump lies ahead on the route the dash floor rises to **70** so the unit always arrives at the ledge able to afford the hop. Cap, drain and regen are the human values throughout, and every level here is times the map's boost scale — at District's and Compound's 5: the arm 625, the floor 250, the mandated tier 300, the floor with a jump ahead 350, the bot's travel floor 1,000; combat reflexes keep their own funding rules (Defense may still spend through the floor to survive).

**Status icons** — gold, on both the unit's marker and its info card: **`!`** while a move order stands (en route plus the 40 s anchor), an **eye** while force-locking (pairing with the colored triangles on the enemy); both side by side when both are active, a **shield** while hiding (which by construction has neither; on the marker it sits centred above the square, or above the diamond's top point), no icon when the unit is autonomous. On the info card the icons are 14×12 px (11×9.5 compact) since 2026-09-26 — the 20×17 originals hung below the card frame once the portrait grew the name row.

**Camera & HUD** — free camera: drag empty ground to pan, pinch / wheel to zoom, `Q`/`E` / right-mouse drag / two-finger twist to rotate about the screen centre; the tilt-shift blur keeps the miniature look with a clear pocket around every unit. Info cards dock in fixed corners — your team bottom-left, enemies bottom-right — each carrying HP and a **stamina bar** (the enemy's stamina bar is hidden online). The joystick, action buttons and the bottom-center stamina bar are gone: fire / dodge / jump / sprint are bot decisions, and each unit's stamina lives on its info card. On Factory, Lobby, Station, Airport and Flashpoint the tall airborne dressing (pipes, trusses, signage, ceiling grids) hides while command mode stands and returns in classic — visual only, nothing collidable. **Lighting is the map's own** (b1027b, user 2026-10-30, "the map looks yellowish in command mode"): the diorama used to re-light every map in a warm daylight grade (Lobby and Airport excepted since 2.1g), which turned grey streets and walls yellowish; command mode now keeps each map's normal lights and sets only its pale background, the fog push-out and the camera planes.

### Command mode online

- **Queue-room pick, hidden from opponents.** The Shooter / Command chip sits above the roster in the waiting room; the pick locks when the match starts (no mid-match toggle online) and is never sent to the other team — only teammates see it, as the gold **[CMD]** roster tag. Behavior will still hint it; accepted. The page takes its view from the pick at every match's start (b1105ir), as the server does — until then a match on the last one's map, seat and mode kept the last one's view.
- **Your unit — and your bot teammate (2026-08-22).** A commander drives their own unit and, when the teammate slot was **bot-filled at match start**, that bot too (same tap/drag orders; the bot's lock triangles wear its own slot color; each unit has its own 0.5 s order rate limit). Human teammates are never commandable, and the commandable set freezes at match start — a mid-match disconnect turns that human's unit into a plain bot, but it is not adopted. The set is frozen anew at every match's start, a rematch's too: a teammate gone before a rematch begins leaves a bot that is the commander's from that match's start. The server sends the set with its snapshots (`startBotSlots`) and the page reads it once a match (b1105ir — until then the page kept the first match's answer through a REMATCH).
- **Server-authoritative orders.** The commander's client sends dedicated order messages; the server re-validates each one against the same pathfind checks (with a 500 ms rate limit per ordered unit — your own unit and the bot teammate throttle separately, and clears are never rate-limited) and answers with an ack the selection glow waits for. Commander clients skip movement prediction entirely — the unit is server-driven like any bot, and command timers run on the server clock.
- **Teammate share (2v2).** A command teammate sees your full annotations (destination ring, icons, lock triangles) in their own diorama. A **classic** teammate gets an in-world render: a ground ring in the commander's slot color standing at your unit's ordered destination, plus your lock triangles framing just outside their own crosshair when they face the pinned enemy. Both indicators die with the commander's unit.
- **Information hiding.** Snapshots are filtered per team: the enemy's boost value, standing orders and bot-intent state never reach your client. Boost-inference tells (overheat, sprint-lock, thruster effects) deliberately stay visible. Spectators watch in classic view with no command overlays.
- **Disconnects.** 1v1 keeps the instant forfeit; in 2v2 a disconnected commander's orders clear and the unit fights on as a plain bot. The players who stay are told either way (b1105is: *A player who leaves is named*, under Online).

## Units

Reading the DPS table: the shotgun rows are the most theoretical — all 8 pellets only land point-blank, and at their 80 lock range the pattern is fully open; the 7-shell magazine burns in ~1.7 s before per-shell regen throttles the long run, where Rabu's box magazine keeps 76.9. Aris's burst is real for the 8-bolt spike (72 damage in ~2.4 s; 96 on her 12-damage bolt until b1105jo), then collapses to the worst sustained figure in the game (7.5; 10.0 until then). The sniper rows use cycle math (the 1 s cooldown + the 0.5 s floor charge) at full range-tier damage. Hina's 72.9 (83.3 on her 4-damage round until b1105jg) tops the automatics by a wide margin; eight automatics sit between 40 and 55 — Atsuko, Marina, Asuna, Minori, Yuuka, Saori, Subaru, Koyuki — where fights are decided by accuracy curves, uptime and positioning rather than raw DPS, and Maki and Kurumi sit under them, level at 31.3 since b1105jg gave Maki a 4-damage round (35.2 on her 4.5 until then). The marksman rifles sit under that band on purpose: Fubuki's and Aris's 336 ms slot and Iori's forced 0.75 s cooldown trade cadence for the heaviest per-shot chunks below the snipers.

Unit 13 is the lightest bullet in the game on Hina's cadence: the 48 ms tick slot (20.8 shots/s) in an SMG chassis and a 71-round drum — ~3.4 s of continuous fire (≈178 damage per drum) behind a 2 s reload. Suppression-first: her value is steady chip on the longest trigger in the SMG class, plus the boost her stream forces targets to burn escaping; the kill usually needs cross-fire or a second drum. Since 0.5.9 the SMG spread profiles follow the real guns; from the 2026-09-22 bloom port they did it through the base cone (Marina's PPSh kept a 0.06 base, Atsuko's EVO3 came down to 0.03), and since b1105fw, every base 0, through the cap alone: the WWII PPSh hoses wide, blooming to 0.11, the modern EVO3 shoots tight, blooming to 0.08.

Units 14 and 15 (user 2026-10-22) are Saori's and Atsuko's chassis with guns of their own. Subaru's HK33 is the M4 with 0.005 bloom per shot: the cap at the 13th round, where the M4's 30-round magazine ends one round short of it (from the 0.02 base until b1105fw, the 9th and the 21st), recovering at the rifles' 0.17/s from 200 ms after the last round (the M4's 0.035/s until 2026-10-24; until b1105fx a shooter standing still added no bloom, so a planted Subaru had the M4's cone exactly). Yuuka's MPX is the evo3 with 850 label RPM and 0.01 bloom per shot: a 70.6 ms cooldown lands on the 80 ms tick slot, Asuna's cadence — 64 and 80 ms are the only slots near — so she fires 12.5/s to Atsuko's 15.6, and her cone reaches the cap at the 9th round where the evo3's takes 28 (the 6th and the 18th from the 0.03 base until b1105fw), recovering at the rifles' 0.17/s from 200 ms after the last round (the SMGs' 0.05/s until 2026-10-24). Since b1105di (2026-11-08) the picker groups the units by class, so both sit with their classes.

Unit 17 (user 2026-10-23): Kurumi is the shield bearer, the only unit off the 100 HP base — **120 HP** (125 until 2026-10-24), her bars as long as her HP (the corner bar, the overhead pill and the command card are 1.2 of the 100 HP base length: `hpBarScale`, a unit's `hp` over the base; user 2026-10-24), and she is **never stunned** (`stunImmune`, a unit field read by the one hit-stun routine, `applyHitStun`: the hit's damage and momentum cut stand, the slow does not). The price is the UMP45's kit: a walk of 10 (Koyuki's tier), the shotguns' reach (80 since b1105eg doubled every lock range; 40 / 50 until then), 600 RPM on the 112 ms slot (8.9 rounds/s) with Atsuko's 3.5 bullet (31.3 burst DPS, 18.7 sustained — still the lowest of the automatics; Maki's burst is level with hers since b1105jg), a 25-round magazine on a 2 s reload, and a cone of 0 (0.03 until b1105fw) that opens by 0.015 a round to Marina's 0.11 (sure-hit ∞ → 29), recovering at the rifles' 0.17/s from 200 ms after the last round — frozen while she sprays (112 ms a round), back from the cap to the base in 0.65 s after (0.47 s to the 0.03 base) (the SMGs' 0.05/s until 2026-10-24; no delay, and 0.3/s, for an hour the same day). (The **reload hold** — `reloadHolds`, a unit field: no walk and no sprint while the magazine is changed, the dodge and the jump open, the same for a player and for the bot — came and went twice the same day; the field stays for any unit to carry.) Her rounds stun as the rifles' do (100 ms at 0.25). Sudden Death puts her at 1 HP like everyone, so she still dies to the first hit there. (Her HP and bullet were tuned through the day of 2026-10-23 — 150 / 3.5, 100, 150 / 2.5 — on player feedback, "a bloody tank" at 150, and settled at 125 / 3.5: a 30-round rifle magazine, 120 damage, still falls 5 short of her, and she kills a 100 HP target in one magazine with 4 rounds to spare.) Her pictures are the user's. (Unit 16, Rio's M1911, was added and removed the same day; the key is left unused.)

Unit 18 (user 2026-10-23): Rabu's M590 is Haruka's SDASS — the concentrated pattern, the 250 RPM, the shotguns' reach (80 since b1105eg; 40 / 50 until then) — on a **10-shell box magazine** that reloads as one in 2 s where the other shotguns load shell by shell: ten blasts in 2.2 s (320 damage point-blank, 76.9 sustained DPS against the shell-loaders' 26.7), then a 2 s gap with nothing in the gun. The bot empties the magazine in one pull (`botFireCap` 10, as Marina's drum; the shell-loaders fire 4 and rest). The only shotgun on the magazine rule; the key after Kurumi's. Her pictures are the user's.

Units 19 to 22 (2026-11-08, PT then 9.5) each grew from a unit already in the game. **Hiyori** (Unit 19, NTW-20) is Aru's sniper with a 3-round magazine, a walk of 10 (12 until b1105db) and tiers of her own on Aru's 15 / 50 zones: 60 / 40 / 20. **Iori** (Unit 20, KAR98K) is Fubuki's rifle with a bolt-action beat: a forced 0.75 s cooldown after every shot (the snipers' ring on the fire button), a 5-round magazine of 13-damage rounds on a 2 s reload, and Fubuki's accuracy, bloom, spindle and ribbon trail. **Maki** (Unit 21, RM338) is a machine gun on Koyuki's reach, bullet and bot rules: 100 rounds of 4 (Koyuki's 4.5 from b1105dt to b1105jg, 5 before) at 500 RPM — the 128 ms slot — and a 0 cone (0.02 until b1105fw) opening by 0.003 a round to 0.06, recovering at 0.045/s, so her capped cone keeps a 53 u sure-hit line. **Minori** (Unit 22, AKM) is Saori's chassis with the AKM's numbers: 5 damage (4.5 from b1105dt, 5 again since b1105eg), 600 RPM on the 112 ms slot, 0.015 bloom a round to a 0.11 cap recovering at the rifles' 0.17/s, and the autos' 33%-hit gate, which since the doubled ranges closes inside her lock (see the measured hit rates).

**Reading the stun column** (`duration @ move-scale`): every landed hit slows the victim's movement to *move-scale* for *duration* — e.g. `100 ms @ 0.25` means crawling at 25% speed for 100 ms. A new hit's stun takes over when none is running or when it is heavier (a lower scale); an equal or lighter hit during a running stun leaves it as it is. The slow is on the unit's whole movement — its own walk or sprint speed and the momentum a sprint banks from it (the hit also cuts what was banked) — so a stunned sprinting unit runs at the move-scale of its sprint, player or bot, offline or online (b1104e, user 2026-11-04). Until then a bot banked the sprint's momentum from its unslowed speed and the stun never reached that part of its speed: online always, offline since b1104d (before that the offline client slowed the summed speed, as a player's). A player and a bot also jump and stop sprinting by the same rules (b1104f, user 2026-11-04, "make player unit aligned with BOT"): a jump banks 0.7 of the speed the body had as the tick began, a sprint's carried part included — a player's had banked only the tick's input speed (a jump out of a sprint launched at 20, a bot's at 32) — and a sprint's end walks on at the walk speed, where a player had walked at 4.55 for 180 ms. Since b1104g a bot's jump also goes off at the speed it moves at that tick, as a player's: the sprint's base when it sprints, the walk when it walks or stands (user 2026-11-04, "make BOT aligned with player unit" — it had launched at the sprint's base whatever it was doing, so its jump from a walk went off at the sprint's base, 11.76, instead of its walk — 10 to 16 by unit, Saori's 12). **In practice the slow itself is a minor stat**: sprinting pays straight through it (written 2026-08-01, while a bot's sprint carried through the stun online; since b1104e a stunned sprint runs at the move-scale too, but a stun lasts only its column's 100 ms or less, so the sprint is back at full speed as soon as the hits stop — and everyone sprints away from fire anyway, stun or not), while a walking target was already highly hittable — so the currencies that actually decide fights are the damage actually landed and the boost the target burns escaping, not the movement penalty.

**Reading the spread columns (2026-09-22, spread bloom — ported from the demo line, owner questionnaire):** every cone angle is in radians and describes the FULL cone — a shot deviates from the aim line by at most half of it. Horizontal spread (HA) is gone from every unit. Instead each shot **blooms** the cone: firing adds *Bloom / shot* to the gun's spread, up to *SA cap*, and once the *Recovery* delay has passed since the last shot the extra spread drains back at the listed rate (the marksman rifles — Fubuki, Aris and Iori — recover between the shots of a burst too — *no delay* — which is what shapes them: Fubuki's second quick shot leaves at 0.043 rather than 0, Aris's at 0.063 rather than 0.02). Since angular error grows with distance, a cone has a **sure-hit distance** against a standing target — 3.2 ÷ spread, 3.2 being the hit capsule's width — inside which every shot lands; beyond it, hit chance falls off roughly as sure-hit ÷ distance. The table gives it at the base cone and at the cap: a fresh trigger pull is pin-point, a long spray is not, and tapping keeps a gun near its base numbers. Since b1105fw every base cone but Aris's is 0 and its sure-hit distance endless: a fresh trigger pull, or a recovered cone, puts its round on the aim line at any range, and what decides the hit is the target's drift during the round's flight (see the measured hit rates). **Standing still keeps no gun pin-point any more** (b1105fx, 2026-11-11; user: "Make bloom take effect in any firing condition"): every shot adds its *Bloom / shot*, standing, walking or sprinting, and the *Recovery* delay counts from the last shot in every case. Until then a shooter that had stood on the ground at under 1 unit/s for 200 ms fired without adding bloom, and whatever bloom it carried drained at the gun's recovery rate even while it kept firing — the price was being a stationary target; and since 2026-11-03 a sprint, even held in place or pressed into a wall, did not count as standing (sprint held with no direction had left the unit at speed 0 and its shots without bloom). The rule's stamp (`stillSince`, `tickStillness`, `isStill`) and its two numbers (`BLOOM_STILL_SPEED` 1, `BLOOM_STILL_DWELL_MS` 200) went with it, in the shared sim (online, and the client's prediction) and the offline client alike. The shotguns ignore the cones entirely: their pellets fly a fixed 8-point pattern that opens toward ~5.8 wide over the first 70 units of flight — at their 80 lock range it is fully open (it was a tight ~3.3-wide cluster at the old 40), and the volley dies at 150 (100 until b1105et); Hoshino's pattern is additionally stretched 1.4× horizontally (details below).

**Preferred engage distance:** every unit's fighting range is its **lock range ± 15** (`BOT_BAND_HALF_WIDTH`, constants.js; ± 7 until b1105jc, 2026-11-17) — the band where bots hold position, orbit, and fire: the shotguns and Kurumi 65–95, the other SMGs 85–115, the assault rifles 95–125, the machine guns 105–135, the marksman rifles 120–150, Aru and Hiyori 185–215, Kei 235–265 (the lock ranges of b1105et, 2026-11-10). One rule for all weapons: retune a lock range and the combat distance follows. **Since b1105eg (2026-11-09) it is one rule for both modes too**: every lock range was doubled and the 2v2 values were removed, so a 2v2 bot holds the same band as a 1v1 bot (until then a second, 2v2 number compressed the 2v2 bands into 50–70, so long-lock units would not hang back while a teammate died alone at the front). **Facing a shotgun** (b1105br): while a bot's current target is an enemy shotgun its band centres on at least 160 (`BOT_SHOTGUN_ENGAGE_RANGE`, 110 until b1105et; a longer lock range of its own stays, a shotgun bot keeps its own, Sudden Death is untouched) — every class but the snipers fights a shotgun at 145–175. The band is the plain bots' alone: a Sudden Death bot reads none (its own reach, `sdOwnReach`). The player-side lock reticle reads the unit's lock range.

**Measured hit rates** (re-measured 2026-11-11 on 9.7 b1105fw, every base cone 0 but Aris's 0.02, and Hina's row and panel again at b1105fz for her 0.16 cap and 0.06/s recovery and at b1105ga for her 150-round drum and her own 20% gate, and Marina's row and panel at b1105je (2026-11-17, 9.8) for the 20% gate she shares with Hina since — nothing else since b1105fw touches the other rows: b1105fx's standing-still change leaves the strafing shooter as it was, and a full run at b1105fz agrees with them within 2 points; every unit at its lock range and round speed as they stand: b1105et's lock ranges, b1105fd's and b1105fh's round speeds, and Hina gated since b1105fe; the run before, earlier the same day on 9.6 b1105fv, had the old base cones, and the runs before that, 2026-11-09 on b1105er and 2026-11-10 on b1105es, the older ranges and speeds as well — their numbers are kept under *Reading it*; the harness is `shared/tools/hitcurve.mjs`, the chart `shared/tools/hitcurve_png.py`, both in the tree, the whole run 19 s; two runs agree within 2 points, Aris's sprint row within 3 on her 8-round magazine). Once a cone grows with every round, one percentage no longer describes a gun: what matters is **how the hit chance decays across a magazine while the shooter is on the move**. So the protocol is a walking full-magazine spray, run on the shared sim (the code the server runs) on Plain Field — its boundary walls taken away for the run, since the machine guns' 120, the marksman rifles' 135 and the snipers' 200 and 250 reach the walls of the 240 × 240 field or past them — 200 runs per series:

- **Shooter:** strafes sideways at her own walk speed (16, 12 or 10 u/s; Hina 8), reversing every 400 ms so she is never still and every round blooms (since b1105fx a standing shooter's rounds bloom as well), and holds the trigger through **one whole magazine** (tap-fired for the shotguns and snipers; a charging sniper is rooted by the sim, as in play), **obeying the bot fire gate** (`botMayFire`). The marksman rifles' line — the 100% sure-hit line — has no end for Fubuki's and Iori's 0 base cone and stands at 160 for Aris's 0.02, beyond their 135 lock range, and closes far inside it after a round, so the three fire every round from a fully recovered cone, exactly as their bots do. Since the doubled ranges the autos' 33%-hit line (8.84 over the cone) closes inside Minori's lock range: at 110 she fires six rounds, from cones of 0 to 0.075, before her cone passes it, and then holds fire until the cone is fully recovered — 0.75 s from a burst's last round to the next one's first (Kurumi's line, 80.4 at her 0.11 cap, sits right at her 80). Marina did the same at 100 — thirty rounds, from 0 to 0.087, and 2.0 s holds — until b1105je put her on the 20% line (11.42 over the cone), which stands at 103.8 at her 0.11 cap, past her lock: she fires her whole drum. Hina, on her own 20% line since b1105ga (the autos' 33% from b1105fe), fires thirty-two at 120, from 0 to 0.093, and holds about 1.8 s (twenty-five, from 0 to 0.072, with 1.5 s holds on the 33% line at b1105fz). Every other auto's line stays beyond its lock range even at the cap, and Koyuki and Maki are ungated. Every unit fires from **her own lock range** only.
- **Target:** a real unit — the 3.2-wide hit capsule, not the Range's signs — ping-ponging on a ±35° arc at lock range around the shooter, so it always moves across the line of fire, at three speeds: standing, the shooter's own walk speed, and that walk speed + 11.76 (the sprint boost). Reversal at the arc ends is instant, so the speed never dips. Shots aim at the target's current position, exactly as the game does (no lead).
- **Scoring:** every round is a hit or a miss (shotguns: pellets landed ÷ 8 per blast; Kei's beam counts as a round). Each curve is the hit chance of the *n*-th round of the magazine, averaged over the 200 runs; the numbers in the table are the mean over the whole magazine and, in brackets, the mean of its first third → last third.

*Shotgun rows:* Hoshino fires the 1.4× wide fan, Haruka the concentrated cloud (the two swapped patterns on 2026-10-10), and Rabu carries Haruka's pattern on her 10-shell magazine. At their 80 lock the pattern is fully open, so even a standing target takes about a third of the pellets — at the old 40 it was half open and Haruka's cloud landed 78%. b1105fw's 0 base took away the volley centre's small jitter (up to 0.64°); the three rows moved by a point at most. The picture is current (2026-11-17, b1105je).

*Reading it:* the first→last gap is the bloom, and since b1105fw every cone but Aris's opens from 0, so a magazine's first rounds land every time on a standing target and on a walking one. **The assault rifles:** Saori and Asuna open a magazine at 100% on a standing target and close it at 72% and 60% — at 110 their capped 0.06 cone's sure-hit line (53) is about half the range — and Subaru, the fastest bloom, runs 96→58. **Minori's sawtooth** is her gate: at 110 she fires six rounds, from cones of 0 to 0.075, before her cone passes the 33% line, holds until it is fully recovered, and starts again — 81% on a standing target. **The SMGs** at 100: Atsuko 100→54 on a standing target, Yuuka 78→49, Marina 88→35 across her 71-round drum, fired in one pull since b1105je (88→76 in bursts of thirty behind the 33% gate until then): from round thirty-eight her cone is at its 0.11 cap, where about a round in three lands. **The machine guns** at 120: Koyuki's cone reaches its cap at round 31 and sits there at ~26% — at 120 its capped cone's sure-hit line is 27 u — while Maki's 0.06 cap holds ~56%; **Hina**, on her own 20% line since b1105ga, fires her 150-round drum in bursts of thirty-two from cones of 0 to 0.093 with a hold of about 1.8 s between them, 73% on a standing target: the wider line spends more rounds from wider cones (80% at b1105fz, in bursts of twenty-five to 0.072 behind the autos' 33% line, whose 0.16 cap and 0.06/s recovery had shortened the holds from 2.7 s to 1.5 s; her cone never reaches the cap behind either gate). **The sprinter** is where the 0 base costs: every round is aimed where the target is, and during the flight a sprinter moves more than the capsule's half-width (1.6) at every automatic's lock range but Kurumi's, so a round from a 0 cone passes behind it — the first round of every other automatic lands 0–2% — and only a cone opened wide enough throws some rounds ahead of the body: the autos' sprint curves climb from there as the cone opens (Saori 3→35, Atsuko 8→42). **Kurumi** at 80 holds the best sprinting number of the automatics, 52%: in her round's 0.073 s a sprinter moves 1.58, just inside the half-width, so her first round lands every time and her bloom only takes rounds away (63→48). **The marksman rifles** never fire bloomed: Fubuki and Iori, from their 0 cone, land every round on a standing and on a walking target and 1% on a sprinter — at 135 a 1,500 u/s round flies 0.09 s, in which a walking target moves two thirds of the capsule's half-width and a sprinter 1.3 times it; Aris, her 0.02 base kept, lands 100 / 94 / 56, her laser bolt's 0.4 wider radius and whole 64-long body forgiving more of the drift. **The snipers** land every round: Aru and Hiyori at 200 (the 4,500 u/s round crosses it in 0.044 s, a sprinter's drift two thirds of the half-width) and Kei's instant beam at 250. The run before this one, earlier the same day on 9.6 b1105fv (the same ranges and speeds, the old base cones), read Saori 77 / 61 / 31, Asuna 73 / 58 / 33, Subaru 66 / 57 / 35, Minori 71 / 60 / 30 (in fives from cones of 0.02 to 0.08), Atsuko 63 / 52 / 37, Marina 54 / 49 / 38 (in tens from 0.06), Yuuka 53 / 47 / 37, Kurumi 52 / 52 / 47, Koyuki 33 / 31 / 27, Hina 60 / 57 / 43 (in twelves from 0.04, holding 1.4 s), Maki 57 / 54 / 40, Fubuki 100 / 79 / 37, Aris 100 / 94 / 57, Iori 100 / 78 / 37, Aru 89 / 86 / 74 and Hiyori 90 / 87 / 75, the shotguns within a point of now and Kei as now. The runs before that, 2026-11-09 on b1105er and 2026-11-10 on b1105es (the assault rifles at 112, the machine guns at 126, the marksman rifles at 160 with 1,100 u/s rounds, the snipers at 240 with 4,000 u/s rounds, Hina ungated), read Saori 75 / 60 / 31, Koyuki 31 / 29 / 25, Hina 22 / 21 / 19, Maki 55 / 51 / 35, Fubuki 100 / 46 / 1, Aris 100 / 63 / 5, Iori 100 / 44 / 1, Aru 77 / 76 / 63, Kei 100 / 100 / 100 and Hiyori 79 / 79 / 65. Against the 2026-10-24 run at the old ranges and speeds, every cone gun lost hit rate at the longer distances (Saori 99 / 81 / 35 then, 77 / 61 / 31 at b1105fv; Koyuki 49 / 44 / 30 then at 80, 33 / 31 / 27 at 120), and the shotguns the most (Haruka 78 / 46 / 18 then, 39 / 31 / 11 at b1105fv). Before b1105es the machine guns fought at 160 (Koyuki 24 / 22 / 18, Hina 16 / 16 / 14, Maki 44 / 41 / 29) and the marksman rifles at 112 (Fubuki 100 / 75 / 15, Aris 100 / 93 / 37, Iori 100 / 74 / 14; Aris's b1105er row read 95 / 39 on the moving targets, from a count that gave some of her rounds two bolts).

Projectiles fly straight (homing is zeroed universally). The targeting reticle is an **enemy-firing indicator**, not a range indicator: green by default, it flashes red while your current target is firing and stays red for the whole time a sniper is mid-charge with you as the target (see the sniper section). Being inside lock range is not signalled to players at all — the number only shapes bot behavior (bots hold their engage band around it). A faint in-lock tracer tint that once keyed to it was removed 2026-08-01.

### Units 2, 11 & 18 — the shotgun blast

- A trigger pull fires **one flying pellet cluster** carrying a fixed 8-point pattern (randomly rotated each shot, so no two blasts look alike while the spacing geometry never clumps). Each pellet keeps its **own hitbox** and dies individually on walls or the target; damage = pellets landed × 4 (all 8 point-blank = 32, all three shotguns; was × 5 / 40 before 2026-09-24).
- The pattern leaves the muzzle bunched and grows toward full width (~5.8 across) over the first **70 units** of flight. At the 80 lock range (40 until b1105eg doubled every lock range) it is fully open, so a blast at the lock lands as a full spread — at 40 it was ~57% open, ~3.3 across, a concentrated cluster; the pellets fly at 500 u/s (350 until b1105eg) and the volley dies at 100 (`pelletRange`, below).
- **Hoshino's wide fan (Unit 2):** her pattern is stretched **1.4× horizontally** after the per-shot rotation — the cloud is 1.4× wider and exactly as tall as Haruka's (fully open, as at her 80 lock: ~8.1 × 5.8; ~4.6 × 3.3 at the old 40). More graze coverage along the dodge axis — the dodge-catcher to Haruka's concentrated slug. (The fan was Haruka's from 2026-07-14 until the owner swapped the two patterns on 2026-10-10; nothing else differs between the two shotguns.)
- One blast = one simulated/networked object instead of 8 — the wire-cost half of the old online "shotgun lag" fix; the projectile broadphase (see Implementation notes) removed the other half, the dense-map CPU cost.
- **Reach — `pelletRange`, 150 units (37.5 m; b1105et, 2026-11-10 — 100 from 2026-10-25, PT first).** A volley dies once its centre has flown `pelletRange` from the muzzle (despawn reason `range`); before, only the common 2.2 s TTL ended it, 770 units at 350 u/s. And a bot holding a gun that carries the field does not pull the trigger on a target beyond it: the shotguns have no bloom gate (`botMayFire` passes every multi-pellet gun), so until now a shotgun bot with a clear line fired at any distance, and on Compound four of them kept a sky of pellets alive. One field on the three shotgun entries (Units 2, 11, 18), in both unit tables (`shared/src/sim/constants.js` and the client's `UNIT_DATA`); a gun without the field flies and fires as before. The frozen-brain references were re-frozen at b1025a for the hold.
- **The offline mirror on the sim's broadphase (b1025b, user 2026-10-25: "the online SG pellet mechanics, PT offline first").** The shared sim has tested each pellet's step only against the boxes and floors near it since the online shotgun fix; the offline client's volley branch still walked every box and every floor of the map, eight pellets a volley, every frame. On Compound that is 22,666 boxes and 16,878 floors: 1.16 ms per pellet-step measured, about 10 ms a frame with four shotgun bots — the offline "Compound lags with shotguns" report, and why four Aris did not lag (one bolt each). The branch now takes its candidates from `obstaclesNearSegment`, for the walls and for the floors alike; the index returns a superset, and 60,000 random pellet steps on Compound, Town and Dust stopped at the same walls and floors both ways. The single-round branch still scans the whole list (one segment per round, so a third of the cost at most); online play never had the problem, the server runs the shared sim.

### Aris (Unit 7) — flight & laser bolts

- **Flight kit**: a jump tap in the air re-fires the jump impulse (12 boost per pop, no cooldown); *holding* jump sustains a climb at sprint speed; air-sprint flies **level** (dedicated fly art); the air-dodge holds altitude. Boost does not regen while airborne — altitude is a spent resource.
- Sprinting into a jump **carries the sprint momentum** through the air.
- Her shot is a **64-unit-long laser bolt**: the thin cyan cylinder you see *is* the hitbox (both derive from one spec entry). It grows out of the muzzle — the body never reaches behind the spawn point — and hits with its whole length, so a dodge must clear the entire passing beam, not just its nose.

### Sniper charge & sprint-cancel

- All three snipers (Aru, Kei, Hiyori) hold their shot on a **1 s charge** (locked in place). Holding sprint cancels the charge and fires early — but never before a **0.5 s floor** (costs ½ a dodge's boost). So the shooter picks any release point in the **0.5–1 s** window, and the target always gets at least that much glint-to-bullet warning.
- **Online floating unlock:** against a human defender the 0.5 s floor counts from the moment their client *actually rendered* the glint — the defender's client acks the glint's first frame and the server slides the earliest release to that ack + 0.5 s — the defender's half-second of SEEN warning is absolute. If the ack never arrives (defender's tab backgrounded, client stalled; the server waits up to 0.5 s), the earliest release becomes press + 1.0 s — exactly a normal full charge, so the attacker's worst case is simply losing the fast cancel for that shot. Offline play and bot defenders (who see server truth instantly) are unchanged.

**Sniper timing at a glance** (worked example: defender's network delay 0.05 s each way; projectile flight and 16 ms tick rounding excluded). Column definitions — *release time*: server clock, from processing the attacker's FIRE input to creating the projectile; *glint duration*: on each player's own display, from the frame the glint is first drawn to the frame the shot is drawn; *read & decide*: on the defender's side, from the glint's first drawn frame to the latest DODGE press that still reaches the server before the projectile exists.

| Release time | Glint duration (attacker's screen) | Glint duration (defender's screen) | Read & decide time |
|---|---|---|---|
| 0.60 (earliest the server permits) | 0.60 | 0.60 | **0.50** |
| 0.70 | 0.70 | 0.70 | 0.60 |
| 0.80 | 0.80 | 0.80 | 0.70 |
| 0.90 | 0.90 | 0.90 | 0.80 |
| 1.00 (server auto-fires) | 1.00 | 1.00 | **0.90** |

Three properties the table encodes: glint duration equals the release time on **every** screen (both endpoints of the interval shift by the same delivery delay, so its length is preserved for any observer); the defender's read & decide time is always the release time minus their round trip (one delivery lost at each end); and the earliest-release fence sits at ack + 0.5 s precisely so the read & decide column can never fall below 0.50 — the guarantee is produced by *placing the fence*, not by adjusting any clock. The full charge is the one release with no fence involvement: it fires at press + 1.0 s flat, so its read & decide time shrinks with the defender's round trip (lag-taxed like every ordinary attack), while the fast cancel's 0.50 is lag-proof.
- The **dodge** is the counter: a step grants **0.3 s** of i-frame immunity, so a well-timed dodge passes through the shot. Aru's and Hiyori's bullet speed is **4500 u/s** (b1105fd, user 2026-11-10, 4000 before, 2500 until 2026-10-22: near-hitscan — 0.044 s of flight at their 200 lock range, under three ticks); Kei's beam is instant.

### Aru (Unit 3) & Hiyori (Unit 19) — range zones & the lock reticle

- Damage is tiered by distance, **locked at fire time**: under 15 units → **20**, 15–50 → **35**, beyond 50 → **50**. Rushing a sniper is real counterplay; long range stays lethal. Hiyori's NTW-20 is tiered on the same zones: **20 / 40 / 60**.
- The lock reticle shows the current zone — plain brackets (<15), **+ cross ticks** (15–50), **+ inner bars** (50+). It appears both when *you* play Aru or Hiyori (your tier on the target) and when your lock target *is* one of them (which of her zones you're standing in).
- The reticle turns **red** not just when your target fires, but for the whole time a sniper (Aru **or** Kei) is **mid-charge with you as the target** — a continuous danger signal from glint to shot. Kei's live sweep channel also holds it red for the whole channel, whoever it is aimed at — the beam can hit anyone.

### Kei (Unit 6) — Railgun (照射ビーム beam)

- Fires an instant **hitscan beam** (30 damage, one hit per enemy per beam, blocked by walls, ~0.5 s fade) instead of a bullet. The beam also **deletes projectiles** it touches. The hit volume stops one combined body-width (beam radius + target radius) short of the wall it ends on (2026-09-10), so neither the quick beam nor the sweep channel registers on a unit standing just behind cover — before, the end cap reached up to ~3.2 u (channel ~4 u) through the wall.
- Holding the charge to the full **1 s** fires a **sweep channel**: a 1 s locked, steerable beam (1.5× width, **20 damage**, one hit per enemy for the whole channel). The stick steers it — horizontal and vertical — at ~10°/s; sprint cancels the channel. The fire cooldown is paused during the channel and starts when it ends.
- Her glint grows toward **2×** size as the charge fills, telegraphing a full-charge sweep.

**Bots vs. the sniper.**
- **As the shooter:** every sniper bot flips a **50/50 coin** per shot — release at the **0.5 s floor** (a fast snap; for Kei, the quick beam) or hold to the **full 1 s charge** (for Kei, the sweep channel). No in-between releases.
- **On defense:** when a glint aimed at it appears — from **any** enemy, locked or not (mirroring the human's edge-indicator awareness; earliest active charge wins) — the bot **rolls its reaction per charge**, and the slow roll is charger-aware:
  - **Anti-Aru** (the bullet snipers, Aru and Hiyori): **50% at 0.4 s** (i-frames open ahead of the earliest possible cancel, covering **every floor snap at any range** — but a full hold sails in after they end) / **50% at 0.8 s** (deliberately late: a snap lands first and cancels the pending dodge, but the i-frames ~0.8–1.1 s sit exactly on the **full hold's** impact). Against the shooter's 50/50 snap/hold flip neither side can be read; equilibrium **~50% of charges convert** (snaps beat slow rolls, holds beat fast rolls).
  - **Anti-Kei** (beam snipers): **50% at 0.4 s** (covers the instant quick beam) / **50% at 0.9 s** — the dodge starts just ahead of the **sweep channel's** aimed opening (i-frames ~0.9–1.2 s blanket it) and the follow-up sprint outruns the beam's steering at normal fighting ranges. (An 0.8 s roll would be dead weight here: the quick beam pre-empts it and the sweep outlives it.)

  Either way it's one dodge (0.3 s i-frames) plus a **1 s** committed sprint (1.5 s from 2026-11-01, 0.52 s before), both perpendicular to that sniper's line of fire, the sprint staying so every tick (it circles the sniper); lock and return fire stay on the current target throughout. Mid-charge hits still cancel a pending dodge, and a cooldown- or boost-blocked defender still eats the shot. After the committed sprint expires the bot has no awareness of a still-live sweep; the 1.5 s sprint (2026-11-01 to 2026-11-04) outlasted the sweep channel, so it mattered only when cover ended the sprint early; the 1 s one since is not measured against it.

**Bot trigger discipline.** A bot fires in continuous bursts of a fixed per-unit length (`botFireCap` — the "fire cap"), resting ~0.8–1.5 s between bursts. This rhythm is the bottom layer; the **bloom gate** (Bot logic below, 2026-09-22) sits on top of it and only shapes the machine guns' and the marksman rifles' fire inside their bands. Shots inside a burst pace at the weapon's own RPM-derived cooldown, so retuning a fire rate retunes the bot with it. Every auto's cap equals its **full magazine** — an auto bot fires until the mag runs dry and rolls straight into the reload; the shell-loading shotguns are burst-gated at 4 blasts per pull (Rabu empties her 10-shell box in one); the snipers run their charge cycle instead of bursting.

| Unit | Fire cap | Meaning |
|---|---|---|
| Saori / Subaru / Minori | 30 | full mag |
| Asuna | 25 | full mag |
| Atsuko / Yuuka | 30 | full mag |
| Marina | 71 | full drum |
| Kurumi | 25 | full mag |
| Hoshino / Haruka | 4 | 4 blasts per trigger pull |
| Rabu | 10 | full box magazine |
| Koyuki | 100 | full mag |
| Hina | 150 | full drum (~7.2 s of continuous fire) |
| Maki | 100 | full mag (~12.7 s of continuous fire) |
| Fubuki | 30 | full mag |
| Aris | 4 | legacy formula (half mag) |
| Iori | 5 | full mag |
| Aru / Kei / Hiyori | — | charge cycle, no bursts |

A burst ends early if the mag runs dry (straight into the reload), if line of sight breaks (re-checked every 0.22 s; the burst then restarts from full), or if the target is **spawn-immune** — bots hold fire at immune targets and wake the moment immunity lapses. A bot's OWN spawn immunity does **not** hold its fire: a freshly spawned bot shoots from behind its protection window, same as a player would.

### Fubuki (Unit 10), Iori (Unit 20) & Aru (Unit 3) — ribbon tracers

Fubuki's Ruger rounds — and Iori's KAR98K since b1105dd — draw a **camera-facing ribbon trail** (2026-09-19, the demo line's marksman-rifle treatment): a real quad of world half-width 0.06 that re-aims at the camera every frame — live and while fading — so it reads as a thicker, smoke-like streak that thins with range like a physical object. Aru's PSG1 rounds carry the same ribbon at the demo line's deliberately fatter sniper width, half-width 0.10, fading over the sniper's 1 s — since 2026-10-15 the rifle's tail stays at the muzzle for 1 s (the whole flight) and fades over 1 s, the snipers' over 1.5 s — (2026-09-22, owner: "use the 0.8.3 trails" — with that the BA line's trails match the demo line gun for gun: the six autos on the 1-pixel line, the rifle on the 0.06 ribbon, the sniper on the 0.10 ribbon). **Both ribbons glow** (2026-09-23, owner pick "C" from four in-game samples): the core at the gun's own width sits over a second quad 3.2× wider and much fainter as a soft edge; the fade scales both quads. The samples were additive amber; the owner then asked for the autos' per-map ink (2026-09-23) and, because additive light can never match an alpha-blended line (it read white on the dark maps and as a pale highlight on the bright ones, where the autos' line is a dark stroke), finally for the same **visual** colour (2026-09-24): both quads now blend normally in the autos' ink — light grey `0xbbbbbb` on the dark maps, dark slate `0x3a3f4a` on Lobby, Airport and the Shooting Range — with the core's alpha set so that core over halo composites to exactly the line's 0.55. The streak reads as the same grey as the autos' line, wider and soft-edged. The cost is one extra quad (one draw call) per ribbon trail and the same per-frame re-aim the ribbon already did — no post-processing, nothing on the network. It runs on every map: the three bright-ground maps (Lobby, Airport, Shooting Range) were first left on the plain slate ribbon because additive light cannot darken, but the owner chose the same additive glow there too (2026-09-23, seen beside four normal-blend alternatives). The automatics carry **no trail at all** since 2026-10-14 (owner: the smoky streak stays on the semi-automatic rifles and the snipers — the unit data's `bulletTrail` flag, set in both the client's and the shared copy because online rounds are built from the shared sim data; 2026-10-15, after Fubuki's online trail went missing with the flag in the client copy only); shotgun pellets have none, Aris's bolt is its own trail and Kei's beam is hitscan; Hiyori's rounds take the snipers' 1.5 s fade on the plain 1-pixel line. Since the same day **every single-round gun flies the same spindle tracer** — the sniper's, at six times its old length (20.4 u) and a 0.15 u mid radius (was 3.4 / 0.18, with the autos at half size), and since b1105ek (2026-11-09) 1.8 times that length and 1.5 times that width, 36.72 u and 0.225 u, so the faster rounds stay visible — its body clipped to the distance flown so it grows out of the muzzle and never reaches back over the shooter; the shotgun pellets stay small spheres and the laser bolt its cylinder. Online (2026-10-15, owner: "a circle-like bullet visual near the firing character") the client flies each bullet on along its last snapshot velocity between snapshots (capped at 300 ms), and measures the spindle's body and the trail's start from the shooter's muzzle — the shooter's position in the same snapshot plus the sim's muzzle height, since a normal round's snapshot carries no spawn point — instead of from where the client first saw the round; measured from there the spindle began as a 1 u stub, a 0.3 u disc seen from behind, for one frame on every shot. The first frame now draws the distance already flown, like the offline first frame. The local player's **own rounds go further** (2026-10-15, owner: "the bullet visual spawns away from the firing character, it gives a laggy feel"): the client's prediction already fires them at the muzzle the moment the key goes down, so their spindle and trail are drawn from there at once and fly on, dying on walls and decks like an offline round; the server's copy of the round is never drawn on the shooter's screen: the local round keeps flying its own line to its own end — wall, deck, the drawn enemy's hit capsule, or the copy's going — and, since the **shared spread seed** (2026-10-15, owner: "implement seed"), on the very line the server's round flies: the server draws one random number per match and sends it once in `match:start`, and both the server and the client's prediction sample each round's direction inside its cone from a stream keyed by that seed, the fighter and the shot number (shared `spawnProjectiles`; shotgun volleys the same). The distribution is unchanged and a fresh seed every match keeps matches apart; nothing rides in the snapshots. What remains between the shooter's picture and the judgement is the enemy's movement over one round trip in the aim (0 standing, about 1 u walking, 3 u sprinting), against a hit capsule 1.6 u in radius. A first cut handed the mesh over to the copy and slid it onto the server's line, which read as the bullet swerving mid-flight (owner 2026-10-15). Hits, damage and what the other players see are the server's as before; a local round the server never fired is dropped once the server has run four inputs past it (or after a second); shotgun volleys stay on the server path. Because the prediction's clock is re-based on every snapshot, a late snapshot can make the forward ticks fire a held trigger's round a second time (owner 2026-10-15: "two parallel trails"), so the sim now counts trigger pulls per fighter (`shotsFired`) and stamps each round with its number (`shot`): a predicted round with the number of a local round already drawn is the same shot and is not drawn, the server's copy claims exactly the local round with its number, and a local round whose number the server has fired past without its copy showing is dropped (a plain time window let re-fires through — a protocol model of the prediction showed two to seven ghost rounds per three seconds of held fire at a 100 to 240 ms round trip — and the magazine count cannot serve where autoReload refills it between shots); rounds the replay fires that the forward ticks never did are drawn back-dated. A local round that dies on a wall before the server's copy shows (any wall within a round trip's flight, 60 u at a 100 ms round trip — the tally from the owner's match read 97 copies, 9 claimed, 88 drawn fresh) leaves a record, and the copy bearing its number claims the record and is never drawn, instead of flying the same shot a second time from mid-air. The client prints a tally to the browser console every five seconds while rounds are in flight (`[own-shots <build tag>] …`), and shows its build tag in the tab title. Visual only — hit detection is untouched.

## Bot logic

One bot brain drives every bot — all maps, all modes, offline and online (one module since 2026-10-17, `shared/src/sim/nbrain.js`: the server's `tickBot` and the offline client's `updateEnemy` each build a small host and call `nbBrainTick` once a tick, exactly as the Sudden Death brain is called; every bot slot in 1v1 / 2v2 / Trio / spectator uses the identical body). In **Sudden Death** the cover brain described in that section replaces Pursue / Engage; everything below is the Health Point brain.

**State machine** — `Defense > Maze > Engage > Pursue`, re-evaluated every tick:

- **Pursue** — closes to (or backs off to) the fighting band, **lock range ± 15** (± 7 until b1105jc). Sprints in **latched legs** (2026-09-29): a leg arms only from a **full tank (250)** and runs down to **200**, then the bot walks until the tank is full again — roughly 0.7 s of sprint per 0.7 s of walk, and Maze routing shares the latch. Both bounds are times the map's boost scale: on District and Compound a leg runs from **1,250** down to **1,000**, about 3.6 s of sprint per 0.7 s of walk. (Both bounds used to sit on the 250 reserve: in Pursue the release won at exactly 250, so it never sprinted; in Maze the dispatch floor let one dash tick through every half second — the flickering sprint pose.) Elevation aids: jump up toward a higher target when close, hop off ledges toward a lower one, and on low ground hop onto any mountable ledge it brushes within jump reach.
- **Engage** — the in-band fight: orbits the target with a range correction toward the sweet spot, flips orbit direction to stay inside sight, and runs the peek-cover rhythm (tuck behind cover while the weapon cycles, drift out exactly when it's ready to fire). Two consecutive ticks of driving into a wall also flip the orbit — the tangent is perpendicular to the aim line, so reversing it always points away from the face just hit, and the bot unsticks itself inside the fight instead of breaking off to re-route. Opportunistically hops onto reachable ledges — high ground is the better vantage.
